# Paperclip stable draft — from beta 2026.1120.0-beta.0

> Auto-generated at beta publish from `git log 09e24dd12c..28043f8b93` (baseline: v2026.1117.0 (merge-base 09e24dd12c)).
> Edit freely during the soak: rewrite for release-notes voice,
> fold noise, and call out anything a self-hoster must act on.
> The stable promotion reads this file from master and publishes
> it as the GitHub Release body under the stable version.

## Features

- feat(runner): relaunch a released task as the next run after a restart (#22343)
  > - Executor: a remote warm recovery with a released source, a recorded build, a reusable process configuration and no session move, on a provider that advertises the capability, revalidates the recovery and becomes a relaunch: `relaunchReleased` with the released source and build, the warm checkpoint, and no `remoteWarmRecovery` for the backend. The startup context ends as for a cached relaunch.
  > - Executor: the relaunch's task owner can now come from the new backend (`onPluginTaskOwner`), so the unavailable-reuse fallback and the rotation revert work without a retained owner. Without an owner, no relaunch can have started, so the revert applies.
  > - Backend: a relaunch neither stops a resumed task that has no durable binding nor resolves a `resumeFrom` task.
- feat(connections): guided AWS role setup with a per-connection CloudFormation template (#22233)
  > - `packages/shared`:
  > - `server/src/services/connectors/aws/setup.ts` builds the setup values:
  > - The service method `getAwsSetup` and the route `GET /api/tool-connections/{connectionId}/aws-setup`:
- feat(responsibilities): file company skills under responsibilities (#22275)
  > - Review fixes: the default responsibility view's Skills "+" adds an existing skill, removes a filed one, or opens an agent's skills page. A newest Dashboard version without a skill list is upgraded in place, so the first skill filed after it is undone. Undo files again, or removes, only skills the restoring person can see. A read scope without a skill condition sees no filed skill. `update_skill_details` with only `skillId` is a no-op, as its schema allows.
  > - Migration `0499`: add `responsibility_links.skill_id` (FK to `company_skills`, on delete cascade). Widen the single-target check to four targets. Add a unique `(responsibility_id, skill_id)` index and a `skill_id` index. The migration is idempotent and online-compatible (NOT VALID then VALIDATE, and the indexes are built CONCURRENTLY). It is listed in `MIGRATIONS_WITH_TOP_LEVEL_STATEMENTS`.
  > - Shared: `ResponsibilityLink.skillId`; `ResponsibilitySkillSummary.linkId` and `computed`; `skillId` in the link and lookup schemas; `skill` in `RESPONSIBILITY_FILE_ITEM_KINDS`; optional `skillIds` in Dashboard snapshot links.
- feat(routines): let agents list and change their person's routines in plain terms (#22274)
  > - `list_routines` is now live (it was a scenario-only mock) and keeps its scenario mock.
  > - Add `update_routine`. A `schedule` such as `{ frequency: "weekly", dayOfWeek: "monday", time: "09:00", timezone }` maps to the routine's schedule trigger through the existing routine-offer cadence helpers. `schedule: null` turns the schedule off. `fileUnder` files the routine under a responsibility.
  > - Add `server/src/services/routine-tools.ts`. It calls the routine routes with the run token. Fields and schedule go in one `PATCH /routines/:id` (one transaction and revision, guarded by `baseRevisionId`); filing follows, and if it fails after the routine changed the tool reports what was saved. Each part reports its own undo: the routine revision, or the Dashboard history for filing.
- feat(responsibilities): let agents change areas and responsibilities for their person, with undo (#22261)
  > - Add tools `create_area`, `update_area`, `delete_area`, `create_responsibility`, `update_responsibility`, `delete_responsibility`, `file_under_responsibility` and `remove_from_responsibility`. They are standard-mode only and hidden while Responsibilities is off.
  > - Add the writer rule for agents in `routes/agent-person-authz.ts` (`agentPersonInCompany`, `agentActsForResponsibilityOwner`), used by `routes/responsibilities.ts` and `routes/responsibility-areas.ts`. The person must have directed the run: a person can name anyone as a task's responsible person, and a run can also work for a routine's person or the company default, so those runs do not borrow that person's rights. An agent with no such person keeps the old propose-only rules.
  > - The response names only the version of the responsibility's owner (for area changes, the acting person's), and `X-Paperclip-Dashboard-Owner` says whose history holds it; tools return `dashboardOwnerUserId`. A change Undo cannot reverse (the name, description, order or un-archiving of an area other people's proposed or active responsibilities share) saves no version and is not reported as undoable.
- feat(responsibilities): let agents read a person's areas and responsibilities (#22253)
  > - Add `list_responsibilities` (areas with their responsibilities, responsibilities with no area, totals, and company work filed under no responsibility), `get_responsibility` (one responsibility with its filed items, link IDs, skills and latest status report), and `find_responsibilities_for` (where a project, task or routine is filed; a task can be a `PAP-12` identifier).
  > - Add `server/src/services/responsibility-tools.ts`. It calls the routes over HTTP with the run token and returns compact results with page links.
  > - When an agent sends no `ownerUserId`, the responsibility list, create, summary, automation and dashboard routes use the person the run works for (`onBehalfOfUserId`). A run that works for no person still gets a 422 with a clear message.
- feat(runner): start a released Runner again as the next run (#22288)
  > - Plugin SDK: `describe` can return `resumeIdentityVersion: 1`. `resume` takes an optional `runner: { runId, turnId, itemId }`. With `runner`, a `resumed` result must carry `runner` (the build) and `archivedState`. Without `runner`, `archivedState` is refused.
  > - Runner transport: `retireReleasedController()` retires a released controller and closes its route without detaching the host task. It returns the released source and the build it parked on, read from the control plane (`expectedRunner()`), so a build accepted at an earlier relaunch stays the minimum.
  > - Runner transport: when the driver names a `relaunchReleasedRunner` source, the rotation moves only the control plane, because the host archives the Runner state at launch. The rotation source must be that exact source.
- feat(runner): pin Codex 0.170.0 and Agent SDK 0.3.328 so Fable 5.2 and GPT-6.2 run (#22278)
  > - Claude runtime: `@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk` 0.3.286 → 0.3.328 (Claude Code 2.1.286 → 2.1.328). This changes the root `pnpm.overrides`, `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, the ACPX `claude` profile, and the qualified Claude runtime in `installation-integrity.ts`, with new executable digests for Linux x64, Linux ARM64, macOS ARM64, and macOS x64.
  > - Codex runtime: `@openai/codex` 0.160.0 → 0.170.0. This changes the runner dependency, the `codex-acp@1.6.2>@openai/codex` override, the `codex-acp` patch dependency line, the ACPX `codex` profile, and the qualified Linux x64 runtime with its new executable digest.
  > - The ACP bridges do not change: `claude-agent-acp` 0.73.0 and the patched `codex-acp` 1.6.2. Their `commandDigest` values and profile versions do not change.
- feat(telemetry): send product lifecycle events and agent run failure cause (#22250)
  > - Sync `generated/paperclip-telemetry.ts` from the backend branch as a direct copy. Regenerate `posthog-direct/enum-fallbacks.generated.ts` with `scripts/generate-posthog-direct-fixtures.mjs`. The loader golden file did not change.
  > - Add `afterTransactionCommit()` to `@paperclipai/db`. It runs a callback after the outermost transaction commits. It drops the callback on rollback. Outside a transaction it runs the callback at once. A callback that throws never fails the committed work.
  > - Add helpers `trackModelConnected`, `trackTaskCreated`, `trackTaskCompleted`, `trackTaskBlocked`, `trackPlanCreated`, `trackPlanApproved`, and `trackArtifactCreated`. Add `failureCause` to `trackAgentTaskRun`. Assign the new events to `operational_enum_count` retention.
- feat(analytics): attribute agent work and onboarding steps to a person on Cloud (#22260)
  > - `server/src/telemetry-company-scope.ts`: add `withTelemetryActor({ companyId, person }, fn)`, `scopedTelemetryPerson()`, and `resolveTelemetryPerson()`. The resolver picks the task creator, then the task's responsible user, then the run owner. It ignores `local-board`. `withTelemetryCompany` now calls `withTelemetryActor`.
  > - `server/src/services/posthog-direct/request-context.ts`: a signed-in request user wins (`attributed_via: request`). Without one, the scoped person is used.
  > - `server/src/services/posthog-direct/event-shape.ts`: every Cloud direct event (including `cloud.workspace_started`) has `attributed_via` (`request`, `task_creator`, `task_assigner`, `run_owner`, or `none`). An event with a person keeps today's person-event shape (no `$process_person_profile: false`).
- feat(ui): offer to cancel active subtasks (#22228)
  > - Add one shared cancellation prompt with a counted heading and rows that show the status icon and title. Keep the icons read-only. Preserve waiting, monitoring, and covered-blocker presentation without showing blocker IDs.
  > - Use the cancellation flow for shared task updates, child tasks, Coordinator task controls, routine executions, and **Stop and cancel**. The run stops after the person makes a choice.
  > - Load all pages of readable descendants, including waiting Slack and Speko subtasks. Explicit descendant lists retain these tasks under existing privacy and company filters. Ambient lists still hide waiting conversations.
- feat(server): fit PostHog proxy limits to busy Cloud stacks and make them configurable (#22243)
  > - Raised `DEFAULT_POSTHOG_PROXY_LIMITS` to 128 requests in flight per client address and 1024 in total. The 64 MiB body limit did not change.
  > - Added `PAPERCLIP_POSTHOG_PROXY_MAX_IN_FLIGHT` and `PAPERCLIP_POSTHOG_PROXY_MAX_IN_FLIGHT_PER_CLIENT`. `resolvePostHogProxyLimits` reads them where `resolvePostHogProxyMount` builds the proxy. A value must be a whole number from 1 to 65536. An invalid value logs a warning and uses the default. The server does not stop.
  > - Added `posthogProxyClientKey`. It returns `req.ip` only when it is a public address. For a private, carrier-grade NAT, loopback, link-local or other reserved address, or no address, the proxy applies only the total limit. The key never comes from a client header.
- feat(ui): align routine controls and remember list views (#22182)
  > - Align both Routines toolbars with the requested order and shared search and button styles.
  > - Extract the existing Tasks filter group and checkbox row for reuse by Routines.
  > - Add project, agent, enabled, responsible person, and source filters.
- feat(cloud): migrate durable data online and name volume-free config blockers (#22240)
  > - New `services/online-durable-data-migration.ts`. The pass:
  > - `skill-package-storage-migration.ts`: a new `online` mode. In this mode a row that another owner holds is skipped and counted as `busy`. The row claim and the publication already use compare-and-swap. The offline mode is unchanged and still fails closed on a busy row.
  > - `services/ephemeral-enrollment.ts`: a configuration check maps the volume-free contract errors to blocker codes. Preparation and the eligibility read both use this check.
- feat(adapters): add Claude Fable 5.2 and GPT-6.2 with CLI floors, and refresh sandbox CLI pins (Nov 18 audit) (#22222)
  > - Claude Code (`claude_local`):
  > - Paperclip Runner (ACPX Codex): `resolvePaperclipRunnerProviderProfile` rejects a model whose Codex floor is above the qualified ACPX Codex runtime (`0.160.0`). The error code is `paperclip_runner_codex_cli_version_incompatible`, and the message gives both versions.
  > - Paperclip Runner (ACPX Claude): `resolvePaperclipRunnerProviderProfile` rejects a model whose Claude Code floor is above the version that the qualified Agent SDK bundles. The error code is `paperclip_runner_claude_cli_version_incompatible`. The message gives both versions. A new helper, `claudeCodeVersionForAgentSdk`, maps SDK `0.3.N` to Claude Code `2.1.N` (the SDK's `claudeCodeVersion` field) and returns `null` for any other line.
- feat(routines): ship the redesigned routine page and let live webhooks be tested again (#22205)
  > - `/routines/:id` renders `RoutinePage` in both shells (`RoutineDetail.production.tsx` is removed). Status switch, Repeats rows, default agent, responsible user, project, Asks for, Secrets, Run rules, Managed by plugin, Instructions with Show all, copy and the comment count, and History.
  > - History is paged: 20 tasks per page from `GET /companies/:id/issues?originKind=routine_execution&originId=…&sortField=created&sortDir=desc&limit=21&offset=…`, with a real Load more. Coalesced runs show as "folded into a task".
  > - A failed refetch keeps the loaded page on screen (`useQueryView`); a History failure shows `QueryErrorState` in place.
- feat(connections): AWS connector with credentials minted per connection (#22195)
  > - `packages/shared`:
  > - `server/src/services/connectors/aws/mcp-credentials.ts`:
  > - Gateway (`resolveCredentialHeadersUnrecorded`), credential access (`resolveCredentialHeaders`), catalog discovery and health-failure mapping:
- feat(analytics): ask EU, UK and Swiss visitors before Cloud browser analytics (#22186)
  > - `packages/shared`: `browserAnalytics` has a new optional `consentRequired: boolean | null`.
  > - `packages/shared/src/analytics-consent.ts` (new): the one region policy. `countryRequiresAnalyticsConsent` (EU and EEA with outermost regions and Svalbard, GB with the Crown Dependencies and Gibraltar, CH) and `timeZoneRequiresAnalyticsConsent` (`Europe/*`, EU, EEA and UK zones outside Europe, and unknown zones such as `UTC` or `Etc/*`). The server and the UI both use it.
  > - `server`: `resolveBrowserAnalyticsConfig` sets `consentRequired` from the header that `PAPERCLIP_POSTHOG_COUNTRY_HEADER` names. No header or an unknown code (`XX`) gives `null`.
- feat(telemetry): add company_id to Cloud direct events from background work (#22183)
  > - Add `server/src/telemetry-company-scope.ts`. It has `withTelemetryCompany(companyId, fn)` and `scopedTelemetryCompanyId()`. With no company id, it calls `fn` directly.
  > - `capturePostHogEventContext()` uses the scoped company when no live request gives a company. The request company, user, and session behave as before.
  > - Wrap these emit sites with the company of their entity:
- feat(connections): per-company workload identity issuer with public signing keys (#22083)
  > - `packages/db`: new table `company_workload_identity_keys` and migration `0498`. The migration only creates a new table, so the previous release keeps working. Partial unique indexes allow one `active` key and one `next` key per company.
  > - `server/src/services/workload-identity.ts`: lazy key creation with concurrent convergence, a private-key cache with a fail-closed key match check, the discovery document, the JWKS, `signConnectionToken`, and rotation steps. A key that is no longer active stays published for 24 hours. Paperclip calculates this when it reads the keys, so no background job is necessary.
  > - `server/src/routes/workload-identity.ts`: public `GET /oidc/companies/{companyId}/.well-known/openid-configuration` and `GET /oidc/companies/{companyId}/jwks.json` (404 until a key exists). Board `GET /api/companies/{companyId}/workload-identity` and `POST .../workload-identity/rotation`, with an activity log entry for each rotation.
- feat(dashboard): add an experimental areas and responsibilities dashboard (#22069)
  > - Add the setting "Areas & Responsibilities dashboard" (`enableResponsibilities`, off by default). On, Dashboard in the sidebar opens the new page, and the classic dashboard link is not shown in the sidebars or the command palette. Off, nothing changes.
  > - Add tables and migrations 0492 to 0497 for areas, responsibilities, links to work, metrics, status reports, saved dashboard versions, and model-call records for summaries.
  > - Add the API: areas, responsibilities, links, bars, metrics, status reports, summaries, runs, automation, and the dashboard conversation with versions and undo. Each route checks company access and writes to the activity log.
- feat(connections): end Google access when a Paperclip-managed Google connection is removed (#22065)
  > - `paperclip-cloud-connector.ts`: `revoke()` takes an optional `timeoutMs`, returns the broker's `outcome` from an allowlist, and keeps `REVOCATION_NOT_AVAILABLE` as an allowed rejection reason.
  > - New `tool-access/connections/google-access-removal.ts`. For a managed Google connection, `prepare()` reads each removed grant's refresh token and returns a `send()` step. `send()` calls the broker for each distinct token, one after another, under one 5-second deadline. Every result and every error (including a Paperclip Cloud client that cannot be created) becomes an outcome code. It never rejects and never logs a token. An unexpected error is logged with the error name only and reported as `unexpected_error`.
  > - Order for removing a whole connection: read the tokens, run the whole local teardown (grants, installs, app profile, gateway tokens, runtime slots, catalog, OAuth state, archive the connection), call the broker, delete the sent refresh-token secrets whatever the outcome, then delete the remaining secrets. A failure anywhere in the teardown makes no broker call. Order for removing one grant: read the token, commit the revocation, call the broker, delete the grant's refresh-token secret. `send()` owns the call and the deletion through an injected secret-removal function.
- feat(github): steer active reviews when new messages arrive (#22066)
  > - Add GitHub-owned input steering after the existing authorization and wake admission path.
  > - Require the same task, agent, conversation, responsible person, and source trust level. Keep attachments and fresh-session input on separate turns.
  > - Reserve requester identity before delivery. Activate it after acknowledgement. Consume the exact saved wake and associate automatic review records with the active run.
- feat(analytics): show session replay text by default, mask only customer content (#22057)
  > - `privacy.ts`: `ANALYTICS_MASK_TEXT_SELECTOR` is now `.ph-mask, .ph-no-capture, [contenteditable]:not([contenteditable="false"])`. rrweb masks a text node only when its parent or an ancestor matches the selector. Removed `maskReplayText`, `isReplayTextRevealed`, `ANALYTICS_UNMASK_ATTRIBUTE` and `ANALYTICS_UNMASK_PROPS`.
  > - `product-analytics.ts`: removed `maskTextFn`, so rrweb uses its default star masking. `maskAllInputs`, `blockClass`, `maskAttributeFn`, `mask_all_text` and `mask_all_element_attributes` do not change.
  > - Removed the `analyticsChrome` prop from `Button`, `Label`, `TabsTrigger`, `EmptyState` and `SidebarNavItem`, and from all sidebar call sites.
- feat(google-workspace): gateway sets the REST tool result size limit for all six services; update connector docs (#22050)
  > - `tool.ts`: `defineTool` takes `whenTooLarge` advice per read tool (required for reads, refused for writes) and a `ToolLimits` argument. It measures the final `CallToolResult` once (`withinResultBudget`) and replaces an oversized result with a `too_large` tool error. `FALLBACK_MAX_RESULT_BYTES` (250,000) applies only when a direct caller gives no limit.
  > - `callGoogleWorkspaceRestTool` and `createGoogleWorkspaceRestServer` take `maxResultBytes`. A value that is not a positive integer is refused before any request.
  > - All 23 read tools state their advice: a smaller `pageSize` for list tools, `messageFormat` or `view` for Gmail, a narrower range for Sheets, `fields` for Slides. Write tools are not checked: a completed write is never reported as an error, so an agent is not led to repeat it.
- feat(cloud): read-only durable-data eligibility for online enrollment (#22051)
  > - `routes/ephemeral-enrollment.ts`: new `GET /instance/ephemeral-enrollment/eligibility`. It requires a verified Cloud control claim for `ephemeral-enrollment:eligibility` and sets `Cache-Control: no-store`.
  > - `services/ephemeral-enrollment.ts`: new `readOnlineEphemeralEnrollmentEligibility`. It uses the same check as preparation (`purpose: "startup"`) and returns only the eligibility flag, the canonical blocker names, and whether this server is already volume-free.
  > - `middleware/cloud-control.ts` and `services/cloud-runtime-identity.ts`: register the endpoint and the action. Each assertion stays bound to one action, so a prepare token cannot read eligibility, and the reverse.
- feat(google-workspace): REST-backed Drive tools in server/src/google-workspace-rest-tools (#22028)
  > - `drive/`: `search_files`, `list_recent_files`, `get_file_metadata`, `get_file_permissions`, `read_file_content`, `download_file_content` (reads, `drive.readonly`), `create_file`, `copy_file` (writes, `drive.file`). One file per tool, pure request and result functions, table-driven tests with synthetic fixtures.
  > - `drive/query.ts`: parses Google's MCP search syntax into a tree, checks terms and operators, and writes a new Drive `q` string with every value re-escaped. Adds `trashed = false` unless the query uses `trashed`. Unsupported terms are errors, not ignored.
  > - Content: Google Docs are exported as Markdown (read) or plain text (download), Sheets as CSV of the first sheet (the read text says so), Slides as plain text. Binary files are downloaded and base64 encoded. Every Drive read result is bounded after serialization to 250,000 bytes (the payload is carried twice, in text and structured content); a larger result is an error that says how to read less, never cut-short content. `exportMimeType` must be a format Google lists for the file type, and every request uses the caller's `fileId`. `create_file` and `copy_file` are annotated as non-idempotent writes; an annotations test checks all eight tools.
- feat(mcp): graduate Assistant connections (MCP) out of experimental (#22015)
  > - `packages/shared`: `enablePublicMcp` is a deprecated compatibility key. The schema default is `true`. The feature catalog entry is "Assistant connections (compatibility)" with both defaults `true`.
  > - `server` instance settings: normalization always returns `enablePublicMcp: true`. The Cloud managed overlay skips the key, so a fleet value of `false` cannot disable it. Removed the key from the Cloud staging experimental-key list. Removed the client-mirror policy step that ran only when the toggle changed.
  > - `server` public MCP: personal discovery, OAuth, tools, setup and event delivery no longer read instance settings (`isEnabled()` returns `true` for the personal endpoint without a DB read). Dot, Grok and Muse agent connections keep their own experimental settings.
- feat(public-mcp): let paperclip_create_task assign a person or no one (#22011)
  > - `paperclip_create_task`: `assigneeAgentId` is optional. The tool accepts `assigneeUserId`, with the same shape as the REST issues API.
  > - `paperclip_create_task`: a schema refinement rejects a call that has both assignees. The rejection occurs before the server writes an idempotency receipt or a task.
  > - `paperclip_create_task`: the tool description says that an agent assignee may start work and spend budget, and that a person or no assignee does not start a run. The `scheduling` result text now matches the assignee type.
- feat(connections): name and guide connections to another Paperclip instance (#22018)
  > - `packages/shared/src/paperclip-peer.ts`: detect a Paperclip MCP address, derive a label and name, compare with own origins (loopback hosts on the same port are equal), and build default agent instructions.
  > - `server/src/services/tool-access.ts`: default name and default agent instructions for a new Paperclip connection. Instructions that the person supplies are kept.
  > - `server/src/routes/tool-access.ts`: refuse the instance's own address before any network request.
- feat(google-workspace): REST-backed Docs and Slides tools; mark Calendar update_event destructive (#22029)
  > - `docs/`: `read_doc` (`documents.get` with `includeTabsContent=true`) and `update_doc` (`documents.batchUpdate`), a renderer for every tab, child tab, heading, list (real numbering), table, link, header, footer, footnote, chip, suggestion and comment, and an index structure where each paragraph's `text.length` equals `endIndex - startIndex`.
  > - `slides/`: `read_presentation` (`presentations.get`; with `fields` it returns Google's resource as is) and `update_presentation` (`presentations.batchUpdate`), a renderer for every slide's text, tables, groups, media and speaker notes.
  > - `documents-shared.ts`: the 250,000-byte limit on the final serialized result (an error, never a cut-short result), the revision-conflict error, comment rendering, and argument checks.
- feat(cloud): browser product analytics through a same-origin PostHog proxy (#22012)
  > - Uses the shared Cloud PostHog config (`resolveCloudPostHogConfig` in `server/src/services/cloud-posthog-config.ts`, already on master from the server direct-sending change). This PR does not change it.
  > - `server/src/services/cloud-posthog-browser.ts`: `resolveBrowserAnalyticsConfig` returns `{ token, apiHost: "/_ph", uiHost, environment, stackId, replayEnabled }` only when `PAPERCLIP_POSTHOG_BROWSER_ENABLED=1`. `environment` is always set: the explicit variable, else `staging` for a `*.staging.paperclip.app` host, else `production`.
  > - `GET /api/auth/get-session` carries the new optional `browserAnalytics` field (the existing signed-in bootstrap request, so no new request on the cold path). `packages/shared` has `browserAnalyticsConfigSchema` and the session schema field.
- feat(google-workspace): REST-backed Sheets tools in server/src/google-workspace-rest-tools (#22027)
  > - `sheets/get-values.ts`: `get_values` over `spreadsheets.values.get`, formatted values, rows first (the REST defaults, sent explicitly). Google's answer is returned unchanged (`range`, `values`).
  > - `sheets/get-spreadsheet.ts`: `get_spreadsheet` over `spreadsheets.get` with `includeGridData`, `ranges` and `fields`. `commentsIncluded: true` is refused because REST cannot return comments.
  > - `sheets/update-values.ts` and `sheets/update-formulas.ts`: `spreadsheets.values.update` with `valueInputOption` `RAW` and `USER_ENTERED`. Each description names its option.
- feat(google-workspace): remaining Gmail read tools in the REST tool set (#22014)
  > - Add `get_thread`, `list_drafts` and `list_labels`. Their schemas come from Google's Gmail MCP reference.
  > - Add `get_draft`, `list_threads` and `search_messages`. Google's reference does not list them, so their schemas copy the sibling tools. The README lists them under "Not verified against Google's servers".
  > - Move separately stored body reads from `get_message` into `readMessageBodies` in `gmail/message.ts`. All tools that return bodies use it. A body that cannot be read fails the call with "Could not read the full body of this message".
- feat(google-workspace): per-service transport switch for REST-backed tools (off by default) (#22016)
  > - `packages/shared/src/google-workspace-connectors.ts`: adds `GOOGLE_WORKSPACE_TOOL_TRANSPORT` (every service is `google_mcp`), `transport` on the result of `googleWorkspaceToolTarget`, and `googleWorkspaceServiceToolNames`.
  > - New `server/src/services/google-workspace-rest-dispatch.ts`: the only importer of the REST folder. It keeps only the bearer token from the gateway's credential headers, passes the gateway's egress-guarded fetch and cancellation signal, and returns either the MCP result or a token problem. `googleWorkspaceRestTransportGaps()` lists allowlisted tools that a `paperclip_rest` service does not implement. Today Calendar is eligible (9 of 9) and Gmail is not (3 of 9); nothing is flipped.
  > - `server/src/services/tool-gateway.ts` (`executeRemoteHttpTool`): for a `paperclip_rest` tool, the gateway rechecks the current grant, calls the adapter, and returns through the same result handling as the MCP path. Google's insufficient-scope answer maps to `oauth_insufficient_scope`. A rejected token forces a refresh and returns `oauth_refreshed_retry_required` without a replay. A passed deadline returns `tool_timeout`. The audit record names the protocol `Google Workspace REST`. The grant recheck is now one shared helper for both paths. A timed-out Google Workspace write, on either transport, now returns `tool_timeout` with a warning that the change may or may not have been made; a timed-out read keeps the plain timeout message.
- feat(google-workspace): REST-backed Calendar tools in server/src/google-workspace-rest-tools (#22017)
  > - `calendar/`: nine tools, shared event mapping (`event.ts`), input building (`event-input.ts`), JSON schemas, synthetic fixtures, and `probeCalendar()` (one `calendarList.list` with `maxResults=1`) for a health check.
  > - `calendar/time.ts`: time zone arithmetic with `Intl` only. It reads offset-less times in a given IANA zone, handles DST gaps and repeated hours (Temporal `compatible` rule), and never guesses a zone.
  > - `google-http.ts`: adds `www.googleapis.com` to the allowed hosts, but only under `/calendar/v3/` (checked after URL normalization). Adds PATCH, PUT and DELETE. All are writes: never retried, and failures after dispatch carry the uncertain-outcome warning. A POST marked `readOnly` (free/busy) is a read. Adds `If-Match`. Maps 412 to "nothing was changed" and 410 to not found.
- feat(connections): let users delete data read through a connection (#22010)
  > - `packages/db`: new `tool_connection_content_deletions` table and migration `0490` (online compatible: new table and its indexes only).
  > - `packages/shared`: `ToolConnectionStoredContent`, `ToolConnectionContentDeletion` and counts types; optional `contentDeletedAt` on `ToolRedactedValueSummary`.
  > - `server`: `RunLogStore.remove`, which deletes every copy of a run's log (`composeRunLogStore`); `content-deletion.ts` service; deadline worker wired into startup and pre-drain; `GET /tool-connections/:id/stored-content`, `POST` and `GET /tool-connections/:id/stored-content/deletion`, and `DELETE /tool-connections/:id?deleteStoredContent=true`; OpenAPI entries.
- feat(telemetry): send Cloud server telemetry directly to PostHog (#22006)
  > - Add `server/src/services/cloud-posthog-config.ts`. `resolveCloudPostHogConfig()` returns `null` unless the instance is Cloud-managed and has a token and a stack ID. `isPostHogDirectDeliveryEnabled()` also requires an environment and the kill switch on (`PAPERCLIP_POSTHOG_DIRECT` is not `0`).
  > - Add a narrow `TelemetryTransport` seam to `TelemetryClient` in `packages/shared`. With a transport, each flushed batch goes to the transport and never to the collector. Without one, the client does what it did before. A transport can capture per-event context at track time. The context does not go into the collector envelope.
  > - Typed helpers hash `task_id` unless the transport sets `hashesPrivateRefs: false`. Private `source_ref` values (for example a private repository name) are customer content, so they stay hashed on every path, Cloud included, with `source_ref_hashed: true`.
- feat(runner): accept a resume-compatible build for a relaunched released Runner (#21976)
  > - Plugin SDK: optional `resumeRelaunchedRunnerVersion` on the description, `relaunchedRunnerVersion` on the `resume` operation, and `runner` on the `resume` result.
  > - `runnerVersionCanResume`: a new version rule. A newer semantic version can read older state. Equal versions are compatible across builds. A prerelease is older than its release.
  > - `DurablePrpControlPlane.acceptRelaunchedRunner`: checks the build against the expected build. It then commits the claim's unused bootstrap ticket in one store write before it changes the expected build. It refuses when an authenticated Runner is connected, the ticket is unknown or used, the digest is malformed, or the version cannot read the parked state.
- feat(google-workspace): REST-backed Gmail tools in server/src/google-workspace-rest-tools (#22000)
  > - New folder `server/src/google-workspace-rest-tools/` inside the existing server package. No new npm package, no new dependency, no workspace, Docker, lockfile or release change. Every changed file is in this folder.
  > - `index.ts` is the only entry point. `boundary.test.ts` enforces that the folder imports only its own files, Node built-ins, `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` and `zod` (both existing server dependencies; tests may also import `vitest` and `@paperclipai/shared`), and that no other server code imports the folder yet.
  > - `google-http.ts` is the only code that calls `googleapis.com`. It sets the bearer header, allows only listed hosts, has a 20 s deadline and a 20 MB response limit, and retries a read once. It never retries a write. Every write that fails after it is sent says that the change may or may not have been made.
- feat(connections): show a data-use disclosure in the Google Workspace picker (#21998)
  > - `ui/src/features/connections/googleWorkspaceDisclosure.ts` (new): a pure function `googleWorkspaceDataUseDisclosure(selection)` returns the plain sentences for the selected services and the use/sharing sentences. It also exports the privacy policy URL (`https://paperclip.ing/privacy`).
  > - `ui/src/features/connections/GoogleWorkspaceServices.tsx`: `ServicePicker` shows the disclosure above the footer when one or more services are on. With no services on, it keeps the existing "Select at least one service to continue." message and shows no disclosure. The privacy link opens in a new tab. The dialog now scrolls inside 85% of the viewport height, so the footer stays reachable on a phone. This one picker serves new setup and reconnect, in the Apps flow and in task-hosted setup.
  > - `ui/src/features/connections/GoogleWorkspaceServices.test.tsx`: tests for each service at each access level, picker order, Gmail send wording (read only never mentions sending; read & write only says that Paperclip does not send), the empty selection, the rendered block, the link, and the block's position before the footer.

  > - Assert that the fallback returned by the server includes this model.
  > - Preserve the existing first model, claude-fable-5-1.
- feat(links): cache rich link status in the database and load it on idle (#21746)
  > - Add an additive, repeat-safe `link_previews` migration. Store channel, resource kind, free-form status, JSON metadata and freshness. The table has no task relationship or GitHub status enum.
  > - Bound each company/viewer cache to 1,000 rows across concurrent server instances. Remove rows expired for over 30 days during writes. Limit provider lookups to 600 requests per minute per scope/process. Keep cached batch reads available during backoff.
  > - Add a database store owned by the link service. Reuse canonical results across tasks. Keep company and viewer isolation. Save hover and idle results. Preserve last known status during an outage and cache unavailable results.
- feat(ui): add foldable sidebar subtasks with bounded refreshes (#21649)
  > - Add shared parent groups to both sidebar layouts. Preserve Classic row actions and active-child visibility.
  > - Keep unread state personal to each task. Folding and paging do not mark tasks read. Counts include readable children beyond ten.
  > - Target live refreshes with authorized current and previous parent IDs. Reuse parent metadata from the narrow run-binding read. Resolve fallback lookups once per delivery batch. Keep the previous summary fallback for older servers.
- feat(native): send launch preparation and run digests to plugin task prepare (#21717)
  > - `packages/paperclip-runner/src/live/runner-provider-launch.ts`: add `runnerLaunchPreparationDigest` and `runnerLaunchRunDigest`. The preparation digest drops the MCP assignment and binding; the run digest takes the full requirements and includes them. Both use the same canonical JSON as `runnerLaunchRequirementsDigest`, with their own schema tags so the three digests never collide. Export them from the package and the server's vendored runner shim.
  > - `packages/plugins/sdk/src/environment-tasks.ts`: add optional `preparationDigest` and `runDigest` to the `prepare` operation, and optional `launchDigestVersion: 1` to the task description. The capability gates both digests.
  > - `server/src/services/native-runtime/plugin-runner-task.ts` and `native-session-executor.ts`: send both digests on `prepare` when the description advertises `launchDigestVersion: 1`.
- feat(server): add an instance-admin server metrics endpoint for the scale ramp (#21708)
  > - `server/src/services/server-metrics.ts`: a per-process collector. It uses `performance.eventLoopUtilization()`, `monitorEventLoopDelay` (20 ms), `process.cpuUsage()`, the main thread's `/proc/self/task/<pid>/stat` on Linux, and six 10 s buckets of native `perf_hooks` histograms. One unref'd timer rotates the buckets. Nothing on the hot path awaits, allocates per call or does I/O.
  > - `server/src/routes/instance-server-metrics.ts`: `GET /api/instance/server-metrics`, guarded by the existing `assertInstanceAdmin` (the check that `/instance/database-backups` and other instance routes use). It returns numbers, a random per-process `processId`, `uptimeSec`, `buildRevision` and `windowMs`. It returns no SQL, names, IDs or hostnames.
  > - `server/src/app.ts`: when the flag is on, two middlewares time agent API requests from arrival to the API router, the route is mounted, and shutdown stops the collector. When the flag is off, nothing is mounted.

- feat(ci): report the required checks on merge-queue commits (#21545)
  > - Added `.github/workflows/merge-queue-checks.yml`. It reports `ci / verify` and `ci / e2e` on merge-group commits.
  > - Added `scripts/__tests__/merge-queue-checks-workflow.test.mjs`. It fails if the names differ from the names that `pr.yml` reports, or if the workflow gains a checkout, an action, a secret, a token permission or a self-hosted runner.
  > - `pr-trusted.yml` now runs that test and `scripts/merge-group-image.test.mjs` in a step that skips test files an older checkout does not have. The second test came with #21096 but no CI step ran it.
- feat(connections): machine sign-in (MCP OAuth client credentials) for organization connections (#21473)
  > - `oauth/client-credentials.ts`: keypair generation (RS256, or ES256 when the service requires it), RFC 7638 key IDs, and RFC 7523 assertions (`iss` = `sub` = client ID, `aud` = token endpoint, 60 s `exp`, unique `jti`) with Node `crypto` only.
  > - `oauth/client.ts`: the token exchange accepts a signed assertion and then sends only `client_assertion_type` and `client_assertion` (no `client_id` or secret), as the extension specifies.
  > - `oauth/client-credentials-service.ts`: discovery of the sign-in service (protected-resource metadata, then RFC 8414/OIDC metadata with issuer check, or a token URL entered directly), issuer pinning, vault-only key and secret handling, draft creation, test-and-activate, and no-downtime rotation (test the new secret first; stage a new key and switch only after the provider accepts it).
- feat(mcp): show live progress for long connected-app tool calls in the task chat (#21449)
  > - `mcp-http.ts`: `mcpHttpRequestInit` can add `_meta.progressToken`. `readMcpHttpResponse` gives each notification on the response stream to an observer before the result. The observer cannot fail or delay the call. New `mcpProgressNotificationOf` matches a notification to its token.
  > - `tool-gateway.ts`: remote HTTP and local stdio `tools/call` from an agent run send a per-call token and forward progress to a reporter. Calls without a run send no token.
  > - New `server/src/services/tool-call-progress.ts`: checks each update (finite, increasing, plain single-line text of at most 200 characters). It publishes a `heartbeat.run.tool_progress` live event at most every 250 ms (latest wins) and saves the latest update on the invocation at most every 1 s. Nothing is awaited by the call. A final `done` event goes out when the call ends.
- feat(mcp-tasks): add a demo MCP server that uses Tasks, test against it, and keep audit records out of agent errors (#21464)
  > - Add an MCP Tasks demo server to `packages/kv-demo-mcp-server` (new bin `paperclip-tasks-demo-mcp-server`; run `node packages/kv-demo-mcp-server/dist/tasks/main.js` after a build). It is stateless MCP 2026-07-28 with `server/discover`, and it declares `io.modelcontextprotocol/tasks`. It has six tools: `slow_report`, `needs_approval` (form question), `will_fail`, `cancellable`, `expires`, and `quick_echo`. It saves task state to a file, so it can restart mid-task. It needs no credentials. It has a status page at `/` and JSON at `/api/state`.
  > - I put it in the existing demo package, so there is no new workspace package and no `pnpm-lock.yaml` change.
  > - Add `server/src/__tests__/mcp-tasks-demo-server.test.ts`. It runs the real gateway against the real demo server over HTTP, with one test per tool plus a restart.
- feat(mcp): ask an app's form questions in the task chat and send the answer back (MCP elicitation) (#21466)
  > - **Plan:** `doc/plans/2026-11-13-mcp-elicitation.md` covers the spec, the inventory, the decisions, the journey, tests, and risks.
  > - **New module `server/src/services/mcp-elicitation.ts`.** It is the single mapping for direct calls and MCP Tasks:
  > - **New table `tool_mcp_elicitations`** (migration 0479). It is company-scoped, with one row per question round. It holds a keyed call identity, the sealed state, the questions and their cards, a claim time, and a 24-hour expiry.

  > - `.github/workflows/docker.yml`: the job `runner_image` replaces `runner_image_report`.

- feat(slack): show useful progress without extra messages (#20827)
  > - Select completed `agentMessage` items from the normalized PRP `progress` channel. Keep unknown channels, reasoning, tool output, and raw provider fields out of progress text.
  > - Sanitize each note. Keep at most two sentences and 400 characters. Add a task link when a public board URL is available.
  > - Prefer public notes over general tool status. Remove repeated text and combine bursts into the latest pending note.
- feat(plugins): send configured environment on plugin task resume (#21352)
  > - Rename `resumeCredentialVersion` → `resumeLaunchInputsVersion` in the SDK `describe` result schema, executor plumbing, owner input, docs and tests.
  > - With the capability, `resume` also adds `configuredEnvironment` when the provider advertises `configuredEnvironmentVersion: 1`. A normal resume does not mark the variables as delivered, since a live Runner keeps the environment it started with.
  > - Docs: the "Release and resume" section describes both fields; the "Configured environment" section points to it.
- feat(ssh): use the agent AI account in new environments (#21248)
  > - Make the retention-pagination fixture include an eligible agent error explicitly. Keep deterministic archived and kept error agents as exclusion cases. Preserve the strict kind, pagination, and oracle comparisons.
  > - Default new SSH API records to managed authentication when no choice is sent. Honor explicit choices and preserve absent fields on legacy reads and updates.
  > - Start SSH ACP with a non-login shell after preparing its private home. Keep the remote user's prepared command path. Sandbox launches retain their login-shell policy.
- feat(apps): ship the redesigned sign-in step for a pasted MCP server (#21469)
  > - `ui/src/features/connections/GenericMcpConnectStep.tsx`: new step component (same props as the old `LinkConnectStep`). It uses stable field ids `generic-mcp-key`, `generic-mcp-client-id`, `generic-mcp-client-secret` and a `data-testid` on the sign-in Change/Done link.
  > - `ui/src/features/connections/ConnectionSetupFlow.tsx`: render `GenericMcpConnectStep`; delete `LinkConnectStep`, `GENERIC_AUTH_MODE_OPTIONS`, `SegmentedOption` and unused imports; change the pasted-link stepper label from "Add your key" to "Connect" (`STEP_LABELS` is used only by the pasted-link flow; curated apps keep their own `["Add your key"]`).
  > - `ui/src/pages/apps/generic-mcp-connect.ts`: `oauth_challenge` and `oauth_manual_client_required` guidance now say "below" instead of "under Advanced authentication".
- feat(queue): show tasks waiting for an agent's slot as waiting, with their place in line (#21405)
  > - Server: a new module `server/src/services/heartbeat/queued-run-line.ts` now holds the facts that the queued-run dispatcher orders by (`queuedRunDispatchFacts`). The dispatch path in `queue.ts` and the new "place in line" read use this one builder, so the line the UI shows is the dispatch order. The logic moved without change, including the recovery-share fix from #21391.
  > - Server: `executionProjectionsForRuns` adds `agentQueue` (agent, limit, running count, position, queued count) and the label "Waiting for <agent>" with cause `agent_at_capacity` to a queued, never-started run when its agent has no free slot. It reads one batch of agents and running counts. Then it builds one line per full agent (at most 8 agents per read), using only the three context keys that ordering needs. No new polling.
  > - Server: the place in line uses `queuedRunDispatchLine`, an O(n log n) replay of the dispatcher. The plain replay rescans the queue after each step, which is quadratic (measured: 3.6 ms at 300 runs, 76 ms at 3,000, 600 ms at 10,000, all on the event loop). Dispatch itself is unchanged: it stops after the free slots. Each agent's line is memoized for 5 s and shared by concurrent reads. In a preview worktree, the line applies the same creation-time cutoff as dispatch.
- feat(mcp): keep long provider calls durable with the MCP Tasks extension (#21263)
  > - **Off by default:** MCP Tasks are behind the new instance experimental setting `enableMcpTasks` ("Durable app actions (MCP Tasks)"), default off. When it is off, Paperclip never declares the extension, nothing attaches to or polls tasks, pending rows do not block idle sleep, and behavior matches master. A test covers the off state.
  > - `mcp-http.ts`: the tasks extension is declared per request, only on HTTP `tools/call` and `tasks/*`. Discovery, listings, and stdio servers do not declare it. `tasks/*` requests put the task ID in `Mcp-Name`.
  > - New `mcp-tasks.ts`: parses task results, bounds the server's timing hints, sends `tasks/get`, `tasks/update`, and `tasks/cancel`, and seals task IDs at rest (AES-256-GCM, bound to company and connection; a keyed hash gives uniqueness).
- feat(presentation): offer Move it forward now while a continuation is still waiting to start (#21385)
  > - `server/src/services/task-interruption-presentation.ts`: the fresh-continuation read also loads the record status, schema, `recordedAt` and reconciled run. A `continuing` state can now carry `pendingSince` when the primary record is `resolved`, `continuationDelivery` is `pending`, the schema is the fresh-continuation schema, and the reconciled run is the observed run. These are the same facts that `undeliveredContinuation` checks at click time.
  > - The projection offers `canRetry: true, retryAction: "move_forward"` (state `working`) when `pendingSince` is 15 minutes or older, no person Retry for that run already waits, and the run's agent is the current assignee. Under 15 minutes, nothing changes.
  > - No button when the system gave up delivery (`invalidated`; the normal Retry rules apply), when the delivery is `not_requested`, when a person already asked, or for a held restore (the existing execution-blocker post-filter removes the offer).
- feat(mcp-apps): view messages with consent, app context, full screen and provider tool icons (#21266)
  > - **`ui/message`.** The host shows "<App> wants to send: …" with **Send** and **Dismiss** under the widget (and inside the full-screen dialog). Nothing is sent before the click. Send posts the text as the viewer's own comment through the chat's normal send path (`handleThreadAdd`). `POST /api/issues/:issueId/mcp-app-views/:viewId/messages` then logs `issue.mcp_app_message_sent`. It accepts only the caller's own comment on the same task, matched by `clientRequestId`, from the last 10 minutes. One proposal per view can wait at a time. The view gets `{}` after the send, or -32000 "Message sending denied" after Dismiss. Only `user` text up to 8,000 characters is accepted.
  > - **`ui/update-model-context`.** `PUT /api/issues/:issueId/mcp-app-views/:viewId/model-context` stores the latest context per view in `tool_mcp_app_views.model_context` (text blocks and `structuredContent`, 16 KB at most). Updates are applied in order and deduped in the browser and on the server. A change logs `issue.mcp_app_context_updated`, and an empty update clears the context. The task's next run gets the three most recently updated contexts as an `## App context (untrusted)` wake-prompt section. The section quotes the data as JSON and says it is third-party data, not instructions. The read runs in parallel with the other run preparation and never fails the run. CLI adapters and the native runner render it through the same `renderPaperclipWakePrompt`.
  > - **Who may do this.** Messages and context need a signed-in person with `issue:comment` on the task. Agents and API keys get 403. Other tasks and companies get 404. `message` and `updateModelContext` are advertised in `hostCapabilities` only where the viewer can send messages.
- feat(presentation): let a person move a waiting continuation forward without starting a second run (#21373)
  > - `server/src/services/task-interruption-presentation.ts`: for a task whose latest run is `queued`, created 15 minutes ago or more, and named as `continuationRunId` by a delivered fresh-continuation record, the projection returns `state: "working"`, `canRetry: true`, `retryAction: "move_forward"`, responsibility `system`, `observedRunId` = that queued run. It does not offer this when a person's wake already joined the run, when the run's agent is not the assignee, or when a person-owned wait applies (approval, reconnect, budget, pause and the others are checked first). Two small reads run only for queued runs older than 15 minutes. The existing restore-hold check that withdraws `canRetry` also withdraws `retryAction`.
  > - `packages/shared/src/types/execution-projection.ts`: new optional `retryAction?: "move_forward"` on `TaskInterruptionPresentation`. It only changes the label; `canRetry` still decides whether Retry is offered.
  > - `ui/src/components/ExecutionBlockerNotice.tsx`: a `working` presentation with `retryAction: "move_forward"` shows "Waiting for the agent to finish other work. Paperclip continues this task by itself." and a "Move it forward now" button that calls the same task Retry. Token-only classes; `pnpm check:token-gates` is clean.
- feat(plugins): send the claimant's credential on plugin task resume (#21335)
  > - `resume` gains an optional `credential`, with the same schema and validation as `submit`'s.
  > - `describe` gains an optional `resumeLaunchInputsVersion: 1` capability.
  > - With the capability, `resume` also carries `configuredEnvironment` (same builder as `submit`) when the provider advertises `configuredEnvironmentVersion: 1`. A normal resume does not mark the variables as delivered, since a live Runner keeps the environment it started with.
- feat(plugins): deliver configured environment to plugin Runner tasks (#21280)
  > - **SDK contract** (`packages/plugins/sdk/src/environment-tasks.ts`):
  > - **Server**:
  > - **Codex** (`codex-security-config.ts`):
- feat(ci): read a registry build cache in the Runner image build (#21310)
  > - The build legs pass `--cache-from type=registry,ref=ghcr.io/paperclipai/paperclip-runner-buildcache:<arch>` to the first build command. They do not log in. Their permissions stay `contents: read` and `packages: read`.
  > - A new `cache` job writes the cache with `--cache-to type=registry,...,mode=max` and `--output type=cacheonly`. It runs only for a `master` push in the canonical repository, for inputs that had no image. Its permissions are `contents: read` and `packages: write`. It has no `id-token` and no `attestations` permission.
  > - The `cache` job needs the `publish` job, and no job needs the `cache` job. A cache write cannot delay or fail a publication.
- feat(environments): forward the warm idle window to plugin lease acquisition (#21305)
  > - `PluginEnvironmentAcquireLeaseParams` has a new optional `runnerLifecyclePolicy`.
  > - Core sends it only for warm leases.
  > - `doc/plugins/PLUGIN_RUNNER_EXECUTION.md` documents the field.
- feat(server): read a retained provider pack from its Runner image (#21308)
  > - `server/src/services/native-runtime/retained-provider-pack-oci.ts`, for a pack whose `payload.runnerInputsDigest` is 64 hexadecimal characters:
  > - The same file, for every other pack: the old path, unchanged in what it checks. Its code moved into functions that both paths use (`request`, `pullToken`, `platformImage`, `receiveTransportLayer`).
  > - The downloaded layer file is removed as soon as it is unpacked. Before, the importer removed it after its checks, so `retained-provider-pack-source.ts` loses that one line.
- feat(ci): report the Runner image on master and build the app image's Runner path on pull requests (#21307)
  > - `.github/workflows/docker.yml`: new job `runner_image_report`.
  > - `.github/workflows/docker-app-check.yml` (new): on a pull request, builds the `build` and `runner-provider-pack` targets with `PAPERCLIP_BUILD_COMMIT` set, for `linux/amd64` only.
  > - `server/src/__tests__/docker-runner-workflows.test.ts` (new, 90 lines): only security properties. The report job has no credential and one read permission, nothing can read it, and its two steps have time limits. The check has no credential, cannot publish, and exports nothing. In both, every action is pinned by commit and no shell script holds an expression.
- feat(runner): record the OpenRouter host that served each OpenCode model call (#21298)
  > - New `createUpstreamServedTap` (`packages/paperclip-runner/src/drivers/opencode/upstream-served.ts`). It is a pass-through stream. It pushes each chunk downstream first, then it reads `provider` and `id` from the first SSE `data:` event (or from a JSON body at its end). It scans at most 64 KiB of a stream and 1 MiB of a JSON body. It skips encoded bodies and unknown content types. Any parse problem or recorder error stops the scan and leaves the response unchanged.
  > - `startOpenCodeProviderProxy` uses the tap only for 2xx responses. The OpenCode session keeps the most recent served call. Each `item.completed` usage payload and `session.usage()` now carry `upstreamProvider` and `providerRequestId`. The usage dedupe fingerprint does not include these fields, so the number of usage events does not change.
  > - The OpenCode app-server proxy adds the two fields to `thread/tokenUsage/updated`. Rust `usage.reported` now fills its existing `providerRequestId` field and adds an optional `upstreamProvider`. The TypeScript runnerd transport keeps both fields when it rehydrates the usage notification. `usage.schema.json` documents `upstreamProvider` (the protocol manifest and schema bundle are regenerated).
- feat(ci): add the Runner image resolver and a no-rebuild mode for the Runner vendor step (#21306)
  > - `scripts/runner-image-resolve.mjs` (new, 185 lines), with two commands that share a directory:
  > - `mode` is `published` only for a complete publication whose full index names these inputs and both platforms. Both images are pinned by index digest. The slim digest is the one the full index names in `io.paperclip.runner.slim-digest`; the slim tag is never read on its own.
  > - Every other answer is `inline`, with the last four values empty:
- feat(ci): publish both variants of the Runner image from master (#21230)
  > - `.github/workflows/runner-image.yml`: adds the trigger `push` to `master` and the job `publish`.
  > - Lockfile rule. A `master` push builds from the committed `pnpm-lock.yaml` and resolves nothing, so the digest is a function of the commit. When the committed lockfile is behind the manifests that the Runner build reads, the run writes a notice, publishes nothing, and stays green. The commit that merges the lockfile refresh publishes the image. Pull requests and manual runs resolve the lockfile first, always build, and never publish.
  > - Hand-off. Each leg joins its two OCI archives into one OCI layout that names the images `slim` and `full`. The layout stores the two shared layers one time. `record` then reads the layout back, each image by its name, and checks every blob against its digest. The leg's record binds the layout to both image digests, the architecture, the inputs digest, and the commit. Those digests are the digests of the archives that part 3 unpacks and checks, so the tree that is checked and the image that is pushed are the same bytes. The publish job accepts only records for its own inputs and commit, and only a layout that still has the recorded bytes.
- feat(docker): split the Runner image into stable layers and add a slim variant (#21229)
  > - `Dockerfile`: two final stages. `runner-artifacts-slim` is two `COPY --link` layers. `runner-artifacts` starts from it and adds two more. The build stage sorts its output into the four layers. Every path is where it was in part 2: consumers copy by path and see no difference.
  > - Stable bytes. The files of layers 1 and 3 get timestamp 0, and the large dependency files get it before their transport archives are written. A file under the pack's `dist` is the Runner's own output, so it and its archive are in layers 2 and 4.
  > - `scripts/create-provider-pack-archive.mjs`, two separate commits. **This script also writes the application image's archives.**
- feat(docker): build and check the Runner alone as the runner-artifacts target (#21204)
  > - `Dockerfile`: eight new stages that end in the target `runner-artifacts` (`FROM scratch`). They install with a filtered, frozen lockfile from a context that holds only the Runner's inputs. The base is a Node image on Debian bookworm, pinned by digest. No existing stage changed. `cloud` is still the default target.
  > - The tree holds: the stripped daemon, the Runner package's complete `dist` with the build stamp that its own build records, the provider pack with its transport archives, the Grok provisioning inputs, the transport contract source and a bundled transport module, the lockfile, and `runner-image.json`.
  > - `scripts/runner-image-identity.mjs`: computes the image identity. It hashes every file under the paths that the build copies from the build context (path, executable bit, content; symbolic links by target), the whole `Dockerfile`, and the two platforms. The build runs it on the files that the build context supplies after `.dockerignore`.
- feat(mcp): host the MCP Skills extension for connected apps (#21219)
  > - **Client capability.** Every stateless request declares `io.modelcontextprotocol/skills`. Legacy handshake sessions do not. The capabilities that `server/discover` advertises are remembered beside the protocol era.
  > - **Discovery.** Catalog refresh calls `skills/list` (paged) only when the server declared the extension. A listing inside its `ttlMs` is reused. A skills failure never fails the tool refresh. Nothing runs while a run starts.
  > - **Schema.** New `tool_mcp_skills` (company, connection, skill URI; frontmatter, sorted manifest, manifest digest, review state) and `tool_mcp_skill_files` (verified bytes keyed by connection, URI and digest). Both cascade with the connection.
- feat(ui): redesign Settings → Secrets around what people manage (#21225)
  > - **List.** Rewrote the Secrets tab in `Secrets.tsx`. Added `secret-list-parts.tsx` for the Provided by and Used by cells, the help popover and the managed groups. The Folders / Flat toggle is removed. Folders are always on and search covers all folders.
  > - **Managed grouping.** Added `secret-origin.ts`. It infers the origin of a secret from the name and key conventions of the server modules that create secrets (`tool_app.*`, `tool_oauth.*`, `slack_manager.*`, `chat.*`, `ai_*`, `routine-*`, `agent-observability.*`). It is used for presentation only.
  > - **Agent usage.** Added `agent-secret-access.ts`. It holds the grant and revoke logic that was inside the details panel, and builds one usage index from the agents list for all rows.
- feat(runner): let a provider pack record the Runner image it was built in (#21228)
  > - `packages/paperclip-runner/scripts/build-provider-pack.mjs`: reads `PAPERCLIP_RUNNER_INPUTS_DIGEST`. When it is set, the script checks its form and writes `payload.runnerInputsDigest` after `runnerSourceRevision`. When it is not set, the manifest is the same as before, byte for byte.
  > - `server/src/services/native-runtime/remote-provider-pack-verification.ts`: the manifest type has the optional field `runnerInputsDigest`. No check reads it.
  > - `server/src/services/native-runtime/remote-provider-pack-verification.test.ts`: one test. A sealed manifest with the field passes verification. A manifest whose field was changed after it was sealed fails, because the pack digest covers the field.
- feat(ci): send one in ten CODEOWNER PR runs and their outside checks to Honeycomb (#21192)
  > - `scripts/ci-profile/authorize.mjs`: add `exportMode` (label or one-in-ten sample) and `associatedOwnerPr` (the CODEOWNER checks without the label).
  > - `scripts/ci-profile/trace.mjs`: add `normalizeChecks` and spans for outside checks. Add the sample rate and the new root fields. Sampled runs do not warn about absent test artifacts.
  > - `scripts/ci-profile/export.mjs`: select the mode. Read check runs, commit statuses and workflow runs for the head commit. Wait a maximum of 15 minutes for outside apps that are not complete. Sampled runs read no artifacts.
- feat(mcp): speak stateless MCP 2026-07-28 to provider servers, keep older servers working (#21140)
  > - `mcp-http.ts`: `negotiateMcpProtocolVersion` probes with `server/discover`. A `DiscoverResult` or a modern error (`-32020`/`-32021`/`-32022`) means a modern server. Anything else means a legacy server, which gets `initialize`. A `401`/`403` keeps the challenge for OAuth discovery. The era is remembered per connection and endpoint.
  > - `mcpHttpRequestInit` builds every request. It always sends `Mcp-Method` and `Mcp-Name` (Base64 sentinel when unsafe). For modern versions it adds `_meta` (`protocolVersion`, `clientCapabilities` with `extensions["io.modelcontextprotocol/ui"]` and URL elicitation, `clientInfo`) and `Mcp-Param-*` headers from `x-mcp-header`.
  > - Legacy `initialize` now requests 2025-11-25 and advertises the MCP Apps extension.
- feat(ui): browser page-load tracing to Honeycomb for hosted instances (#21156)
  > - `server/src/browser-tracing-config.ts`: resolves the browser tracing config. It returns `null` unless `isCloudManagedInstance()` is true and `PAPERCLIP_BROWSER_TRACES_ENDPOINT` is set. It accepts only a Honeycomb ingest key (`hcaik_` prefix), requires `https` (except loopback), and fails closed with one value-free warning on any invalid setting. Optional: `PAPERCLIP_BROWSER_TRACES_INGEST_KEY`, `_SAMPLE_RATE` (default 1), `_SERVICE_NAME` (default `paperclip-web`), `_ENVIRONMENT` (default `SENTRY_ENVIRONMENT`).
  > - `GET /api/auth/get-session` adds `browserTracing` (config or `null`), the same delivery path as the front-end Sentry DSN. The shared `authSessionSchema` has the new optional field `browserTracingConfigSchema`.
  > - `ui/src/lib/browser-tracing/page-load-recorder.ts` and `controller.ts` (main bundle, no OpenTelemetry imports): one `page.load` measurement per hard load (from navigation start) and per client navigation (from the history change). A measurement ends at the first painted frame where no full-page loading state is mounted and either every page-declared content signal is ready or no query without data is fetching. Redirects continue the measurement. A new navigation interrupts it. 30 s times out. The sample decision is made once per page session before any download.
- feat(connections): render MCP Apps views in the task chat, starting with Circleback (#21119)
  > - **Shared:** `packages/shared/src/mcp-apps.ts` adds MCP Apps types, `_meta.ui` and resource parsing (current and legacy keys), and a CSP builder that keeps only well-formed `https` origins.
  > - **DB:** migration `0471` (`online_compatible`) adds `tool_catalog_entries.ui_meta` and a new `tool_mcp_app_views` table.
  > - **Server, catalog:** discovery keeps each tool's `_meta.ui`. It is not part of the version hash, so a view never quarantines a reviewed tool.
- feat(docker): build the image in the merge queue and promote it on master (#21096)
  > - **`docker-merge-group.yml` (new, `on: merge_group` only).** It runs only for this repository's master queue. Workflow permissions are `{}` and the job has `contents: read` only. There are no secrets, no registry login, no `cache-to`, no `push: true`, no environments, and checkout uses `persist-credentials: false`. It runs on GitHub-hosted runners, not the paid AWS fleets. Steps: build `target: production` with the same build args as master (`PAPERCLIP_BUILD_COMMIT` is the merge-group SHA, which becomes the master SHA). Read the public registry cache. Write `type=oci` to a tar. `docker load` it and run `scripts/assert-orphan-reaping.sh`. Only then write `record.json` and upload `merge-group-image-<arch>` (3-day retention).
  > - **`scripts/merge-group-image.mjs` (new).**
  > - **`docker.yml` `build-and-push`.**
- feat(inbox): rebuild compact cards and share task review controls (#20961)
  > - Add personal, unassigned, company, all-assignee, and archived inbox views. Add claim, transfer, personal close actions, receipts, and history controls.
  > - Use compact decision cards with a single count line, one-pixel borders, and equal space above, below, and to the right of each primary action.
  > - Open artifacts and synchronized confirmation/approval controls in a shared side panel. Share their revision drafts and action state with the task thread. Other requests keep one editable form on the inbox card while reviewing an artifact.
- feat(inbox): route requests to people and record personal outcomes (#20959)
  > - Add nullable company and project decision owners and a company budget owner.
  > - Add audience views and personal badge counts to the canonical attention projections.
  > - Add claim, transfer, withdrawal, personal archive, snooze, and restore operations with permission checks and activity records. Lock addressed transfers to the current addressee or routing administrators, and validate the recipient against canonical resolution policies.
- feat(db): classify migrations for application under the running release (#20913)
  > - `packages/db/src/migration-compatibility.ts`: `classifyMigrationSql(sql)` returns `{ compatibility, classified, reasons, override, overrideError }`. It parses statement by statement (breakpoints, `;`, comments, string and dollar quoting, guarded `DO` blocks). A statement that it does not recognize is `unknown`. A migration takes the worst class of its statements.
  > - Lock and duration hazards have their own reason codes (`lock_hazard_*`) and classify as `unknown`, not as compatible with a warning.
  > - Override: `-- paperclip:migration-compat online_compatible: <justification>` (or `requires_staging`) in the leading comment block of the migration file. The justification is mandatory (10 characters or more). A malformed, repeated, or misplaced directive is an error and never improves the verdict.
- feat(runner): upgrade ACPX to 0.22.0 and move patched behavior into Paperclip (#20770)

  > - Delivery receipts: Runner confirms a permission reply when `onAcpMessage` sees the exact outbound reply. Question and extension replies count as delivered when Runner hands them back to ACPX. New `interaction-delivery.ts`.

- feat(runner): export authorized read responses as artifacts (#20606)

- feat(ui): ask self-hosted users to star Paperclip on GitHub after 5 done tasks (#20453)

It reads the health endpoint and shows only on self-hosted instances (never on Cloud), never during onboarding, and only after 5 done tasks in the selected company. It waits for any other open dialog to close. It stores a "seen" flag in `localStorage` when it opens, so a reload does not show it again.

The re-reads of the issue after a checkout use the existing `issue_context` name.

- feat(cli): manage agent grants and keep remote login on the public origin (#20323)

## Fixes

- fix(issues): keep a held task blocked when its agent tries to resume it (#22245)
  > - `server/src/routes/issues.ts`: new `assertAgentMayLeaveBlocked`. It gets the status that the request will save. When an agent moves a `blocked` task to a status other than `blocked`, `done` or `cancelled`, it reads `getExecutionBlocker`. If a recovery hold applies, it returns 409 with `recoveryActionId`, `cause` and `nextAction`. The task does not change.
  > - `PATCH /issues/:id` calls the check one time, after the execution policy transition and before any write. It uses the final status. This covers a status change, `reopen`, `resume`, and a `done` request that a review or approval stage changes to `in_review`.
  > - `POST /issues/:id/comments` calls the check when the comment moves the task to `todo` (`reopen` or `resume`). The native `comment_on_task` tool uses the comments route. Agent comments without `reopen` or `resume` do not move a `blocked` task.
- fix(native-runtime): name the closest assigned tools when a tool name is mistyped (#22327)
  > - `server/src/services/native-runtime/assigned-mcp-tools.ts`:
  > - `editDistance` is a local helper in the same module. It has the same algorithm as the private helper in `packages/shared/src/config-schema.ts`. That shared file is pinned as a reviewed SDK reference source, so an export from it would require a new pin review. A second commit tried this, and the runner support suite failed on the stale pin, so the third commit reverted it.
  > - `server/src/services/native-runtime/assigned-mcp-tools.test.ts`: add a regression test.
- fix(cloud): migrate run evidence during online preparation (#22337)
  > - Add log and provider trace pages to online preparation. Keep independent cursors and the existing request budget.
  > - Share the evidence page owner with background migration. A competing in-process request reports `owner_busy`.
  > - Reuse the existing native archive guard for settled history whose canonical coverage cannot be proven.
- fix(tools): retry a tool audit write that loses a deadlock instead of failing the call (#22329)
  > - `server/src/services/tool-gateway.ts`: `writeToolCallEvent` runs its `tool_call_events` insert through `retryTransientLockContention`. The helper retries only lock timeout, deadlock and serialization failure (`55P03`, `40P01`, `40001`): 5 attempts, with 100 ms × attempt backoff. Other errors fail as before.
  > - `server/src/__tests__/tool-gateway-connector-telemetry.test.ts`: new test with a real two-transaction deadlock between an issue-then-run locker and the audit insert of a real `executeTool` call. The fixture now also returns the issue.
- fix(plugins): refresh unchanged-version bundle descriptions (#22295)
  > - Refresh plugin, environment-driver, and direct config-property descriptions for installed Core bundles at the same version.
  > - Compare all other fields before refreshing. Keep pending approval and uninstall behavior. Keep runtime default objects intact, even when they contain a field named `description`.
  > - Cover description updates and refusal of permission, entrypoint, schema, default-value, and approval-state changes.
- fix(telemetry): report a Paperclip runner agent's underlying harness as adapter_type (#22324)
  > - `server/src/services/agent-task-run-telemetry.ts`: new `telemetryAdapterType(agent)` helper. It returns `agentHarnessType(agent.adapterType, adapterConfig)`. `agent.task_run` sends this value as `adapterType`.
  > - `server/src/routes/issues.ts`: `agent.task_completed` calls the same helper. Thus, the two events cannot report different adapter types for the same agent.
  > - A legacy agent (for example `claude_local`) sends the same value as before. `agentHarnessType()` returns a legacy adapter type without a change.
- fix(github): end a superseded automatic review with a clear outcome (#22218)
  > - `server/src/services/chat-github-reviews.ts`: the stale-head check now builds the error in a new `supersededAutomaticReview` helper. The helper looks for a newer automatic delivery for the current head of the same pull request. It matches the repository id and pull number that the event saves, because a new delivery has no conversation id until it is processed. It counts only deliveries that are newer than the run's own event, in the states `received`, `processing`, `processed`, or `retry`. The query stays scoped to the company and endpoint. The 409 error message names both commits, says if a newer review is queued, and tells the agent to stop. The error details carry `reasonCode: "github_review_superseded"`, `retryable: false`, `requestedCommit`, `currentCommit`, and `newerReviewQueued`.
  > - The first sentence of the message does not change, so existing logs and searches still match.
  > - The run still cannot read or review the newer head. Authorization does not change.
- fix(connections): split GitHub Copilot into its own connector (#22238)
  > - Add a `github-copilot` app definition with the existing `copilot-token` method. Remove that method from `github`. Restore the GitHub description and the `developer` category.
  > - Add the GitHub Octicons Copilot mark (MIT license, from `primer/octicons`) as light and dark brand assets.
  > - Map the `github` AI provider to the `github-copilot` catalog entry in `aiConnectionCatalogSlug`. New Copilot accounts save under a "GitHub Copilot" application.
- fix(dispatch): schedule a clean loop-limit continuation with its person's work (#22312)
  > - `queued-run-dispatch-order.ts`: add a `cleanContinuation` dispatch fact. A clean continuation is not system recovery, so it takes the tier of its task (person or work) and the normal readiness, priority and age order. The module stays domain-agnostic: the wake reason is defined once (`ISSUE_CONTINUATION_NEEDED_WAKE_REASON`, used in `RECOVERY_WAKE_REASONS`).
  > - `queued-run-dispatch-order.ts`: one core predicate (`countsAsRecoveryStart`) drives `isSystemRecoveryRun`, the in-pass recovery counter, the place-in-line counter and the start-history counter. Every start with a recovery wake reason that a person did not ask for still uses up the recovery share. A clean continuation therefore does not add recovery-reason starts.
  > - New `queued-run-provenance.ts`: the clean-continuation causes, the source-run check and `QUEUED_RUN_ORDER_CONTEXT_KEYS`.
- fix(restart-chaos): grade double executors on local runner identities only (#22307)
  > - `tests/restart-chaos/multi-server.ts`: reads `payload.targetKind` for runner identity events and skips `remote` identities in `MS_double_executor`.
  > - `tests/restart-chaos/multi-server.test.ts`: adds a red/green test with three sandbox relaunches of one run.
  > - `tests/restart-chaos/SCENARIOS.md`: states that sandbox identities are not graded, and why.
- fix(tasks): keep a due automatic retry working while it is dispatched (#22306)
  > - `server/src/services/task-interruption-presentation.ts`: a `scheduled_retry` run whose retry time has passed shows `working` (no Retry) for `NATIVE_RESUME_DISPATCH_GRACE_MS` (2 minutes), the same rule that a due native resume already uses. After the grace, a retry that never started still falls through to one Retry.
  > - `server/src/services/task-interruption-presentation.test.ts`: adds a regression test for a just-due `transient_failure` and `native_environment_blocker` retry (0 s to 119 s overdue). One existing assertion now uses a retry that is 10 minutes overdue, so it still proves that a stuck retry offers Retry.
- fix(cloud): resolve or name the remaining online move blockers (#22294)
  > - Skill installation conversion returns a `code` for each blocked installation (for example `source_files_unavailable`, `saved_version_incomplete`, `catalog_origin_unavailable`, `legacy_source_unsafe`, `source_changed`). The pass reports `skills:<code>`. An unknown cause stays `skills:blocked`.
  > - A spent budget during conversion now stops the page. Before, it was reported as a blocked skill and the page moved past it.
  > - A catalog skill whose files are gone converts from the bundled catalog package only when the catalog content hash equals the recorded origin hash and the last audit recorded the installed bytes as equal to that origin with no local changes.
- fix(runner): wait for run.attach before restoring a Codex session on a fresh Runner (#22267)
  > - Before it creates fresh durable state, the durable runner tells the executor (`observe_fresh_durable_state`).
  > - The native executor then marks a saved Codex session as waiting for `run.attach`. The Codex executor reads the mark when it loads the session.
  > - The Codex executor does not restore the provider until attachment, and refuses commands that start work. Snapshot, close, drain, suspend, shutdown, interrupt, cancel and stop still pass.
- fix(cloud): let an online move proceed when Cloud supplies the database listener (#22263)
  > - `services/ephemeral-enrollment.ts`: when the only configuration failure is the listener, and the listener is unset, the readiness check passes. The listener check is the last check in `assertEphemeralServerConfig`, so all earlier settings were already valid.
  > - `services/ephemeral-enrollment.test.ts`: tests for an unset listener (eligible, and preparation succeeds) and a mismatched listener (still blocked).
- fix(cloud): move idle local workspaces in the online durable-data pass (#22270)
  > - Add two phases to the online durable-data pass, after `runtime_skill_assets` and before the native phases:
  > - A workspace that the owner refuses is deferred with each owner reason as `workspaces:<reason>`, for example `workspaces:active_execution`, `workspaces:task_binding_required`, `workspaces:workspace_provider_unsupported`, `workspaces:workspace_source_busy`, or `workspaces:execution_rows_busy`. An unknown error becomes `workspaces:owner_failed` and its message is not echoed. The page continues with the next workspace.
  > - Both phases use a cursor and a limit that the pass can halve after a budget timeout, like the other phases.
- fix(runner): tell ACPX agents which field a refused semantic call failed (#22037)
  > - New `acpx_tool_failure_feedback` module in `runner-core`. It reads a failed tool result and builds a bounded message from:
  > - It reads the result as an object, an issue list, or a `{success:false, contentItems:[{inputText}]}` envelope whose text is JSON or starts with `code:`.
