- Let plugin workers serve connector tools (plugin transport) (#22019)
  > - `packages/db`: migration `0491` widens `tool_connections_transport_check` with `plugin` (`NOT VALID`, annotated `online_compatible`), and adds `plugin_connector_legacy_access`, seeded with one row per installed legacy-tools plugin and company.
  > - `packages/shared`: method schema gains `settingsSchema` and `tools` (`{ kind: "static", list: [{ name, displayName?, description?, risk, parametersSchema }] } | { kind: "dynamic" }`), only for `transport: "plugin"`; tool names must match `[a-z0-9_]`; plugin-served methods use `none` or `api_key`. The connect API accepts `settings`.
  > - `packages/plugins/sdk`: `ToolRunContext.connection` (`id`, `appSlug`, `methodKey`, `grantId`, `settings`, `credential?`); `ctx.connectors.register(appSlug, toolName, fn)` and `ctx.connectors.listTools(appSlug, fn)`; new `listConnectorTools` RPC; `executeTool` routes connector calls by `appSlug`. A worker refuses to start when a declared static tool (or dynamic lister) has no handler. The test harness gains `executeConnectorTool` and `listConnectorTools`.
- Let plugins add connector types to the Connectors catalog (#22004)
  > - `packages/shared`: new capability `connectors.provide`; manifest `apps` validated with `appDefinitionSchema`. The validator rejects apps without the capability, duplicate slugs, host-derived fields (`plugin`, `aiConnectionRouter`, `experimentalFeatureKey`, `availability`), and methods that need provider code in the host (REST adapters, chat channels, AI runtimes).
  > - `packages/shared/src/plugin-apps.ts`: reversible namespaced slugs (`plugin-<hex plugin key>-<slug>`), the catalog entry builder, and `reviewedToolRisk`.
  > - `packages/shared/src/app-definitions.ts`: catalog lookups (`getConnectableAppDefinition`, `getAppStoreDefinition`, ...) also resolve the current plugin apps. The set is replaced on each read of plugin state, so a built-in app is never shadowed.
- docs(connections): record Nov 17 Google Workspace progress in memory, checklist and plan (#22007)
  > - Working memory: three new decisions (no new npm packages, the scope handling contract, agents merge their PRs on the coordinator's approval at green checks and Greptile 5/5).
  > - Working memory: new "What has shipped" table (#21996, #21998, #21999, #22001 and #22000, with merged commits).
  > - Working memory: the single copy of the 14-scope table. "Known mismatches" now lists only what is still true after #22001.
- docs(plans): Cloud sends analytics directly to PostHog (#22003)
  > - Add `doc/plans/2026-11-17-cloud-direct-posthog.md`.
- Add a Cloud run liveness check for inspection authority (#22005)
  > - `server/src/routes/customer-success.ts`: new `/run-liveness` route and `verifyRunLivenessQuestion`. The shared JWS checks (signature, kid, typ, issuer, audience, subject, time window, jti) move into one helper that `verifyInspectionPermit` also uses, with the same behavior. Each token type has its own `typ` and `aud`, so a permit is never accepted as a liveness question and the reverse.
  > - `server/src/services/customer-success-authority.ts`: `customerSuccessRunLiveness` uses the run rules of `customerSuccessRunAuthority` (agent in the company with status idle, running or active; run of that agent is `running`, not finished, not write-revoked). It does not need a managed identity key. It runs in a read-only transaction and creates no actor, session, activity or key.
- docs(connections): Google Workspace approval checklist, code-fix plan and working memory (#21996)
  > - Added `doc/plans/2026-11-17-google-workspace-production-checklist.md`: the "done means" criteria, the 14-scope contract, and checklists for decisions, baseline, product and code, policy, security assessment, reviewer environment, live acceptance, demo video, submission, release and ongoing work.
  > - Added `doc/plans/2026-11-17-google-workspace-code-fixes.md`: ten ordered code fixes (disclosure in the picker, scope lock to 14, `gmail.modify` removal from the managed client, injection screening check, revoke on last disconnect, retention audit, scan findings, gate removal, REST-backed tool set, scope cleanup). Each item is marked confirmed or to check.
  > - Added `doc/memory/google-connector-memory.md`: a temporary file with decisions, open questions, environments, Google app layout, scopes, review history and privacy-policy gaps. It says to delete it once the connector is released.
- test(restart-chaos): keep a run waiting on an active model request as its U1 owner (#21986)
  > - `tests/restart-chaos/universal.ts`: `liveOwner` uses `PROVIDER_TURN_LIMIT_MS` (10 minutes) as the quiet limit for a `running` run with `providerTurnActive`. A preparing run keeps `PREPARATION_LIMIT_MS`. Other running runs keep `silentRunMs` (3 minutes).
  > - `tests/restart-chaos/universal.test.ts`: a regression test in the t001 shape. A run that is quiet for 7.5 minutes during an active provider turn and then succeeds passes U1 with `ownerlessMs` 0. A run that stays quiet for 15 minutes still fails U1.
  > - `tests/restart-chaos/README.md`: the R5 note says that U1 uses the same bounds, with the t001 evidence.
- test(runner): copy a standalone sleep binary where /bin/sleep is a multicall binary (#21726)
  > - `packages/paperclip-runner/scripts/stage-runner-binary.test.mjs`: add `standaloneSleep`. On Linux, when `/bin/sleep` and `/bin/echo` have the same bytes, the two tests copy `/usr/bin/gnusleep` as the stand-in for the daemon. On each other system the tests use `/bin/sleep` as before.
- perf(runner): reuse the sandbox's identical runnerd instead of uploading it on every cold boot (#21949)
  > - `mayUsePreinstalledRunnerArtifact` accepts a preinstalled `paperclip-runnerd` when an artifact is configured and the controller has that artifact on disk.
  > - In that case the existing SHA-256 check (`runner_remote_preinstalled_artifact_mismatch`) is mandatory. A different, missing, or unreadable image copy still falls back to the upload.
  > - When an artifact is configured, a failed discovery probe counts as "no preinstalled runnerd", so the controller uploads the configured file as before.
- test(ci): stop the live-run census hanging server shards, and name any stalled test file (#21941)
  > - `issue-recovery-live-run-agent-census.test.ts`
  > - New `scripts/ci/stalled-file-reporter.mjs`, enabled in GitHub Actions from the root `vitest.config.ts`: when a running file starts no test or hook and reports no result for 8 minutes (`PAPERCLIP_VITEST_FILE_STALL_MS` overrides), it prints a `::error file=...` annotation naming the file and the step it was in, cancels the run and exits 1. The slowest healthy server file runs about 5 minutes in total, so 8 minutes without any progress means a stuck file. Tests: `scripts/ci/stalled-file-reporter.test.mjs` (added to `test:release-registry`).
- perf(tasks): open recent tasks without waiting on a slow ten-task preload (#21939)
  > - `activityService.runsForIssue`: select the task's runs by `id IN (runs with issue_id = task UNION run ids from the task's activity)`. Each half uses its own index. The results are the same.
  > - `startIssueChatBootstrap`: when the only request for a task is a background batch that is still loading, send a request for that task alone. All refs of the task point to the new request. The later batch answer only fills keys that are still empty or older.
  > - `loadIssueChatBootstraps` (background): a task whose answer did not arrive is not held back for five minutes, so the next preload can retry it.
- perf(ci): stop printing Postgres TRUNCATE notices in server test shards (#21903)
  > - `packages/db/src/test-postgres-admin.ts`: add `QUIET_TEST_DATABASE_SQL`. Run it on each new database on the admin-URL path.
  > - `packages/db/src/test-embedded-postgres.ts`: run the same statement on the `paperclip` database at each embedded start. It runs on each start, not only when the template is built. Thus a template that was cached before this change also gives a quiet database, and the template key stays the same.
  > - `packages/db/src/test-postgres-admin.test.ts`: new test for the admin-URL path.
- docs: allow the chat webhook ingress hostname in private mode (#18301)
  > - `docs/deploy/environment-variables.md`: the `PAPERCLIP_CHAT_WEBHOOK_PUBLIC_URL` row now tells private-mode operators to add the ingress hostname to `PAPERCLIP_ALLOWED_HOSTNAMES`. It also says that the guard reads `X-Forwarded-Host` before `Host`.
- chore(lockfile): refresh pnpm lockfiles (#21936)
  > - Refresh the dependency lock from the manifests on master.
  > - Refresh the sandbox provider lockfiles from their manifests on master.
  > - Include the matching offline report correction and hashes.
- test(server): give the blocker history case room on a slow shard (#18720)
  > - Raise the timeout of the `it.each(["running", "run", "confirmation", "completed"])` case to 60 seconds. Nothing else changes: same assertions, same 2100-row fixture.
- test(recovery): stop the idle-backstop suite starting real runs and bound every wait (#21924)
  > - `helpers/recovery-population.ts`: optional `wakeOnDemand` (default `true`, so the other suites are unchanged).
  > - `issue-recovery-idle-backstop.test.ts`:
  > - `issue-recovery.test-fixture.ts`: records the queue of each pending pass (`pendingPassQueues()`), so a timed-out wait names the queues it was still waiting for.
- perf(release): number canaries by master position and confirm them outside the release lock (#21917)
  > - `scripts/release-lib.sh`: `next_canary_version_from_source`. A publishing canary refuses a shallow checkout; a dry run (the PR Canary Dry Run job) falls back to the registry number.
  > - `scripts/release.sh`: canary uses the new number; new canary-only `--defer-registry-confirmation` flag skips the registry wait, the dist-tag check and the local tag, and writes the version to `PAPERCLIP_RELEASE_VERSION_FILE`.
  > - `scripts/confirm-canary-publication.sh` (new): waits for registry visibility, checks provenance, checks dist-tags with `--allow-newer-canary`, installs `paperclipai@<version>`, then creates and pushes `canary/v<version>`. A tag already on the same commit is accepted, so reruns are safe.
- perf(recovery): keep a repeatedly deferring task's saved row; stop agent settlement from re-checking its backlog (#21905)
  > - `recovery/work.ts`: a deferring check skips the upsert while this server knows a saved row for the task whose deadline is less than `DEFERRAL_REFRESH_MS` (5 minutes) past. It refreshes the row after that. The refresh comes before the 10-minute stale prune margin, so a still-deferring task's row never looks stale. A boot re-checks every task with a row whatever its deadline, so the exact deadline only matters to the conditional deletes and the stale prune, which still match the value this server read or wrote. A cleared row, a failed write or a reload still drop the server's memory of the row, so the next deferral writes it again.
  > - `execution-control-reconciliation.ts` and `heartbeat/agent-status.ts`: settling an agent after its runs end sets `lastHeartbeatAt` with `updatedAt`, as `finalizeAgentStatus` already does, through a new `settledHeartbeat(now)`. It records the settlement time only when the agent already has a heartbeat; an agent with none keeps none, so the first real heartbeat stays first (the existing "repairs a shutdown flag without inventing a heartbeat" test still passes).
  > - `recovery/changed-tasks.ts`: the comment now states the contract: every write that settles an agent's status after its runs end sets `last_heartbeat_at`.
- chore(lockfile): refresh pnpm lockfiles (#21915)
  > - Refresh the dependency lock from the manifests on master.
  > - Refresh the sandbox provider lockfiles from their manifests on master.
  > - Include the matching offline report correction and hashes.
- perf(recovery): load changed rows, not every task, for company hints, boots and reconnects (#21872)
  > - `recovery/work.ts`: the full-pass bookkeeping now decides a row filter per load.
  > - New table `issue_recovery_deferrals` (queue, issue, company, due_at), in the same migration. A row exists while a task's check has deferred itself (grace period, back-off, live owner lease). A boot or reconnect reload also loads these tasks and checks them again, which rebuilds each deadline. Deferrals cannot be rebuilt from changed rows: several defer with no limit while none of the task's rows change (see the table below). The store is built so that any race or lost write costs at most one extra check, never a missed one:
  > - New table `issue_recovery_full_passes` (company, queue, started_at), migration `0488`. A full pass writes it only after every task it loaded has been checked (a check that fails keeps it unsaved), so a server replaced mid-pass leaves no record and its replacement runs the full pass again; a server with no memory of its own reads it. This keeps the hourly full pass on one schedule for replaced and overlapping servers. Reads and writes are one statement each and only happen when a full pass is due or done. A failed read means a full pass; a failed write means one extra full pass later.
- test(recovery): bound every wait in the agent-row census and stop it starting real runs (#21867)
  > - Fixture agents take no on-demand wakes (`wakeOnDemand: false`), so a recovery check cannot dispatch a run. The census counts queue checks, so this does not change what the tests measure.
  > - The idle-agent guard writes the configuration of an agent that never ran, instead of ending the second agent's live run.
  > - The held agent's waiting task is a settled review, not a `todo` task.
- test(restart-chaos): count distinct warm close failures and let R5 wait out environment acquire (#21852)
  > - `warm-shutdown.ts`: `shutdownCloseFailures` and `idleCloseFailures` are now lists of distinct `{ runId, reason, lines, firstLine }` entries, keyed by run and failure reason. The reason drops the JSON detail after the error text (for example timings), so retries of the same failure merge. A line with no parsable run keeps its own message as the reason. Each distinct shutdown failure still adds a problem, so the verdict is unchanged in meaning: any distinct failure fails the wave.
  > - `warm-shutdown.ts`: new `watchFirstIdleCloseFailure` splits server output into lines per stream and reports the first idle-close failure once. New optional `sandboxCaptures` input and output, listed in the markdown section.
  > - `supervisor.ts`: new `onServerOutput(chunk, source)` hook, called for the primary and the second server's stdout and stderr. A hook error is recorded in the timeline and never breaks logging.
- test(recovery): record a live process's OS-observed start time in owner-proof tests (#21847)
  > - `fresh-continuation-fallback.test.ts`: `liveIdentity()` now records `observedProcessStartedAt(process.pid)`, the OS-observed start time that a run records. It throws if the host cannot report the time, so the test cannot pass by accident.
  > - `completed-turn-cleanup-hold-settle.test.ts`: the live controller stamp now uses `currentProcessStartedAtMs()`, the value that this server stamps on its own claims. It had the same `Date.now() - process.uptime()` pattern.
  > - No product code changed. No assertion changed or became weaker. The ten-minute upper bound assertion stays.
- perf(recovery): stop counting a run start as a change to every task of its agent (#21831)
  > - `server/src/services/recovery/changed-tasks.ts`: the assignee-agent clause of `changedTaskCondition` now requires `ag.status <> 'running'`. A comment explains why run lifecycle writes are left out.
  > - `server/src/__tests__/issue-recovery-live-run-agent-census.test.ts` (new): a staging-shaped census on the embedded test database with the real recovery workers and a fake clock. It seeds 800 settled in_review tasks with a succeeded latest run (3 workers, about 266 tasks each), 30 in_progress and 30 todo tasks without live runs, and one live native run. The run's agent gets the execution-start write, and the run publishes a run hint every 10 seconds for 30 simulated minutes. It also has three correctness guards: a task of the live agent whose own row changes without a hint is checked within the backstop bound; a resumed agent's task is checked within the backstop bound when the resume sends no hint; and the hourly full pass still checks every settled task while the run stays live.
  > - `server/src/__tests__/issue-recovery-deadlines.test.ts`: a fast test that a run-start write is not a change and a pause during the run is a change.
- perf(runner): check a listening Runner's backlog every 10 ms, not every reconnect delay (#21821)
  > - `durable/transport.rs`: add `RunnerTransportEndpoint::retry_delay()`. A listener returns `min(configured, 10 ms)`. A dial endpoint returns the configured delay without change.
  > - `durable/runner.rs`: the four places that waited `config.reconnect_delay` (no pending connection, failure backoff base, two disconnect paths) now use `endpoint.retry_delay(config.reconnect_delay)`.
  > - `DURABLE_TRANSPORT.md`: document the listen-mode connection check interval.
- perf(recovery): stop re-checking settled workspace flags and snapshot ledger rows every pass (#21795)
  > - `remote_workspace_unsaved_files` lists a dirty workspace only when its writer run can still be a preparation-only writer: stage `preparing`, no `runtime_mode_resolved_at`, no process, session or runner id, no finalization row and no work-started event (or no writer at all). The SQL lives with the settle step in `remote-workspace-unsaved-files-settlement.ts`.
  > - The unsaved-files settle reads the writer run without a lock. It takes `for update` only after every check passes, just before it writes, and checks the locked row again.
  > - `remote_snapshot_ledger` lists only `pending` rows with no object key, whose operation is a workspace save, and whose workspace epoch has moved past the row's writer. Uploaded rows belong to snapshot retention, as before.
- perf(native-runtime): reuse a same-content image provider pack across builds (#21784)
  > - `remote-provider-pack-verification.ts`: new `acceptProvenance` option. The remote script accepts a sealed pack with the same content and prints its manifest. The server checks that manifest (`sameContentManifest`) and returns it. A forged or different report fails with `installed_manifest_invalid`.
  > - `native-session-executor.ts` (`prepareRemoteProviderPackOnTarget`): staged and preinstalled verification use the option when `acceptProvenanceDifferences` is set. The function returns the selected manifest. The run caller sets the option only through `providerPackProvenanceReuseAllowed` (not retained, no explicit custom pack, selectable authority), and selects a different manifest through the pack authority.
  > - `provider-pack-layers.ts`: `verifyArchive` skips the hash for an archive it already verified with the same file identity.
- perf(recovery): name the run or task in run writers' recovery hints instead of the company (#21765)
  > - `server/src/services/issue-recovery-notifications.ts`:
  > - `server/src/services/recovery/work.ts`: a load for an empty graph returns no rows. The `assigned-work-invariant` queue emits the same `check` debug event as `stranded-assignments` (with `PAPERCLIP_DEBUG_WORK_SIGNALS=assigned-work-invariant`), with the load reason.
  > - `server/src/services/recovery/changed-tasks.ts`: the assignee-agent part of the changed-tasks condition skips an agent whose last write was its run heartbeat (`last_heartbeat_at = updated_at`). A resume, pause or configuration write (no heartbeat stamp) still counts.
- test(server): accept dash's exit code for a sandbox-denied shell write (#21792)
  > - The write step now checks that the outside file still contains `PRIVATE` first, then that the command was not stopped and exited with a non-zero code. It no longer requires exit code 1 from the shell.
  > - The read step and the success step are unchanged.
- perf(native-runtime): name why a preinstalled provider pack was not reused (#21781)
  > - `remote-provider-pack-verification.ts`: the compatibility check names the failed condition (`failureCheck`). The remote verification script prints a fixed `paperclip-pack-check=<name>` line, and for a manifest mismatch it names the differing manifest fields. Missing files now give a named check instead of a raw stack trace.
  > - `native-run-trace.ts`: a failed `measure` records the error's `failureCheck` as the safe `check` attribute.
  > - `remote-provider-pack.ts`: `provider_pack.select` records the last preinstalled rejection (or `no_preinstalled_pack`) when it uploads.
- test(runner): remove coreutils and pid-file timing assumptions from static checks (#21787)
  > - `stage-runner-binary.test.mjs`: the test helper passes `argv0` to `spawn`. The copied `sleep` runs with `argv0: "sleep"`, so a multicall binary still acts as `sleep`. The fresh-build fixture is now a copy of `/bin/sh` and runs `-c "exit 0"`, so its bytes always differ from the old `sleep` artifact.
  > - `materialize-kimi-distribution.test.mjs`: the child writes `pid.tmp` and renames it to `pid`, so a read never sees a partial file. The test waits until the pid is valid or the command settles. The 3 s command timeout and 5 s test timeout are unchanged.
  > - `provision-kimi-package.test.mjs`: the wait for the `download-ready` marker continues until the child exits, instead of a fixed 1 s budget. The existing 5 s kill timer still bounds it.
- refactor: drop span and run-log event catalogs from the observability docs (#21779)
  > - `doc/observability.md` is now 619 lines. It keeps: how to enable tracing, `service.version` resolution, scope, how to enable Sentry, DSN delivery, environment attribution, privacy settings, server request data, the core of "Run failure context", browser data, fail-open behavior, the default capture set, and operator responsibilities. Kept text is unchanged except the intro and two sentences noted below.
  > - Deleted from `doc/observability.md`: budget, Slack receipt, task detail, and browser tracing notes; controller preparation phases; native Runner spans; sandbox startup spans; sandbox duplex transport; shared skill preparation; invocation attribution; and about 500 lines of per-failure detail in "Run failure context".
  > - Replaced that per-failure detail with one paragraph. It names the failures that `unexpected` mode keeps out of Sentry, and says the remaining diagnostic contexts are defined in `server/src/sentry.ts`. "Default capture set" now says the same.
- perf(db): keep dedicated connections for the next burst (#21769)
  > - `withDedicatedDbConnection` keeps a released client idle when fewer than `DEDICATED_CONNECTION_IDLE_MAX` (4) are idle, as before, or when idle plus in-use clients stay within the dedicated limit (`DATABASE_DEDICATED_CONNECTION_MAX`, default 24). A client whose action failed, an ended client and clients after shutdown are still closed.
  > - Each retained client keeps the main pool's `idle_timeout` (default 60 s), so its session closes after that idle time, as the main pool's do. The next use reconnects.
  > - The worst case stays at `2 x DATABASE_DEDICATED_CONNECTION_MAX + 4`: a new client opens only when no client is idle, and a client is kept above 4 only while idle plus in-use is within the limit. `databaseConnectionBudget()` and every pool maximum are unchanged.
- perf(server): cache the instance settings row per process (#21768)
  > - New `server/src/services/instance-settings-cache.ts`: a read-through cache keyed by the database client. Concurrent misses share one read. A cached row is at most `INSTANCE_SETTINGS_CACHE_TTL_MS` (2000 ms) old, measured from the start of the read. Every caller gets its own copy. Reads through a transaction handle skip the cache.
  > - `instanceSettingsService` `get`, `getGeneral` and `getExperimental` use the cache. Write paths (`update`, `updateGeneral`, `updateExperimental`, and the feedback vote save) still read the row fresh.
  > - Every settings write publishes an `instance-settings-changed` work signal through the existing work-signal channel (`@paperclipai/db/work-signals`). Inside a transaction it is the first signal of the write, so its `pg_notify` payload goes out before the other wakes of the same commit (for example `status-card-evaluation`). This process drops the cached row when the write commits. During a deploy overlap, when work notifications cross processes, the other process drops its cached row on receipt, before a worker woken by the same write reads the settings. A reconnect of the notification listener also drops the row.
- test(scaling): keep ramping through recovered runs, read spend from cost records, and wait for termination before deleting agents (#21776)
  > - `criteria.ts`: replace `failed_runs_zero` with `failed_runs_unrecovered_zero` (immediate) and `failed_run_rate` (at the step end). Step metrics add `runsEnded`, `runsFailedRecovered`, `runsFailedUnrecovered`, `runsFailedPending` and `board.byRoute`.
  > - `ramp.ts`: a run counts as failed once it was seen `failed` or `timed_out`. It is recovered when its task reaches `done`. It is unrecovered when the task rests not done, needs Retry, or has no live run for `--recovery-grace-minutes` after the failure. A task whose interruption has an automatic retry scheduled (`state: retry_scheduled`, `nextRetryAt` or `retryWait`) does not count as needing Retry. Spend comes from `costs/by-agent` for the run's agents on each poll, and each step reports the spend in its window. Board probes keep a distribution per route.
  > - `config.ts`: add `--max-failed-run-percent` (default 2) and `--recovery-grace-minutes` (default 15).
- test(server): check the agent row lock with FOR SHARE so lock checks cannot block each other (#21775)
  > - `agentRowLocked()` uses `FOR SHARE NOWAIT` instead of `FOR UPDATE NOWAIT`. The product takes `FOR UPDATE` on this row (`agent-file-store.ts`, `agent-instruction-revisions.ts`), which conflicts with `FOR SHARE`. Two `FOR SHARE` checks do not conflict.
  > - New test: three concurrent checks all report unlocked, a `FOR UPDATE` held on another connection is reported locked, and the row reads unlocked again after release.
- perf(server): reuse sandbox provider schemas for five seconds on the remote-run path (#21758)
  > - `resolvePluginSandboxProviderConfigSchema` now returns the retained schema without a database read when the entry was checked less than `PROVIDER_SCHEMA_REUSE_MS` (5,000 ms) ago and the plugin manifest revision did not change. After that, it uses the existing cheap revision check, as before.
  > - A lookup sequence number stops an older, slower lookup from replacing a newer retained entry.
  > - A missing provider is still never retained, so a provider that becomes available is used on the next operation.
- test(scaling): wait for agent setup and gate the ramp on a first started run (#21757)
  > - `setup.ts`: add `waitForAgentsReady`, `agentReady` and `describeAgent`. The harness reads the company agent list until each agent has setup state `ready` and is not paused. It stops when an agent reports a setup error for 60 s, or after `--agent-ready-minutes` (default 10), and prints the agent's status, setup state, holds and Paperclip's reason.
  > - `ramp.ts`: before the first step, a preflight creates one agent and one task and creates the rest only after that task's run starts. A new check stops the ramp when no run has started within `--start-timeout-minutes` (default 5) of the first task. The stop reason names the task status, any execution blocker, the run status, error code, error and liveness, and the agent's setup state. New stop criteria: `agents_ready` and `run_started`. Run records keep `error`, `errorCode` and `livenessReason`.
  > - `config.ts`: add `--start-timeout-minutes` and `--agent-ready-minutes`.
- perf(release): stage the verified host release daemon instead of compiling it in publish_canary (#21555)
  > - `packages/paperclip-runner/scripts/build-runner-binary.mjs` (new): when `PAPERCLIP_RELEASE_RUNNER_ARTIFACTS_DIR` is set, it checks the release daemons with the existing `releaseDaemonManifest` (source revision equal to `HEAD`, digest, architecture, build metadata) and stages the host target's daemon at `dist/bin` with the existing `stageRunnerBinary`. Otherwise it runs `pnpm run build:binary` as before.
  > - The Runner `build` chain calls this script in place of `pnpm run build:binary`. `build:binary` itself and the image build are unchanged.
  > - `stage-release-runner-binaries.mjs` exports `metadataContract`.
- docs(skills): add aggregate-bound, lost-runner, and bug-class lessons to fix-failed-paperclip-run (#21745)
  > - Section 4: a repair that makes recovery start more runs states its worst-case company-wide start rate during an outage and what bounds it.
  > - Section 6: re-run CI jobs lost to a runner shutdown; re-run an unrelated failing test once, then check master and tell its owner; after one instance of a bug class, check every entry point or counter of that class.
- refactor: split the runner-e2e and restart-chaos READMEs into a runbook and a catalog (#21743)
  > - `tests/runner-e2e/README.md` is now 767 lines: credentials, local commands, the Daytona image, evidence and cleanup, billing, the dashboard, GitHub Actions, and the report preview.
  > - New `tests/runner-e2e/SUITES.md` (3,055 lines) holds every per-suite section. The two blocks of suite notes that had no heading now have one.
  > - `tests/restart-chaos/README.md` is now 592 lines: run, fault timing, Daytona mode, hosted mode, universal checks, grading notes, pieces, output, and the crash-point suite.
- perf(release): build canary daemons during source verification and keep their dependency cache (#21554)
  > - `release.yml`: `release_runner_source` no longer needs `verify_canary`. On a canonical master push it starts at once. `publish_canary` still requires `verify_canary.result == 'success'`, so nothing publishes from unverified source.
  > - `release-verify.yml`: the native Rust cache `shared-key` drops the Runner source hash. It keeps the Cargo manifest and configuration, host and target.
  > - `release-verify-workflow.test.mjs`: tests that a canonical master push selects the source without the proof, that a fork, other ref or other event does not, that `publish_canary` still needs the proof, and that the cache key contains no source globs.
- perf(recovery): re-check only changed tasks between hourly full backstop passes (#21729)
  > - `server/src/services/recovery/work.ts`:
  > - `server/src/services/recovery/changed-tasks.ts` (new): the set-based "changed since" condition. Each part uses an existing index: `issues (company_id, updated_at)`, runs by `(company_id, context issueId)`, wakes by `(company_id, payload issueId)`, interactions and comments by `(company_id, issue_id)`.
  > - `server/src/services/scoped-deadline-work.ts`: a load result may set `keepUnseen`. Then a completed scan does not drop the pending entries it did not load, so their saved deadlines stay.
- perf(db): carry scope hints in cross-process work notifications (#21681)
  > - `packages/db/src/work-signals.ts`: the per-level pending notifications are now a map of topic to `{ hints, unscoped }`. Savepoint merge and rollback keep the same rules as before.
  > - The hints follow the local "settled" semantics: the union of the hints from `signalDatabaseWork` and `publishDatabaseWorkSettled`. An unhinted `publishDatabaseWorkSettled`, `notifyDatabaseWorkOnCommit`, or a topic with no hints sends the unscoped payload.
  > - A hinted payload is `{version: 1, origin, topic, hints: [...]}`. The field is additive and the version stays 1.
- perf(recovery): read an agent's own reporting chain and each pass's review waits once (#21724)
  > - `server/src/lib/agent-reporting-chain.ts` (new): `readAgentReportingChain` reads the agent's managers with one recursive CTE up `reports_to`, limited to the agent's company. `UNION` stops the walk at a cycle. A root agent costs no statement.
  > - `server/src/services/agent-invokability.ts`: `evaluateAgentInvokabilityFromDb` evaluates against the chain, not the company list. It reads nothing when the agent's own status is not invokable, because the status then decides the answer and the chain is not part of it. Every caller benefits with no change: wake enqueue, queued-run claim, retries, run dispatch, wake-queue promotion, continuation delivery, task watchdogs, thread interactions, native completion feedback, native session resumption, native review participants, agent conversations and recovery.
  > - `server/src/lib/agent-records.ts`: `getById` computes `orgChainHealth` from the chain, not from the company list.
- test(e2e): accept every dependency wake producer for the one automatic resume (#21732)
  > - `task-dependency-resume-evidence.ts`: the wake source must be one of `issue.blockers_resolved`, `issue_graph_liveness.backstop`, `workspace.finalize` (the producers of this wake reason in `server/src`).
  > - Still required: one dependent run, `invocationSource` automation / `triggerDetail` system, wake reason `issue_blockers_resolved`, the resolved blocker id, start after blocker completion, committed native run, no leftover checkout.
- test(restart-chaos): let R5 wait out a slow model request and stop calling held wakes undispatched (#21730)
  > - `grader.ts`: add `PROVIDER_TURN_LIMIT_MS` (10 minutes). `deadRunningRun` uses this limit instead of the 3-minute `STALL_LIMIT_MS` when the run sample has `providerTurnActive`. A run with a tool in flight or a live process is still skipped as before.
  > - Why 10 minutes: the observed slow request took 303.6 s. Common provider HTTP clients (the OpenAI and Anthropic SDKs) time out a request at 10 minutes. After 10 minutes the provider client has given up, so continued quiet means the run is dead, not slow. A turn whose process died also keeps `providerTurnActive` set, so the bound still catches it.
  > - `release-swap-grade.ts`: omit the "N queued wake(s) never dispatched" note while the task has a queued, running or scheduled-retry run. The stale-run note stays.
- test(e2e): find the native Plan chip in the Coordinator chat thread (#21727)
  > - `planStageRootSelector(native)`: native root is a settled task-chat turn or the task chat thread. `expectPlanStageVisible` uses it. The revision-name check from #21701 is unchanged.
- perf(chat): keep GitHub review check deadlines on company-wide rebuilds (#21725)
  > - `server/src/services/chat-channels/github/review-check-work.ts`:
  > - `server/src/services/chat-channels/github/review-check-work.test.ts` (new, real PostgreSQL):
  > - `server/src/__tests__/chat-channels.integration.test.ts`:
- perf(server): read the instance role and membership once per GET request in authorization (#21716)
  > - New `server/src/services/authorization-read-scope.ts`: `authorizationReadScope` (an AsyncLocalStorage scope for GET and HEAD) and `readOncePerRequest`.
  > - `authorizationService.isInstanceAdmin` and `getActiveMembership` use `readOncePerRequest`. The existing batch snapshot memo still comes first.
  > - `issueReadSqlCondition` and `projectReadSqlCondition` use the same `admin:<userId>` key for their instance admin check.
- perf(server): list skills without a folder transaction when bundled folders exist (#21715)
  > - `ensureBundledCategory`: a plain read fast path when the category folder and the bundled root exist and the name matches. Create and rename still run under the company folder lock.
  > - `pruneEmptyBundledCategories`: a plain read pre-check. The delete still runs under the lock, and it computes the empty set again under the lock.
  > - New test `company-skills-list-statements.test.ts`: a warm skills list read has no transaction and no advisory lock and at most 45 statements; two concurrent first ensures create exactly one folder; two concurrent first list requests create no duplicate folder; a renamed category is repaired and an empty category is pruned through the locked path.
- perf(server): read the issue detail with fewer database statements (#21713)
  > - `issueService.getRelationSummaries` and `getCurrentScheduledRetry` accept an optional known `companyId` and then skip the issue read.
  > - `issueService.findMentionedProjectIds` accepts the loaded issue (`companyId`, `title`, `description`).
  > - `issueService.getAncestors` accepts the known `parentId` and walks the chain in one recursive query. It keeps the order (nearest first), the limit of 50, and the stop at a cycle or at a task that is its own parent.
- perf(chat): read the run state of a GitHub response sweep page in one batch (#21711)
  > - `server/src/services/chat-channels/github/response-run-state.ts`:
  > - `server/src/services/chat-channels/github/response-execution.ts`: `githubResponseExecution` takes an optional pre-read state. It throws a `{ forbidden }` state as the same 403 that the single read throws.
  > - `server/src/services/chat-channels/github/terminal-response-work.ts`: `load()` reads each page's run state with one `readGitHubResponseRuns` call. `check()` reads the run state once: after the acknowledgement it reads the row only, and keeps a request that is still owed for the next check, which reads its run state again first.
- perf(native): accept a plugin's in-preparation project-auth check on warm attach (#21710)
  > - `packages/plugins/sdk/src/environment-tasks.ts`: add optional `projectAuthChecked: true` to the `prepared` result, with a doc comment that defines it.
  > - `server/src/services/environment-task-runtime.ts`: when a `prepared` result has `projectAuthChecked`, record `runnerTaskProjectAuthValidation` for the launch's configuration, provider and projects, without `atSubmit`. This is the proof that `submit` and `read_file` require.
  > - `server/src/services/native-runtime/plugin-runner-task.ts`: do not send `check_project_auth` when the prepared result has `projectAuthChecked`.
- test(server): stop the accounting identity child from timing out on loaded runners (#21712)
  > - The accounting identity helper child is now `node -e` with no tsx loader and no module imports. It installs a `SIGUSR2` handler, prints `ready`, and waits.
  > - When the test sees `ready`, it reads the child start time with the product function `observedProcessStartedAt(pid)` and then sends `SIGUSR2`. The child exits with code 0.
  > - The test builds the recorded `ControllerProcess` (random boot id, child pid, `currentProcessSpaceHash()`, observed start time). These are the same values the child computed for itself before. The child process is still real, has its own process group, and has exited before the Retry checks run.
- test(e2e): use the Coordinator task panel, inbox and plan controls in QA journeys (#21701)
  > - New `product-journeys/task-panel.ts` helper: opens the side panel and selects a tab (Properties or Artifacts) from the chooser, an open tab, or the "Open a new tab" button. It waits for the page instead of a single non-waiting visibility check, and does not close an already open panel.
  > - task-properties, task-edit-lifecycle, task-dependency-resume, project-task-context and task-document-persistence (and routine/agent journeys that reuse `openDocumentAuthoring`) use the helper. task-properties reopens the panel after reload before it checks "You".
  > - inbox-triage: when the row has no visible "Mark as read" button, open the Mine view, blur search, hover the row and press `r` (as `tests/e2e/coordinator-inbox-views.spec.ts` does).
- perf(db): build the agent request authentication statements once per client (#21707)
  > - `server/src/services/prepared-statements.ts`: add `preparedStatement(prepare)`, the single-statement form of the existing `preparedStatements` helper (one prepared query for each `Db`, held in a `WeakMap`).
  > - `server/src/middleware/agent-run-authentication.ts`: split the query into `agentRunAuthenticationQuery(db, binding)`; `loadAgentRunAuthentication` executes the query prepared once with placeholders.
  > - `server/src/middleware/auth.ts`: add `agentAuthenticationQuery` and `responsibleUserMembershipsQuery`; the JWT path, the agent key path and `loadResponsibleUserMemberships` execute their prepared forms.
- perf(server): bound dedicated database connections with a fair queue (#21693)
  > - `packages/db/src/client.ts`: `withDedicatedDbConnection` admits callers against a per-owner limit (`DATABASE_DEDICATED_CONNECTION_MAX`, option `dedicatedConnectionMax`, default 24) with a first-in, first-out queue. The slot is released after the connection is returned or closed.
  > - Nested callers (an active dedicated hold of the same owner in the async context) and callers inside an open transaction of a client from this process are admitted without a wait. The transaction marker is an `AsyncLocalStorage` scope that ends when the transaction callback ends.
  > - A queued caller is admitted over the limit after `DATABASE_DEDICATED_CONNECTION_WAIT_MS` (option `dedicatedConnectionWaitMs`, default 30000).
- refactor: remove doc/execution-semantics.md and its references (#21699)
  > - Delete `doc/execution-semantics.md`.
  > - Remove links and pointers in `doc/PRODUCT.md`, `doc/LOW-TRUST-PRESETS.md`, `doc/TASK-WATCHDOG.md`, `doc/composer-stop.md`, `doc/native-workspace-finalization-recovery.md`, `doc/run-log-events.md`, `doc/runner-internals.md` and `doc/workspace-repositories.md`.
  > - `doc/TASK-WATCHDOG.md` now points at `server/src/modules/active-run-watchdog/` for the silent active-run watchdog.
- perf(server): download stale agent files before the agent row lock (#21694)
  > - `agent-file-durability.ts`: the download loop moves into `downloadAgentFiles`, unchanged. `withAgentFilesRestoredBeforeLock(work)` runs `work` in a scope. Inside the scope, the first restore of an attempt that needs a download throws a private deferral error, which rolls back the transaction. The wrapper stages the download in `.agent-files-<uuid>` next to the root and runs `work` again. `restoreDurableAgentFiles` uses a staged directory only when its generation equals the head it reads under the lock. Staged directories are removed when the scope ends.
  > - Only the first restore of each attempt can defer, so nested restores of other agents keep their old behavior. Outside the scope, the behavior is unchanged.
  > - `agent-file-store.ts` (`locked`, so every store read, write, apply and working-copy preparation) and `agent-instruction-revisions.ts` (`readCommittedForRuntime`, `readCurrent`, `materializeCurrent`) run their transactions in the scope.
- perf(server): capture a remote agent-file checkpoint before the directory lock (#21690)
  > - `agent-directory-working-copies.ts`: `checkpointWarm` captures the remote checkpoint before `serial()` for a disposable remote copy (hosted servers only), with the same inputs that the locked capture uses.
  > - Under the lock, `checkpoint()` reuses that capture on its first attempt only when the current row equals the row the capture used (`adoptionAuthority`, which excludes only `updatedAt` and `nextAttemptAt`) and the target is the same. If the row changed, it captures again under the lock, as before.
  > - If the early capture fails, the code logs a warning and captures under the lock, as before. The early capture's scratch is removed after the lock is released.
- test(scaling): add a real-agent ramp harness for hosted instances (#21695)
  > - `scripts/scale-agents-ramp.ts` and `scripts/scale-ramp/`: the harness. It reads the base URL from a flag or the environment and the credentials from the environment only. It creates or reuses a company, then creates agents, tasks, a project and an optional provider secret. It ramps concurrency in steps (default 10, 50, 100, 200, 500, 1,000) and keeps each agent on one small deterministic task at a time.
  > - It measures per step: tasks started, finished, failed and needing Retry; failed runs; peak running agents; queue wait; time to first token and first activity (from sampled run events); run duration; board GET latency. An optional metrics endpoint supplies event-loop, statement and pool figures. A missing source is reported as "not measured", never as zero.
  > - It evaluates the criteria from the plan's finish line plus harness criteria, writes them into `result.json` and `SUMMARY.md`, and stops at the first failure.
- refactor: cut DATABASE.md to setup and schema-change rules (#21700)
  > - `doc/DATABASE.md` is now 377 lines. It keeps the three database modes, client tuning, the schema change workflow, migration compatibility, the migration authoring checklist, migration snapshots, the rule for retries after a lost connection, transaction-aware work signals, plugin namespaces, and backups. The wording of the kept text is unchanged.
  > - New `doc/database-client-internals.md` holds the response-size diagnostic, the notes on the postgres.js patch, and the transaction memory note. The text is moved without wording changes.
  > - "Experimental remote workspace snapshots" moved to `doc/workspace-storage.md`, which links to it.
- perf(recovery): back off assigned-work re-checks that find the same refused state (#21691)
  > - `server/src/services/recovery/assigned-work-sweep.ts`:
  > - `server/src/services/recovery/work.ts`: a task that waits on `waiting_unchanged` is marked on its queue entry. When an event reloads the task, the entry becomes due at once instead of keeping the later deadline. A backstop reload keeps the deadline.
  > - `server/src/services/recovery/assigned-work-invariant.ts`: the new activity text, and the optional `updatedAt` on the agent fact.
- perf(recovery): wait for an assignee that cannot run instead of retrying its continuation every pass (#21687)
  > - `server/src/services/execution-recovery-resolution.ts`: before the wake, delivery calls `deferForUnwakeableAssignee`. When the assignee cannot run, delivery skips the wake. The pending-page query skips continuations whose stored retry time is in the future. The file stays under 1,000 lines (997).
  > - `server/src/services/recovery/continuation-delivery-attempt.ts`: `deferForUnwakeableAssignee` reads the assignee `FOR SHARE` and evaluates it with `evaluateAgentInvokabilityFromDb`, the rule `heartbeat.wakeup` uses (including the setup exception). In the same transaction it stores `continuationDeliveryRetryAt` and a wait count on the recovery action. The wait is 1 minute and doubles per refusal up to 1 hour. The row lock orders the wait against a concurrent status change, so a resume cannot be missed. `continuationDeliveryDue` is the shared "due now" condition. `readmitAgentContinuations` notifies the continuation worker, then clears the stored time for one agent's waiting continuations.
  > - `server/src/services/execution-control-work.ts`: the continuation schedule asks for a pass in 15 seconds only when a continuation is due. Otherwise it returns the earliest stored retry time.
- perf(server): size run-log uploads and object journal IO from open run logs (#21685)
  > - `server/src/lib/active-run-slots.ts`: a small helper that gives a slot count from an active run count, clamped between a floor and a ceiling.
  > - `server/src/storage/s3-client-limits.ts` and `s3-provider.ts`: the S3 client sets `maxSockets` to 50 (the SDK default) from a named constant, so the budget is explicit.
  > - `object-byte-journal.ts`: the object operation limit is the upload slots plus a read reserve of 4, from the number of open run-log writers. Open writers are counted with `trackObjectJournalWriter()`. When the limit grows, waiting operations start at once. When it shrinks, running operations finish and no new ones start until the count is below the limit.
- perf(workspaces): read a downloaded snapshot archive once, in the encrypt pass (#21683)
  > - `captureSandboxWorkspaceArchive` no longer re-hashes the downloaded archive. It checks with `lstat` that the download is a file with the receipt's byte size, and fails with `remote_workspace_capture_transfer_mismatch` when it is not.
  > - `uploadWorkspaceSnapshot` accepts an optional `expectedArchive` (`sha256`, `byteSize`). After the encrypt pass, it compares the sealed plaintext digest and size with it. A mismatch throws `remote_workspace_capture_transfer_mismatch` before the attempt enters `metadata.uploads` and before `putFile`. The scratch ciphertext is removed as before.
  > - `saveRemoteWorkspace` passes the sandbox receipt as `expectedArchive` when it captured an archive.
- perf(server): skip the per-request stale instance-admin purge when no row exists (#21680)
  > - The Cloud trusted-header path reads the user's active memberships together with an `exists` check for an `instance_admin` row, in one statement.
  > - The purge runs only when that check finds a row, when the user has no active membership row yet (first request), or when the read fails.
  > - The session path and the Cloud path share one membership filter function, so both still resolve the same access set.
- perf(recovery): keep a finished task's settled attention settled and halve graph hint fan-out (#21676)
  > - `server/src/services/issue-recovery-actions.ts`:
  > - `server/src/services/recovery/service.ts`: the `source_terminal` branch of `reconcileActiveRecoveryActions` uses `sourceTerminal: true`.
  > - `server/src/services/issue-recovery-notifications.ts`: `notifyIssueGraphRecovery` publishes one graph hint per task queue and no longer adds a `{companyId, backstop: true}` hint per queue. A new `issueRecoveryBackstopHint(companyId)` builds that hint string.
- refactor: cut DEVELOPING.md to a setup and workflow guide (#21670)
  > - `doc/DEVELOPING.md` is now 660 lines: prerequisites, start dev, health checks, local run, test drive, Docker, instance layout, database, storage, secrets, worktrees, test commands, Storybook, CLI, and an index of the other docs.
  > - 15 new docs hold the moved text without wording changes: `ci-workflows`, `test-infrastructure`, `worktrees`, `hot-restart-deploys`, `heartbeat-internals`, `runner-internals`, `workspace-repositories`, `workspace-storage`, `skills-and-teams-catalog`, `ui-internals`, `case-handoffs`, `chat-connector-development`, `instance-configuration`, `integration-api-notes`, `database-backups`.
  > - "Publish a branch Storybook" moved to `doc/STORYBOOK-DEPLOYMENT.md`. The privacy rollout notes moved to `doc/ISSUE-PRIVACY.md`.
- perf(native-runtime): stop re-finalizing native results whose finalization error is recorded (#21674)
  > - `surfaceNativeControllerNamespaceAttention` (shared helper): before it creates attention, it checks for a resolved incident for this run that was resolved at or after the owner's current lease expiry. If one exists, it writes nothing.
  > - New `settledNativeControllerNamespaceAttention` SQL predicate. It is shared by the helper, the release candidate page, and the release deadline query, so all three use the same rule.
  > - `settleFailedRunOwner` (expired-owner path) does not surface attention when the task is `done` or `cancelled`. The restart-recovery claim path has the same guard.
- perf(native-runtime): stop re-surfacing settled controller namespace attention (#21675)
  > - `surfaceNativeControllerNamespaceAttention` (shared helper): before it creates attention, it checks for a resolved incident for this run that was resolved at or after the owner's current lease expiry. If one exists, it writes nothing.
  > - New `settledNativeControllerNamespaceAttention` SQL predicate. It is shared by the helper, the release candidate page, and the release deadline query, so all three use the same rule.
  > - `settleFailedRunOwner` (expired-owner path) does not surface attention when the task is `done` or `cancelled`. The restart-recovery claim path has the same guard.
- perf(ui): show a returning task's saved chat before any server response (#21671)
  > - `ui/src/lib/persisted-query-cache.ts`: saves the reads the first chat frame waits for (`taskChatFirstFrameRef`): recent comments, their input facts, recent activity, the recent run window, interactions, attachment metadata, live and active runs, work products and the plan document.
  > - `ui/src/lib/issue-chat-bootstrap.ts` and `ui/src/main.tsx`: `adoptHardLoadChatBootstrap` adopts the bootstrap that the HTML started, at app start, under the task's restored id and identifier. Readers that start before the task page mounts share it.
  > - `IssueDetail.tsx`, `LiveRunWidget.tsx`, `IssueRunLedger.tsx`, `useTaskPluginPanels.ts`: their `live-runs` reads share the loading bootstrap. Before, whichever observer mounted first could send its own request.
- perf(ui): open a task restored from the saved read cache through the chat bootstrap (#21650)
  > - `ui/src/lib/persisted-query-cache.ts`: records which queries a page restored from the saved read cache. `isRestoredSavedRead(client, key)` is true until a read or a local write in this page replaces that data.
  > - `ui/src/pages/IssueDetail.tsx`: starts (or adopts) the chat bootstrap when the core task is missing or only restored. It passes the restored task's id and identifier as aliases, so reads keyed by the id share the request while it loads.
  > - `ui/src/lib/issue-chat-bootstrap.ts`: `readFromIssueChatBootstrap` lets a key that holds only restored data share the bootstrap, like an empty key. `startIssueChatBootstrap` accepts aliases.
- test(evals): write the hard-stop setup note in plain words (#21487)
  > - `budgetIncidentSourceDescription` in `tests/runner-e2e/chat-catalog-domains/identity-budget-incidents.ts` builds the note. The note names the task, the recorded hard-stop incident and the separately paused agent. It no longer says that the receipt is an imported synthetic fixture.
  > - The grader is unchanged. It already proves the receipt's origin from saved state (`receiptOrigin`), not from the reply. The note's task identifier, incident reference and paused-agent name are unchanged. The grader matches no literal from the note.
  > - TestOps manifest: no change is necessary. The case's oracle version, source file list, query, context and expected outcome are unchanged.
- perf(github): reuse repository installation tokens until shortly before expiry (#21641)
  > - `githubBotRepositoryToken` keeps a minted token in a per-process, in-memory cache (at most 512 entries, least recently used first out). It never persists or logs tokens.
  > - The cache uses the token until 5 minutes before GitHub's `expires_at` (capped at one hour). If GitHub does not give a usable expiry, the token is not kept.
  > - The cache key holds the company, endpoint, repository id, the exact permission set, and an authority revision. The revision holds the connection id, the endpoint's runtime generation (pause, revoke and availability changes advance it), and for each mint credential its secret ref, version selector, secret status, current version, `updatedAt`, and binding. The installation id is a secret, so its secret ref and version identify it in the key.
- perf(workspaces): start a full terminal workspace pass only at startup and on the hourly backstop (#21630)
  > - `server/src/services/terminal-workspace-work.ts`:
  > - `server/src/services/execution-workspaces.ts`:
  > - `server/src/services/plugin-registry.ts`: the two workspace-provider notifications send `plugin-workspaces` instead of no hint.
- perf(recovery): stop re-asserting a failed legacy run whose decision is recorded (#21621)
  > - `server/src/services/legacy-execution-recovery.ts`:
  > - `server/src/__tests__/issue-recovery-recorded-decision-idle.test.ts`: two new tests on real PostgreSQL. They use the real coordinator, the real issue-state recovery work and the production 5-minute backstop on a fake clock.
- perf(workspaces): read a sweep page's task trees and active runs in two statements (#21618)
  > - `server/src/services/execution-workspaces.ts`: add `readPageIssueTrees` and `readPageActiveRunWorkspaces`, and use them for the per-page pre-filter.
  > - `server/src/__tests__/terminal-workspace-page-batch.test.ts`: two new tests on real PostgreSQL.
- perf(workspaces): inspect only due workspaces and wait for a holding run's release (#21617)
  > - `server/src/services/execution-workspaces.ts`:
  > - `server/src/services/terminal-workspace-work.ts`: a due run uses the due targets instead of a full rotation, unless they overflow or a non-workspace deadline is due. A due run that finds nothing due does nothing. Any hint arms the hourly backstop rotation.
  > - `server/src/services/heartbeat/run-lifecycle.ts`: a terminal run status hints the run's task to the terminal workspace queue.
- perf(chat): scope GitHub review check run hints to the issue and throttle company-wide rebuilds (#21610)
  > - `review-check-work.ts`: the scope has an optional `issueId`. `page()` reads the issue of the latest review for the check head (`reviewIssueId`, through the existing `chat_github_reviews_pull_idx` index) and filters on it for an issue scope. `matches()` uses the same value.
  > - `review-check-work.ts`: set `reconstructionIntervalMs` to `githubReviewCheckReconstructionIntervalMs`: 30 seconds for a company-only scope, 0 for all other scopes.
  > - `chat-run-milestone-notifications.ts`: send the issue with the review check hint when the milestone has one.
- perf(chat): back off GitHub review check head polls while the check cannot change (#21608)
  > - `chat-github-checks.ts`: save `unchangedPolls` in the check result. A poll that finds the same state as the saved state adds one (up to 6). A changed state, a first publication and a recovered failure set it to 0. The next head poll is due after `min(5 minutes, 5 seconds × 2^unchangedPolls)`.
  > - Export `GITHUB_REVIEW_CHECK_HEAD_POLL_MS` (5 seconds) and `GITHUB_REVIEW_CHECK_MAX_HEAD_POLL_MS` (5 minutes).
  > - `chat-channels.integration.test.ts`: two regression tests. They use the real review check queue, the real `dispatch`, embedded PostgreSQL, a controlled clock and one pass each second. The fixture has the hosted state: a review with no run, a delivery with a linked comment, and no run for that comment.
- perf(recovery): read only the run keys that active execution recovery checks use (#21602)

This is all `heartbeat_runs` columns, except that `context_snapshot` holds only `wakeReason`, `issueId` and `nativeQuestionCancellation`, `runner_profile_json` holds only `nativeStartupContext`, `nativeWorkspaceSync` and `remoteWorkspace`, and the stdout/stderr excerpts are null. It does not select `agents.adapter_config`.

The process-lost settle uses that data for the conversation-adapter check, the retry budget, the process-loss diagnostic, the stop metadata and the first-heartbeat flag.

- perf(db): keep postgres.js connection queues proportional to live connections (#21601)
  > - `patches/postgres@3.4.9.patch`: adds one hunk each to `src/queue.js` (ESM, which the server loads) and `cjs/src/queue.js` (CommonJS). All existing hunks are unchanged. The added hunk is:
  > - `packages/db/src/postgres-queue.test.ts`: for both the ESM and CommonJS builds, checks FIFO order, `length` and `remove` results. It compares the queue with a plain array over 300,000 random operations that cross the compaction threshold. It also runs 1,000,000 queries in the pattern where `open` never empties, and checks that the slots scanned by `remove` and the backing array size stay bounded by the live connections.
  > - `packages/db/src/__fixtures__/postgres-queue-pool.mjs` and `packages/db/src/postgres-queue-pool.test.ts`: run a real pool (24 connections, 8 concurrent queries, 20,000 queries) against embedded PostgreSQL for both builds. They check that a dispatch scans no more slots than there are connections.

- perf(chat): read the GitHub response run by issue run indexes (#21577)

It also requires a plan bound: one call must read fewer than 150 `heartbeat_runs` rows while the agent has 1,500 unrelated runs.

- perf(db): reuse dedicated connections instead of opening one per call (#21567)
  > - `packages/db/src/client.ts`: each `createDb` owner now has one set of dedicated single-connection clients, shared with every private client derived from that owner (so nested dedicated actions use the same set).
  > - `server/src/index.ts`: shutdown closes the dedicated set next to the other private pools.
  > - `doc/DATABASE.md`: documents the idle cap, the "not an admission limit" rule, and the transaction-scoped session state contract.
- perf(load-model): model recorded native run rates, the recorded event mix and coalesced delta batches (#21549)
  > - Profiles, the recorded event mix (a 100-slot cycle per agent, offset by agent so the fleet sends the mix at every moment), the span append path, and the coalesced delta batches.
  > - The model test now checks the profile in the report and that both server trace spans and runner events with a progress wake are in the mix, for the recorded profile and for the stress profile with one transaction per delta.
  > - The plan has a new section "Load model at recorded rates": the recorded source (40 recent native runs on one hosted workspace, as aggregates), the profiles, the mix and its server paths, fresh runs at 500, 1,000 and 2,000 agents plus burst and stress, and what they show. It records the 3-connection checkpoint pool as a computed per-process limit on a hosted database, and that SQL building in Drizzle costs about as much main-thread time as result parsing on the hosted instance. A new section "Considered and not done" records why `run.performance.span` rows stay (about 3% of the statements per agent, with readers) and why checkpoint saves per critical event only are not done now.
- perf(checkpoint): write a routine controller checkpoint save in one statement (#21553)
  > - `ROUTINE_CHECKPOINT_SAVE_SQL` and the routine branch in `nativeRunnerCheckpointWriter`.
- perf(tool-access): index enabled policies so each decision checks only candidates (#21563)
  > - Add `server/src/services/tool-access/policy-selector-index.ts`. It compiles a policy selector into a matcher with the same semantics as the raw `selectorMatches`, and it indexes policies by `catalogEntryId`, then by `toolName` (matched against the tool name and the upstream tool name) for policies without a catalog entry selector. Policies with neither key are candidates for every call. Candidates keep their original order.
  > - `decideInternal` caches the compiled index under the same `enabled-policies:${companyId}` key. The new `matchEnabledToolPolicies` keeps the two-step semantics: the first selected policy with unsupported conditions denies the call before any match applies, then the selected policies with matching conditions go to the unchanged decision loop (block, rate limit, trust rule, require approval, allow, explanations).
  > - Decisions without a cache, and decisions that consume a rate limit (which read fresh rows), compile the rows they read in the same way.
- perf(run-events): append events whose only wake is chat progress in one statement (#21559)
  > - `appendHeartbeatRunEvent`: `progressOnly` (progress set, every other wake clear) also qualifies for the one-statement path.
  > - `APPEND_NEW_NATIVE_SOURCE_EVENTS_WITH_PROGRESS_SQL`: the one-statement append, plus `runtime_mode` and the bound task id (`boundRunIssueIdSql`, rendered once) from the locked row, plus a conditional `pg_notify`.
  > - `chat-native-progress-notifications.ts`: `chatNativeProgressCommitNotification` and `publishChatNativeProgressSettled`.
- perf(ui): load the @task mention pool after the task page settles; add a cold-load perf spec (#21557)
  > - `ui/src/hooks/usePageSettled.ts`: new hook. It returns `true` once `whenPageSettles` runs (the page has painted, the tab is visible, no query is fetching, and the browser is idle), or after `PAGE_SETTLED_MAX_WAIT_MS` (5 s). The cap keeps a long-running read from keeping the picker empty.
  > - `ui/src/pages/IssueDetail.tsx`: the mention-pool query is enabled only when `usePageSettled()` is true.
  > - `ui/src/hooks/usePageSettled.test.tsx`: tests that the hook waits for in-flight reads and that the cap applies.
- perf(ui): reveal a returning Mine visit from the saved cache (#20594)
  > - `ui/src/lib/inbox-mine-reveal-sources.ts` (new): the record `["inbox", companyId, "mine-reveal-sources", userId]` holds the approvals visible in Mine, the pending join requests, and the dismissals of those items and of Mine's failed runs. A record over 256 KB is replaced by an empty `incomplete` record that never answers the wait. `readMineRevealSources` accepts a record only for the same user and company.
  > - `ui/src/lib/persisted-query-cache.ts`: the saved cache keeps that record per user and company, only when its data names the same user and company as its key.
  > - `ui/src/pages/Inbox.tsx`:
- perf(run-events): read the wake's transaction id and the replay probe in the lock read (#21528)
  > - `primeDatabaseWorkTransactionId(connection, xid)` (`work-signals.ts`, exported from `@paperclipai/db`): inside a transaction whose id is not yet known, it records the id. Outside a transaction it does nothing. An uncertain commit still resolves with `pg_xact_status` on the same id.
  > - `appendNativeSourceEvent`: the lock read returns the transaction id when the event has any wake. For a probing wake it is one statement: a CTE that reads `next_event_seq` from the snapshot, a CTE that locks the run row (`for no key update`, unchanged), and `exists (...)` for the replay. The wake is still registered after the replay check and before the append.
  > - `lockNativeRunnerCheckpointOwner` returns the transaction id with its owner lock and primes the wake state. Its return value and every caller are unchanged.
- Keep agent-owned connections healthy during catalog refresh (#21539)
  > - Select the same-company, same-connection active agent grant for dedicated connections. Bind agent callers to their own identity. Background and operator work require one unambiguous active grant.
  > - Use that selection in GitHub health checks. Keep missing and ambiguous authorization failures as explicit HTTP 422 responses.
  > - Count only agent grants when projecting reconnect state for a dedicated GitHub connection.
- Start follow-up runs normally when a warm sandbox's window ends during its idle close (#21504)
  > - `server/src/services/native-runtime/native-session-executor.ts` `claimWarmNativeInstructionCopy`: after it retires a stale warm owner whose copy cannot be adopted, it returns `null`. The caller then prepares instructions fresh. Before, it threw `native_instruction_materialization_handoff_unavailable`.
  > - `server/src/services/warm-sandbox-expiry.ts`: the warm-window stop now waits `WARM_SANDBOX_IDLE_CLOSE_MARGIN_MS` (2 minutes) past the window. The in-process idle close (close grace plus the 30 s proof read) finishes first.
  > - `native-session-executor.ts` idle timer: when an idle close fails, it reads the lease rows for that sandbox. If every row is no longer `active` or `retained` (the host gave the sandbox up), it releases the in-process owner. The next run then prepares fresh.
- test(environments): add an opt-in live Daytona proof for warm sandbox expiry (#21463)
  > - New `server/src/__tests__/warm-sandbox-expiry.live.test.ts`. The test is skipped unless `PAPERCLIP_LIVE_WARM_EXPIRY=1` is set. It uses:
