- test(evals): pin the case clock in the original-deadline rejection tests (#21501)
  > - In the four "rejects … original deadline without resetting it" cases, `Date.now` now returns the fixture time until the test jumps it. The "late await" jump is then exactly 1 ms past the observer's deadline. The other three cases fail at the budget check as before.
- Connect Tailscale networks through device sign-in (#21526)
  > - New connections use device sign-in. Existing connected devices and pending legacy OAuth callbacks remain supported.
  > - Pending sign-ins are bound to their company and initiating user. They have a fixed ten-minute deadline. Device state uses server-owned vault secrets with trusted version and digest checks.
  > - The active Tailscale CLI waits for consent and device approval. Cancellation, expiry, secret deletion, and stale completion cannot restore a cancelled connection.
- perf(workspaces): inspect only hinted workspaces after a scoped commit (#21519)
  > - `server/src/services/terminal-workspace-work.ts`: parse scoped hints into targets. Run a targeted inspection when only scoped hints are pending. Keep the full rotation for startup, due deadlines, unscoped hints, continuation pages and failures.
  > - `server/src/services/terminal-workspace-work.ts` also falls back to a full rotation when more than 50 workspaces wait for inspection. It keeps the 1-second pause between rotation pages and runs targeted inspections between them.
  > - `server/src/services/issues.ts`: moving a task also hints its former parent. Removing a task also hints its parent and its execution workspace. Without this, a former parent's workspace could never be reached.
- perf(attention): scope native finalization retention hints to the run's task graph (#21521)
  > - `server/src/services/native-finalization-notifications.ts`: send the company attention retention hint only for a finalization without a task or run.
  > - `server/src/__tests__/attention-retention-work.test.ts`: new test `scopes native run finalization to the task graph of the run`.
- perf(run-events): collect deltas that arrive while a batch commits into one next batch (#21520)
  > - `#flushStreamingEvents` queues one cut on the run's commit chain (`#streamingFlushQueued`). The cut takes every delta queued at that time, sets the first-text flag, commits the batch, and then releases the pending count of that batch.
  > - A flush while a cut waits returns the same chain, so a caller that waits for it (every other event, `checkpointSession`, `completeRun`, `replayEvents`, `provesNoRunProviderWork`) still waits for every delta queued before it.
  > - A batch can now hold more than 100 deltas when commits are slow. The 100-delta rule still asks for a flush; the batch is one statement with a fixed number of bind parameters (#21460, #21511).
- perf(run-events): append in-order native events in one statement (#21511)
  > - `appendNewNativeSourceEvents` (`heartbeat-run-events.ts`) is one statement: a CTE reads `next_event_seq` from the statement snapshot, an `update heartbeat_runs ... returning` locks the run row and moves `next_event_seq` and the source cursor, and an `insert ... select from jsonb_array_elements($9)` writes every row. The text is fixed, with nine bind parameters for any batch size.
  > - **Why the `next_event_seq` check.** A statement that waits for the run row lock keeps the snapshot it took before the wait. Its replay probe cannot see events that the lock holder committed. Every event append (native, ordinary, the run coordinator store, the retry queue) moves `next_event_seq` in its own transaction. So the update requires the value that its snapshot read. PostgreSQL re-checks that condition on the newest row version after the wait, and any append committed meanwhile makes the statement write nothing. The unique indexes on `(run_id, source_event_id)` and `(run_id, source_instance_id, source_seq)` are a second guard: a unique violation from this statement also falls back.
  > - A source without a cursor starts at 0. The probe still finds any stored event of that source, so a run that an older server wrote falls back and recounts.
- perf(recovery): check only blocked tasks whose blockers are done (#21517)
  > - `server/src/services/recovery/blocker-stall-wakeups.ts`: when the company stall scan stops at its page limit, it returns an immediate retry deadline. The recovery-actions pass then continues until the cursor wraps.
  > - `server/src/services/recovery/work.ts`: the shared `register` helper takes an optional extra SQL condition. The `resolved-dependencies` queue gives a condition that keeps only blocked tasks with at least one `blocks` relation and only `done` blockers.
  > - `server/src/__tests__/issue-recovery-deadlines.test.ts`: a new test with 300 blocked tasks behind an open blocker and 1 blocked task behind a done blocker. It runs the real coordinator, notification and SQL.
- chore(lockfile): refresh pnpm lockfiles (#21516)
  > - Refresh the dependency lock from the manifests on master.
  > - Refresh the sandbox provider lockfiles from their manifests on master.
  > - Include the matching offline report correction and hashes.
- refactor(acpx-sidecar): extract the runtime event step and the test process helper (#21419)
  > - `src/cli/acpx-runtime-sidecar.ts`: 74 lines added and 56 removed. The code moved with its comments, with no change.
  > - `src/cli/acpx-runtime-sidecar.test.ts` and `src/test-support/acpx-sidecar-process.ts`: the helper moved, 138 lines added and 132 removed.
- perf(server): add a load model of per-agent server cost and the plan for 1,000 concurrent agents (#21418)
  > - Added `doc/plans/2026-11-13-thousand-concurrent-agents.md`: finish line, evidence, load model result, phases, and owner decisions. It now also has a "Load model after Phase 1" section. That section has a table at 50, 100, 200 and 400 agents and a row at 200 agents with deltas and leases as before Phase 1. The 400-agent delta count was measured before the count fix and is marked as not reliable.
  > - Added `scripts/benchmark-agent-scaling-load.ts`: N simulated agents call the real event append, controller checkpoint save, lease renewal, and agent authentication code against an embedded PostgreSQL. It reports statements for each database pool, event-loop utilization, and lateness for each source.
  > - Added `server/src/__tests__/agent-scaling-load-model.test.ts`. It runs the model end to end in both modes: the default (batched deltas and shared lease renewal) and `DELTA_BATCH_MS=0 LEASE_MODE=single`. It checks that every source finishes without failures and that each pool is counted.
- perf(run-events): commit streaming text deltas in batches (#21460)
  > - `appendHeartbeatRunEventBatch` (`heartbeat-run-events.ts`) appends native source events of one run and one source in one transaction. When the events continue the stored source cursor (from #21435), it sends four statements for the whole batch: the run lock, one replay probe, one `insert ... select from jsonb_array_elements(...)` of every row, and the commit. Any other batch (a replay, a gap, an event with a wake, or a run without a cursor) appends its events one by one in the same transaction, with the single-append checks.
  > - **Storage choice: one row per delta, written by one statement per batch.** I did not store one row per batch. The transcript, live events, run log export, chat progress, replay (`replayEvents`), recovery cursors and the UI all read one row per delta. One statement per batch keeps every reader unchanged, and rows written before this change read the same.
  > - `PaperclipControlPlanePort` sets `pipelinesStreamingEvents`. An `item.delta` appended with the new `streaming` option goes into a queue:
- refactor(runner): remove the direct assigned-gateway path (#21311)
  > - Deleted `packages/paperclip-runner/src/drivers/native-mcp.ts` (`nativeMcpLaunchBinding`).
  > - `drivers/acpx/runtime-host.ts`: removed the native gateway server entry and the name conflict check. The admission gate keeps its message. A run with a binding id is admitted when the sidecar uses the PRP transport with its tool bridge, as the production sidecar always does.
  > - `drivers/acpx/environment.ts`: the name and the URL are not in the provider allowlist.
- perf(runner): keep a quiet Claude provider between turns of a run (#21293)
  > - `drivers/acpx/claude-background-tasks.ts` (new): `ClaudeBackgroundTaskTracker`. It reads raw ACP messages. It reports unknown when the capability is not negotiated, when a task finished, when a live task is not a shell command, when an update is malformed, when a limit is reached, when Claude acts with no client request in flight, or when Claude calls `CronCreate` or `ScheduleWakeup`.
  > - `drivers/acpx/provider-failure.ts` and `acpx-runtime-adapter.ts`: Claude sessions also advertise the `asyncTasks` capability. The raw ACP observer gives each message to the tracker. ACPX 0.22 drops the `async_task_*` updates before the turn event stream, so only the raw observer sees them.
  > - `drivers/acpx/runtime-host.ts`: `backgroundTaskState()`.
- test(runner): guard that a relaunch after a lost launch acknowledgement leaves one Runner (#21477)
  > - New `server/src/services/native-runtime/remote-runner-lost-launch-ack.test.ts`. It is a test only and changes no product code.
- test(decisions): let the concurrent admission test survive a legitimate serialization retry (#21416)
  > - Set the test budget to 2c with 1c reservations. Both decisions are now admitted whether or not one admission retries.
  > - Kept the proof that both admissions are in flight at once (both wait on the same row lock at the same time). This is the no-mutex guarantee.
  > - Kept the checks that both providers run concurrently, both holds are `held` at 1c, both decisions succeed, and two cost events exist.
- perf(server): skip the agent row lock for unchanged agent-file checkpoints and upload before the lock (#21457)
  > - `agent-file-store.ts`: add `authorizeSave(companyId, agentId, actor)` and `saveUnchanged(companyId, agentId, actor)`. `apply` calls it for a checkpoint, then uploads the run's changed files before `locked`, and passes the uploaded objects to `persistDurableAgentFiles`. `locked` takes an optional map of uploaded objects.
  > - `agent-file-durability.ts`: move the verified upload stream into `uploadAgentFile`. Add `uploadAgentFilesBeforeCommit`. It reads the durable head without a lock, skips files the head already has at the same path and digest, and uploads the others. If an upload fails, it stops and the locked commit uploads the rest, so errors are the same as before. `persistDurableAgentFiles` uses an uploaded object when the canonical file has the same digest and size.
  > - `agent-directory-working-copies.ts`: an unchanged checkpoint calls `saveUnchanged` instead of `store.locked(..., true)`.
- perf(native-runtime): share one frozen assigned tool catalog across sessions (#21482)
  > - `server/src/services/native-runtime/assigned-mcp-tools.ts`:
  > - `server/src/services/native-runtime/paperclip-runner-tool-authority.ts`: `close()` also closes the assigned tools. The session executor already closes the authority when it revokes a session's tool authority.
  > - `server/src/services/native-runtime/native-session-executor.ts`: one line. It passes the run binding's `companyId` to `createAssignedMcpTools`.
- perf(recovery): match scoped deadline entries only against scopes of their own company (#21467)
  > - `scoped-deadline-work.ts`: new optional `scopeKey` option (`entry(entry)` and `scope(scope)`). The contract: a scope with a defined key matches only entries with the same key. A scope without a key (for example `*`) is tested against every entry, as before.
  > - The worker indexes the dirty scopes by key, so `next()` and the due filter test each entry only against the scopes of its key and the unkeyed scopes.
  > - When a reconstructed keyed scope drops vanished entries, it visits only the entries of its key (an index built once for each pass).
- perf(native): renew all of a process's execution leases in one transaction, without a recovery wake (#21444)
  > - `native-execution-lease.ts`: a renewal (batched or single) no longer calls `notifyLiveRunRecovery`. A renewal only moves the lease deadline later. Recovery already holds a deadline at the earlier expiry. At that deadline it re-reads the row and moves to the new expiry. A lapsed lease is still found at its expiry. This change does not touch the signals of the writers that release, revoke or take over a lease, or that change the run status. This also removes six notifications for each renewal, which was 1,200 notifications for a batch of 200 runs during a deploy overlap.
  > - `native-execution-lease.ts`: one shared schedule for each database client replaces the timer of each run. When the first lease is due, all leases that are due within a quarter of their own interval renew with it. Thus the leases of a process converge on one batch for each interval.
  > - A batch is one transaction on the liveness pool for each 200 runs: `statement_timeout`, one `SELECT … FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` of the coordinator rows, and one `UPDATE … FROM (VALUES …)`.
- perf(run-events): keep each native source's cursor on the run row (#21435)
  > - Add the nullable column `heartbeat_runs.native_source_cursors` (`jsonb`, `{"<source instance id>": <seq>}`). Migration `0479` is `ADD COLUMN` only and classifies as `online_compatible`.
  > - The run lock read of a native append also reads the cursor of its source.
  > - When the event sequence is at most cursor + 1, the append statement has no `retained` CTE. It moves the cursor in the UPDATE that reserves the sequence, and the receipt uses the cursor.
- perf(ci): shorten the general server and chat test lanes (#21393)
  > - `scripts/test-line-shard.mjs`: `partitionTestLines` takes optional recorded durations. With durations, it balances source lines by recorded time; entries without a record take the median. Without durations, the behavior is unchanged.
  > - `scripts/chat-shard-durations.json` (new): recorded time per collected chat entry, from the CI profile records of PR run [43444854171](https://github.com/paperclipai/paperclip/actions/runs/43444854171). 926 of 928 collected entries are measured; the other two are the bogus collection entries that the launcher already drops.
  > - `scripts/ci/refresh-chat-shard-durations.mjs` (new): builds that manifest from downloaded `ci-profile-*` artifacts. Each executed case maps to the collected `it`/`it.each` entry that registers it. A case without an entry fails the refresh.
- perf(runner): serialise only changed journal records for a checkpoint (#21437)
  > - Add `CheckpointRecordCache` to `durable-prp-control-plane.ts`. It keeps the JSON and the part name of each command and event record, keyed by the record object. It uses the JSON again only when the record has the same keys, in the same order, with the same values (`===`). Commands change some fields in place (`status`, `result`, `controllerSeq`), and this comparison finds these changes.
  > - `captureCheckpoint` accepts the cache as an optional fourth argument. Only `setCheckpointPersistence` and `flushCheckpoint` pass it. All other callers (decode, legacy import, bootstrap inspection) do the full conversion as before.
  > - `checkpointChanges` uses loops instead of array copies. Records that did not change keep the same string, so the comparison is by reference.
- perf(apps): avoid repeated writes during permission saves (#21033)
  > - Keep action entries and access bindings when their saved values match.
  > - Insert new bindings in one query. Remove only bindings and entries that no longer apply.
  > - Read managed approval rules for the selected app. Skip unchanged rules and rules that are already disabled.
- refactor(evals): remove the orphaned chat TestOps dispatch guard (#21241)
  > - Deleted `tests/runner-e2e/testops-chat-dispatch.ts` (31 lines of test support code). No production code changed.
- test(runner): run Kimi layout suites once in prep (#20487)
  > - Removed the four Kimi suite imports from `packages/paperclip-runner/scripts/build-verified-provider-entrypoints.test.mjs` (`materialize-kimi-distribution`, `provision-kimi-package`, `patch-kimi-runtime`, `kimi-metadata-probe`).
  > - Added a comment that says the Kimi suites are selected directly by `test:typescript:prep`, so this aggregator must not import them again.
  > - No changes to `package.json`, routing, or the suites themselves.
- refactor(slack): correct stale status comment and add production acceptance checklist (#20840)
  > - Added `doc/plans/2026-11-11-slack-production-acceptance.md`. It has sections for self-hosted automatic setup, manual and existing apps, conversation behavior, Cloud managed setup on staging, and distribution questions (Marketplace-only workspaces, non-Marketplace rate limits, scope justification, zero-copy).
  > - Corrected the comment in `server/src/services/chat-channels.ts`. It now says that Slack shows the working state through `agents.sessions.setStatus` and the lifecycle reply. No code behavior changed.
- perf(ui): keep the live socket when a cached session is verified (#20591)
  > - `ui/src/context/LiveUpdatesProvider.tsx`: the socket auth key comes from `nextSocketAuthScope`. When the previous scope has the same user and no session id, the first known session id is adopted without a new key. A different user, or a different session id once one is known, still produces a new key and a new connection.
  > - `ui/src/context/LiveUpdatesProvider.recovery.test.tsx`: two tests. A restored session (no id) verified for the same user keeps the socket and invalidates nothing; a later replacement session still reconnects. A verified session for another user reconnects.
  > - `tests/perf/collections/shell-read-waves.spec.ts` and `returning-visit-fixture.ts`: browser checks on a production build with every API response delayed 300 ms. With a saved cache, each of the eight reads runs at most once and the socket opens once. Without a saved cache, session and health run once, no read runs more than twice, and the socket opens once.
- perf(db): send a transaction's work notifications in one statement at commit (#21436)
  > - `packages/db/src/work-signals.ts`: a signal records `(channel, payload)` on the current transaction level. A released savepoint merges its records into its parent; a rolled-back savepoint drops them. The outermost transaction sends all distinct payloads in one statement (`select pg_notify(channel, payload) from jsonb_array_elements_text($2::jsonb)`) after its callback returns and before commit. A transaction that signals nothing sends no extra statement.
  > - The record lives on the transaction object only. A retried callback gets a new transaction object and starts empty.
  > - `publishDatabaseWorkHint` (outside a transaction) is unchanged and still sends at once.
- perf(auth): authenticate a run's agent JWT in three statements (#21434)
  > - **Skip key lookups for a verified run JWT.** The middleware verifies the JWT first (HMAC only, no database). Every stored board key and agent key is `pcp_` followed by letters, `_` and hex (`createBoardApiToken`, `createToken`). A verified JWT has three parts with dots. So its SHA-256 cannot equal a stored key hash, and both lookups always returned no row for it. A token that does not verify still tries the board key, then the agent key, in the same order as before.
  > - **Read only the agent columns that authentication checks.** `companyId`, `status` and `lifecycleHolds` replace `select *`. The agent configuration JSON stays in the database. The agent-key path uses the same columns.
  > - **Read revocation and identity in one statement.** `loadAgentRunAuthentication` now also returns the run task ids, a `pendingIdentityContext` flag (`exists` on a pending context of this run), and the active context `id` and `responsibleUserId` (only when that context belongs to this run). The new `resolveRequestRunIdentity` in `run-identity.ts` uses these columns:
- test(native-runtime): cover released session tool authority and add a catalog memory benchmark (#21440)
  > - Test "refuses a released session's late tool call and completion feedback and admits the next run with only its own assignments".
  > - Test "releases a retired warm predecessor's tool authority when the follow-up fails before admitting its own". A follow-up of the same warm scope with changed configuration retires the predecessor. Then its assigned tool listing fails. The test asserts that the predecessor was closed, that the follow-up failed with that error, and that `sessionToolAuthorityCount()` is back at its starting value.
  > - `server/scripts/benchmark-assigned-mcp-tools-memory.ts`: retained bytes of one assigned tool catalog per session, and schema copies per `definitions()` call.
- perf(ci): run the typecheck lane's checks in parallel and drop its duplicate server type check (#21404)
  > - `scripts/ci-typecheck-lane.mjs` (new): runs the job's checks. It writes shared outputs first, in sequence: links, plugin SDK, server build without type checks, the runtime asset check, the sandbox provider installs, and the Runner typecheck (it rebuilds the eval kernel, which other checks read). Then it runs these checks in parallel: the server declaration layers, each server test layer, each test project, the test programs, the other build-gap workspaces, the CLI guidance test and the release registry tests. A passing check prints its output in a collapsed log group. A failing check prints its full output between `=== FAILED` and `=== end of` lines, with an error annotation. The script ends with a table of each check's start, duration and result, and exits non-zero if any check fails.
  > - `server/scripts/typecheck-layers.mjs`: new `--layer <name>` option that checks one layer against the configs and declarations of an earlier `--declarations-only` run, and an exported `listLayers`.
  > - `scripts/typecheck-test-programs.mjs`: new `--prepare-only` and `--skip-prepare` options, so the installs happen before the parallel checks start.
- perf(ci): cut repeated CPU work in the Runner support suite (#21390)
  > - One Vitest invocation (693 files, 3 workers, the Vitest default of CPUs − 1), then 386 `node:test` tests in about 3 s. About 45–50 s of the job is checkout and dependency restore before the test step.
  > - Summed tracked time was about 1,297 s for 470 s of wall time: effective concurrency 2.76 of 3 workers. Module evaluation was 674 s: 4,164 modules evaluated 77,699 times, because each file runs in a fresh isolated worker.
  > - A CPU sampler (temporary, removed) showed 82% average CPU use with 3 workers and 95–99% with 4 workers. With 4 workers and no other change the wall time did not improve (455 s). The suite was CPU-bound, not idle.
- test(evals): keep the spend fixture's pointer to the tasks that record the charges (#21429)
  > - The setup description now says "Launch's API charges this week are recorded on tasks COM-3 (Morgan) and COM-4 (Riley)."
- perf(ui): read the compact task index on the dashboard and refresh it once per window (#21413)
  > - The dashboard, the activity feed and board chat read `view=index` under a new key `issues.listIndex(companyId)`.
  > - Task activity from other people refreshes that key at most once per 30 seconds (`TASK_INDEX_REFRESH_WINDOW_MS`). The viewer's own task activity refreshes it at once. The first event after a quiet window refetches at once. Later events in the window share one trailing refetch. Nothing from the event goes into the cache. An unwatched index is marked stale and refetches on the next mount. The user's own task changes still invalidate the whole `["issues", companyId]` prefix immediately.
  > - The dashboard takes its ten recent tasks from the index, sorted by updatedAt as before. It reads the status glyph fields for those ten tasks from `issues/summaries`, because the index omits displayStatus and blockerAttention.
- refactor(docker): take the application image's Runner from the runner-artifacts stage (#21332)
  > - `Dockerfile`: remove the five stages. `build` starts from `base`. It copies `/runner-package/dist` and the build stamp from `runner-artifacts`, sets `PAPERCLIP_RUNNER_VENDOR_REQUIRE_CURRENT=1` and runs the vendor check before the first application build.
  > - `Dockerfile`: `runner-provider-pack` starts from `base`. One `RUN` with a bind mount of `runner-artifacts` copies the pack, the receipt and the archives, and moves the large files to their own tree. `build` and `runner-provider-pack` now come after the stages they read.
  > - `Dockerfile`: the three `COPY` instructions of `production` that take the pack are byte-identical. A test pins all three.
- chore(lockfile): refresh pnpm lockfiles (#21439)
  > - Refresh the dependency lock from the manifests on master.
  > - Refresh the sandbox provider lockfiles from their manifests on master.
  > - Include the matching offline report correction and hashes.
- perf(runs): read live runs in a fixed number of statements (#21417)
  > - `server/src/routes/agents.ts`: the live-runs handler decides reads with the existing `getHeartbeatRunReadDecisions` (the run history list already uses it) and builds silence summaries with one `buildRunOutputSilences` call for the readable runs. Unreadable runs are still redacted with `redactHeartbeatRunListRow`, and no silence state is read for them.
  > - Active-run watchdog Postgres adapter: new `findLatestDecisions` and `findOpenStaleRunEvaluations` read all runs of one company in one statement each (`DISTINCT ON`). The single-run `findLatestDecision` and `findOpenStaleRunEvaluation` now call the batch with one run, so both paths use the same query.
  > - Watchdog application: new `createBuildRunOutputSilences` use case groups runs by company. `createBuildRunOutputSilence` now calls it with one run, so the single and batch summaries use one rule.
- perf(runner): keep at most one checkpoint waiting behind a slow write (#21411)
  > - `DurablePrpControlPlane.flushCheckpoint` keeps one snapshot that waits for the write in progress. A newer flush for the same run replaces that snapshot and returns the same promise. A flush for a different run (a warm handoff) still queues its own write.
  > - New tests in `durable-prp-control-plane.test.ts`:
  > - New `scripts/benchmark-runner-checkpoint-backlog.ts` measures retained heap per run, checkpoint writes, and whether finished control planes are collected.
- chore(ci): remove the Docker dependency cache check of the Runner stages (#21331)
  > - Delete `scripts/check-docker-runner-cache.sh`.
  > - Remove the `runner` job and the `pull_request` trigger from `docker-runner-check.yml`. Only the manual jobs remain. The concurrency group no longer reads a pull request number.
  > - Remove `Docker Runner check` from the workflow list of `pr-merge-review.yml`. That notifier acts only on pull-request runs, and the workflow has none now.

Plan subscription fees are separate from API charges." The same facts, scope and exclusions stay. The cost events and the graders do not change.

  > - The test provider mock now counts the calls that start. It resolves a promise when the second call starts.

  > - The `afterEach` awaits `heartbeatService(db).drainActiveRunExecutions()` before the truncate. The registry of in-flight runs is shared by all service instances.
  > - The `afterEach` truncates through the existing `truncateTablesWithDeadlockRetry` helper.
  > - No product code changed.

  > - `tests/e2e/legacy-failure-continuation.spec.ts`: the source run must stay `failed` and must not be replayed. Its `resultJson` may be `null`, or may carry only the superseded stamp. Any other content still fails the spec.
  > - `freshContinuationSupersededAt` has one effect: it revokes the old run's write authority (`agent-run-cancellation.ts`, `middleware/auth.ts`). The old run is already dead (`process_lost`), so nothing changes.
  > - In the wake queue, the stamp only skips the native finalization gate and the reconciliation incident, and this run is legacy. No UI code reads it, so it adds no banner and no Retry.

The saved secret must now have provider `local_encrypted`. The personal step binds the form by the heading "Set your value for Personal publishing credential". Credential handling is unchanged. `manual-private-*.ts` is unchanged.
  > - `external-company-secret-create-dialog.test.ts`: the canary page uses the new dialog. It adds a several-vaults variant (valid), a wrong-storage result and the retired "Create secret" form (both rejected). Source anchors follow `Secrets.tsx`.

- test(restart-chaos): staging twin cells, follow-up/queue/subtask grading, and full Daytona cleanup (#21325)
  > - Cherry-picked the 8 twin harness commits from `fastloop/staging-twin-loaded`: staging-mix under the release-swap driver on Daytona, `--server-from` on Daytona, probe agents verified while the first tasks run, image matched to the `--server-from` checkout, and the knobs `RESTART_CHAOS_K15_KILL_OLD_AFTER_SECONDS` / `_KILL_OLD_SWAPS`, `RESTART_CHAOS_K15_FREEZE_NEW_SECONDS` / `_FREEZE_SWAPS`, `RESTART_CHAOS_MIX_BANNER_REST_SECONDS` and `RESTART_CHAOS_MIX_FIRST_SLOT_SECONDS`. A misplaced doc block in `supervisor.ts` is moved back to its function. No code comments are added.
  > - Cleanup (`daytona-sweep.ts`): after the environment delete, the harness deletes every sandbox that carries both of this wave's UUID labels (company and environment), archived ones included. `assertHarnessMayMutate` still refuses any sandbox without both labels and any `fleet-sbx-*` snapshot. The `paperclip-create-*` name refusal is removed, because every plugin sandbox (the fleet's included) has that name, so the labels are the real ownership proof. A sandbox left after deletion fails the wave (`undeletedSandboxes`, warm-shutdown verdict, `ttft-baseline`).
  > - Follow-ups (`follow-up-delivery.ts`, `wake-evidence.ts`, `staging-mix-retry.ts`, `staging-mix.ts`): delivery comes from the server's own record. A comment that the server judged delivered never becomes a run. A run that the server started from a wake carrying a person's comment posted after the first run started (a retry included) is required, not an extra turn. `completion_once` keeps the first completion as the baseline and does not count a later completion by such a run (truth-table test). The grader does not re-check the server's delivery rule; the server's tests cover it. The summary adds `extra turns: N` and `follow-up answered in a later run: n, queued wait p50/max, posted to answered p50/max`.

- perf(native-runtime): start the first turn without re-copying launch files, and never fail a run on a slow copy (#21287)
  > - `retainReadyNativeProviderArtifacts` (`native-provider-artifacts.ts`): reuse a ready, verified company artifact with the same paths and SHA-256 digests through a lookup only. No file is copied, uploaded or verified again. The lookup (`readyNativeProviderArtifactCandidates`) matches target, paths and digests in Postgres with jsonb containment on the company's ready rows (existing partial index), newest first, limit 4. It does not load or hash other retained versions. No migration.
  > - `shareNativeProviderArtifactCapture` (`native-provider-artifact-capture.ts`): one in-flight capture per company and manifest digest per server. Other runs wait for it, then reuse its result.
  > - The single 300 s clock over the whole capture is replaced by a bound for each network step (upload, verifying download): max(120 s, size at 256 KiB/s). A timeout has the typed cause `native_provider_artifacts_transfer_timeout`. The capture makes up to 3 attempts from the local copy.

  > - The Linux real-process cases use an inspector that wraps the real Linux inspector. For a hidden owner, it keeps only the candidate processes that descend from the process that the case started. All other parts are the real Linux inspector: the TCP table reads, the fd scan, the recheck, and the ancestry reads.
  > - Each Linux block starts an idle, uninspectable process with `PAPERCLIP_RUN_ID`. This process stands for another agent run on the host.
  > - A new case, "refuses board access to an operator process the server cannot inspect while another agent run's process it cannot inspect is running", uses the unwrapped inspector. It pins the host-wide rule in a real-process test.

The spy is restored right after the routes are built. The same pattern is used in `heartbeat-native-restart-suspension.test.ts`.

  > - `release-swap-grade.ts`: shared `drainState` (quiet only when nothing is progressing and no run was created or finished for 90 s, `QUIET_PERIOD_MS`); `failedAtSwap` / `swapForFailedRun` / `failedOutsideSwaps` (a failed run counts at a swap if it was live at the outgoing SIGTERM or failed within 15 minutes of it, `RELEASE_FAILURE_WINDOW_MS`, and at most one swap); `isAutoNotice`; the summary lists failed runs outside any release window and the notice repeats as information.

  > - Create one fixture certificate in `beforeAll`. Set the TLS trust and `https.globalAgent` once for the file, and restore them in `afterAll`.

The loop executes it. The lines inside the two SQL fragments keep their indentation, so the SQL text is byte-identical. The pull request adds no comment. The one existing comment of this read stays at the call site, where the code still makes that choice.

  > - Create one fixture certificate in `beforeAll`. Set the TLS trust and `https.globalAgent` once for the file, and restore them in `afterAll`. A case no longer changes process-wide trust.

- test(restart-chaos): add hosted mode for the release-swap proof on a real staging instance (#21153)
  > - `hosted-guard.ts`: refuses to start unless `--hosted-base-url` is a bare `https://` origin and `--hosted-allow` names the same host. Only one stack under `.staging.paperclip.app` or `.test.paperclip.app` is accepted. The shared staging instance, production, the fleet admin and Cloud hosts are always refused. Every request is checked against that origin before it is sent.

It reports each release's overlap window (two process identities answering), failed and slow requests, time to the first answer from the new build, its shape (`overlap`, `stop_then_start`, `clean_switch`) and probe continuity (requests, failures, longest gap).
- Persist portable Runner ownership through controller recovery (#21066)
  > - Add the optional `sessionHandoffVersion` capability and the ownership task operation.
  > - Save matching ownership claims and fence source checkpoint access. Replay a pending request on the original target.
  > - Restore a portable session using the durable claim and the current credentials for the same account. Start a fresh conversation when the provider reports an unavailable checkpoint, or when Core's persisted compatibility decision selects a different session after transfer. The latter requires an unlaunched target; subsequent recovery must match the fresh task's saved identity. Both paths retain the workspace transfer and source fencing.
- perf(attention): count the sidebar summary without the reads that only a card shows (#21159)
  > - The blocker attention of every blocked task (`readIssueBlockerAttentionBatch`). A blocker card exists only when the tree behind a task is stalled.
  > - The review path classification of every review (`listIssueReviewAttentionMap`). A review with no path is a card, and `stalled` is part of the content a dismissal covers.
  > - At 9,000 tasks these two are 43 of the 89 statements, 3,600 of the 8,400 rows and 154 of the 254 KB of SQL text. Their related-row reads use `IN` lists of up to 900 ids, some in batches of 500. That is the largest cost that remains, and the task list and the feed have it too.
- test(restart-chaos): add the release swap cell RS1 with user-view grading (#21126)
  > - `release-swap-schedule.ts`: the `--release-swap` options and the `--release-swap-short` preset (3 swaps, 12 min apart, 510 s overlap) (count 6, spacing 40 min, overlap 510 s, settle 15 min), their expansion into a K15 overlap storm (adds `--k15-fresh-home --ephemeral-server` on Daytona), the swap plan and the `ReleaseSwapRun` clock (swap k at k × spacing, never while a swap is unsettled; tasks launch until the last swap settled).
  > - `release-swap-driver.ts`: the per-swap driver. It creates probe tasks through the outgoing server's own port when the incoming server spawns, when it enters startup recovery, and so that a run starts about 20 s before the outgoing server's SIGTERM. It samples runs and their owning controller PID every 2 s and records which server started each run. It has a per-swap plug-in list; a plug-in can choose the checkout the incoming server runs (for the later mixed-version variant).
  > - `release-swap-grade.ts`: the pure grade. One verdict per task (`pass`, `needed_retry` with whether the click worked, `open_no_owner`, `blocked_non_person`, `waiting_on_person`, `still_working`), the per-swap numbers (failed runs, runs lost while the old server was alive, time from SIGTERM to each surviving run's next event), duplicate runs and duplicated side effects, the one-number summary, and one failure label per failing task (`nothing pending`, `blocked for a non-person reason`, `needed Retry` with the click result).
- test(e2e): stop Cloud auth health stub from reading a disposed response (#21150)
  > - `tests/e2e/cloud-auth.spec.ts`: get `/api/health` one time through the test `request` fixture, add the Cloud overrides, and fulfill intercepted health requests from that snapshot. The route handler no longer calls `route.fetch()`.
  > - The snapshot also sets `status: "ok"`. A snapshot taken during startup recovery reports `starting`, which would keep the Cloud gate on the starting screen for the whole test.
- test(heartbeat): check preparation span parents only in the attempt trace (#21149)
  > - The test now finds the single `task.execution_attempt` span first. Then it reads only the spans in that span's trace.
  > - The name checks and the parent checks use only those spans. The `native.controller.*` spans are in the same trace, so their checks do not change.
  > - No product code changes. The replay of recorded phases under `task.prepare` is intended behavior (`drainRunPreparationPhases` in `heartbeat/runtime-execution.ts` feeds the native run trace).
- test(restart-chaos): add the staging-mix workload for release-swap waves (#21125)
  > - `staging-mix.ts`: the options (`--workload staging-mix`, `--mix-*`), the seeded plan (interleaved adapter ratio, arrivals over the whole wave, one fault slot per `--mix-fault-spacing-minutes`), the SM task (rounds of a hashing shell command, subtasks, `DONE-<nonce>` comment) and its oracle.
  > - The oracle recomputes each round's chained POSIX `cksum` hash. It fails a missing, short or wrong round, a repeated round, comment or subtask (duplicated side effects), a follow-up that did not reach the ledger, and a parent that did not read its subtasks' results.
  > - The follow-up and Retry step waits for an overlap window, for at most `--mix-hold-minutes`. A swap driver registers the window with `setOverlapProbe`. Until then, the window is a fault in progress while the server still answers (K15 overlap). Retries go through the observer's existing click path, so R3 still counts them.
- perf(server): stop re-reading and re-decrypting unchanged checkpoint headers on every upgrade readiness probe (#21130)
  > - `native-runner-checkpoint.ts` (the owner of header inspection) adds a header read contract and a bounded memo.
  > - `upgrade-readiness.ts` and `native-controller-recovery-inventory.ts` use that contract. The run conditions, the 1 MiB and 8 MiB inspection bounds and the row limits are the same SQL as before. The queries now return material only for rows that the code inspects. One memo exists per database handle.
  > - `inspectNativeRunnerUpgradeHeader` keeps its contract for its other caller.
- perf(attention): index the agents and review paths once for every review of a request (#21132)
  > - `server/src/services/recovery/issue-graph-liveness.ts`
  > - `server/src/services/issues.ts` (`listIssueReviewAttentionMap` only): one classifier for all reviews of the request, a set for the review ids, and the review by position instead of a search. No query changes.
  > - Tests and the reproduction (see Verification):
- Configure managed plugin Runner lifecycle (#21065)
  > - Share agent environment resolution between required plugin selection and the lifecycle RPC.
  > - Pass `environmentDriverKey` to the plugin that serves the selected environment.
  > - Allow managed plugin environments to set `warm` or `per_turn` and persist the selected mode.
- perf(storage): hash and encrypt workspace snapshots off the server thread (#21138)
  > - `server/src/storage/file-crypto-pool.ts` (new): runs file digests and AES-256-GCM passes on worker threads.
  > - Save (`uploadWorkspaceSnapshot`): the one read that digests the archive, encrypts it and digests the stored bytes runs in a worker. The identity check around that read (`archive_changed`) stays on this thread and is unchanged.
  > - Part upload (`fileRangeMd5`): the `Content-MD5` of each part is computed in a worker. The first round of parts is digested before any part is sent, so the parts still leave together and a rate limit still answers one burst (the existing throttle test covers this). Every digest of that round has ended, also after a failure or a cancellation, before the upload settles.
- perf(run-events): append a native event in one transaction of two statements (#21131)
  > - `server/src/services/heartbeat-run-events.ts`
  > - `server/src/services/native-runtime/paperclip-control-plane-port.ts`: `appendEvent` passes `assertWritable` instead of opening an outer transaction. The check runs at the same two points as before.
  > - Tests: see Verification.
- perf(server): start no git process for server info outside a checkout (#21122)
  > - `server/src/server-info.ts`: the default `git show` command first looks for a checkout. A checkout is a `.git` entry in the working directory or in one of its parents, or a `GIT_DIR` value. Without one, the command throws before it starts a process, and `readGitInfo` takes its existing build-commit path. The same check applies when `/api/liveness` reads the snapshot at route creation.
  > - The 3 second refresh, the checkout path, the injected-command path for tests and `processStartedAt` are not changed.
  > - `server/src/__tests__/health-server-info-git-probe.test.ts` (new): regression tests that count the `git` processes that the server starts. The tests use real Git and real directories.
- perf(server): reach readiness without waiting for backlog-sized recovery passes (#21014)
  > - **Volume-free data gate runs first.** `assertEphemeralServerData` now runs right after `claimVolumeFreeControllerState` and the runtime config load. That is before `createApp`, before the listener binds, and before any queued-run dispatch. It used to run at the end of startup. The package qualification gate stays at the end, because bundled plugin installs can change its inputs.
  > - **UI asset publication is faster but still a gate before work.** `createApp` starts the publication while it builds its routes. It awaits the result before its first job, queue or run starts, which is the same point as on master. A colliding build still refuses to start before it admits any work (Greptile review).
  > - **Publication sends Buffer bodies, 8 at a time.** Each asset is read once into a Buffer. The S3 client can replay a Buffer body; it cannot replay a stream. The new code sends no stream bodies, so it no longer reaches the SDK path that logs "non-retryable streaming request". After the first failure, no new assets start. A collision found on one asset outranks a storage outage on another (Greptile review).
- perf(ci): rebalance server test shards and repair the weekly weight refresh (#21016)
  > - `scripts/ci/gh-api.mjs`: job-log downloads get a 15 min timeout and a 512 MB buffer. JSON calls keep 120 s. A failed call now gives a short error and does not print the partial 60 MB log.
  > - `scripts/ci/pr-run-durations.mjs`: new shared `assertManifestSource`. It accepts the optional leading `pull` key that `collectMergedPrSamples` writes. The checked-in Runner manifest test and the collector test both use it, so the collector format stays covered.
  > - `.github/workflows/ci-test-durations.yml`: refresh job timeout 20 -> 45 min (the server lanes alone fetch about 30 large logs).
- perf(docker): take the orphan-reaping pull and pack chmod off the image path (#21013)
  > - `docker.yml` `build-and-push`: new step "Verify PID 1 reaps orphaned processes". It runs `scripts/assert-orphan-reaping.sh` in `ghcr.io/...@<pushed digest>` with `--platform` set to the leg platform. It runs before "Export digest", so `merge-and-push` only receives probed digests.
  > - `docker.yml` `merge-and-push`: removed the two `docker run` probes (tag and digest). "Resolve standard image digest" now runs `standard-image-contract.mjs --resolve "$GITHUB_SHA" --verified-digests /tmp/digests`.
  > - `standard-image-contract.mjs`: `--verified-digests <dir>` reads each probed leg digest from the registry, checks that its bytes hash to that digest, and collects its Linux image digests. It fails unless every Linux image in the standard index is in that set. Registry content is addressed by digest, so this proves that the signed index names exactly the probed bytes, without a second pull.
- test(chaos): W2C changes the agent configuration the way the settings form does (#21011)
  > - `tests/restart-chaos/rewake.ts`: W2C reads the agent and PATCHes `{ adapterConfig: { ...existing, idleTimeoutMs }, replaceAdapterConfig: true }`.
  > - `tests/restart-chaos/shape-cells.test.ts`: the W2C step test expects the full-config patch.
  > - `tests/restart-chaos/README.md`: W2C describes the patch it sends.
- test(native): cover a turn.start timeout that loses the remote Runner, end to end (#21041)
  > - `packages/paperclip-runner/src/live/runnerd-turn-start-timeout-retention.test.ts` (new): runs `executeNativeSession` over a real `paperclip-runnerd` with a launcher-owned (remote-shaped) process. The fake Codex never answers `turn/start`, so the `turn.start` PRP command times out. The test checks that the run fails with that exact error, that the Runner process is still alive after the close, and that its journal is `ready` for the same-run reattachment.
  > - `server/src/services/runner-lost-mid-turn-continuation.test.ts`: a new case with the staging failure record. A person's Retry (`task_retry`, a user intent with a non-hook key, `forceFreshSession`) has only `turn.submitted`. Its refusal has `runnerSettled: false` and `providerTurnIssued: false`. The completion hook requests one automatic Retry. The test checks one unknown-outcome event with `replayed: false`, an idempotent second call, saved-work recovery first, and one queued fresh-session successor. The fixture gets small optional inputs for the wake, the events, and the refusal payload. Other cases are unchanged.
  > - No production code changes.
- test(evals): check the wrong-resolver reply links Alex's request and task-link labels match their targets (#21028)
  > - `external-wrong-resolver.ts`: new check `reply-links-alex-pending-request`. The reply must render a same-origin link to the related task that holds Alex's card (`/<company>/issues/<id or identifier>`). `verifyResult` now follows that rendered link (not a constructed URL) and confirms the "Waiting for Alex" card. Setup records the company prefix. Oracle `paperclip.chat-catalog.external.v2` -> `v3`. Reply wording is still not graded.
  > - `chat-rendered-links.ts`: new shared helper `mislabeledIssueLinks(text, identifierFor?)`. It returns inline Markdown links whose label names a task identifier (same prefix as the target) other than the task the link opens. Labels without such a token, and non-task targets, are not judged. Task-id targets are compared through an optional resolver.
  > - `identity-navigation-preferences.ts`: find-project-view adds `task-links-match-their-labels` with that helper. Oracle `paperclip.chat-catalog.identity.v1` -> `paperclip.chat-catalog.identity.find-project-view.v2`; `chat-rendered-links.ts` joins its source files.
- Reuse retained Runner descriptions during startup (#21004)
  > - Extend native launch capability deferral to retained tasks. Keep managed override checks in their original location.
  > - Reuse descriptions during recovery and fallback only if the physical task matches.
  > - Use the normal status operation to record the initial stopped result before completing and replacing a task.
- test(restart-chaos): add CR6, a plugin stalled during activation at boot (#20983)
  > - `CR6`: a K1 restart (SIGKILL, no hold). The replacement's Daytona plugin worker is stopped for the `--cr3-stall-seconds` stall as soon as it forks. It is found within 60 s, while the server activates the plugin.
  > - `withReplacementPluginStall` in `class-r.ts` runs the CR4 stall (after activation) and the CR6 stall (at fork) beside the replacement's health wait. `supervisor.ts` stays at 1,000 lines.
  > - CR6 joins the plugin-stall faults: the stall option, `class_r_fault_applied` and the README table.
- test(chaos): fail the directory-lock crash point with the lock's own busy error (#20988)
  > - New test `server/src/__tests__/agent-directory-lock-crash-point.test.ts`.
  > - `server/src/services/agent-directory-working-copies.ts`: the crash point's `fail` returns `new AgentDirectoryOperationBusyError()` (same 409 status and message) instead of a plain `conflict()`.
- Refine agent workspaces and organization charts (#20973)
  > - Refine agent headers, matching Chat and New task buttons, suggested tasks, and Explore skills links.
  > - Create identity files through an editable preview title. Add live renaming, selected rows, required-file feedback, and clear save states. Guard managed file renames and active root changes under the file-store transaction. Use the lifecycle configuration integration for the root change so lifecycle invalidation and configuration history remain owned by that module; register the file store as an explicit transactional workflow caller.
  > - Anchor the save tray to the viewport bottom. Show it only for changed values and retain saved feedback before dismissal.
- Reuse Runner plugin capability checks (#20987)
  > - Defer the early managed project auth capability query for new plugin tasks to native Runner setup.
  > - Pass the broker capability from the Runner description to the task owner. Reject a missing capability before sending the token.
  > - Cover new and retained tasks and verify that broker preparation works without a second description query.
- test(restart-chaos): stall the plugin during reattachment (CR4) and at launch (CR5) (#20966)
  > - `CR4`: a K1 restart (SIGKILL, no outage hold). When the replacement is healthy, the harness finds its Daytona plugin worker (it waits up to 60 seconds for the worker to fork) and stops it for the stall.
  > - `CR5`: the CR3 stall, fired 3–8 seconds after the task is assigned (`CR5_FIRE_WINDOW_MS`), so that it covers the run's launch. T1's `fault_crossed_tool` check does not apply to CR5.
  > - `--cr3-stall-seconds` sets the stall for CR3, CR4 and CR5. `class_r_fault_applied` requires that each of their stalls ended.
- Add turn-scoped attachment downloads to native Runner (#20956)
  > - Add the PRP v3 `runner.attachment.download` command for native workspace providers.
  > - Issue short-lived grants for exact authorized wake and historical attachments. Recheck the active run, source, size, and SHA-256 at the tenant endpoint.
  > - Download current-wake inputs before provider dispatch. Download historical files when the authorized chat reader asks for them. Preserve SSH and sandbox command staging as a fallback.
- test(chaos): run waves in Cloud's volume-free server mode; make W2 phase 1 yield (#20930)
  > - `assertIsolatedServerEnvironment` (`tests/runner-e2e/harness-env.ts`) takes `expected.generatedSecretsMasterKey`. It admits `PAPERCLIP_SECRETS_MASTER_KEY` only when that parameter is set and equals the value exactly. `PAPERCLIP_SECRETS_MASTER_KEY_FILE` stays refused. The check runs over the final server environment.
  > - `--k15-fresh-home` implies `--ephemeral-server` and defaults `--storage` to `s3`. A fresh instance home without the volume-free mode loses the agents' instruction bundles, which staging keeps in Postgres and object storage.
  > - `--ephemeral-server` (`config.ts`, `types.ts`, `main.ts`, `supervisor.ts`, new `ephemeral-server.ts` with its unit test `ephemeral-server.test.ts`): it needs `--storage s3` and `--env daytona` and refuses K14. Every server of the wave gets `PAPERCLIP_EPHEMERAL_SERVER`, `PAPERCLIP_DURABLE_AGENT_FILES`, `PAPERCLIP_SHARED_UI_ASSETS`, the wave's Postgres as `PAPERCLIP_DATABASE_LISTEN_URL`, one stable `PAPERCLIP_SECRETS_MASTER_KEY` (also written to the config key file) and `RUN_LOG_S3_*` in the wave's object store.
- Fix image attachments in remote native workspaces (#20939)
  > - Add a remote staging owner with bounded commands, stdin transfer, hash verification, opaque paths, and safe image extensions.
  > - Confine Linux filesystem operations through directory descriptors. Refuse symlinks and replacement files during cleanup.
  > - Bind current wake files and authorized historical external-chat reads to the selected lease transport.
- test(restart-chaos): staging class R cells CR1-CR3 and sandbox capture on every Daytona wave (#20909)
  > - `class-r.ts` (new) adds three faults, selected with `--faults`:
  > - `daytona-runner-logs.ts` adds `CLASS_R_PROBE`. It reads process start times and `wchan`, sockets, the runtime file listing, and the tails of the Runner's and the provider's state and log files. It also adds `executeInWaveSandboxes`, the fixed-script runner that CR2 uses.
  > - `main.ts` and `supervisor.ts`:
- refactor(runner): rename codex-runtime-adapter to acpx-runtime-adapter (#20938)
  > - Rename `src/drivers/acpx/codex-runtime-adapter.ts` to `src/drivers/acpx/acpx-runtime-adapter.ts`, and its test file in the same way. Git records both as renames with no content change, except the test file's import path.
  > - Update the imports in `acpx-runtime-sidecar.ts`, `codex-acpx-driver.ts` and four test files.
  > - Update the `dist` import in `scripts/grok-native-smoke.mjs` and the test file key in `scripts/vitest-lane-durations.json`.
- test(evals): retarget instance-settings from the removed Agent Chat toggle to the Cases experiment (#20897)
  > - Premise change (explicit): query "Enable Agent Chat on this instance." becomes "Turn on Cases for this instance."; context and expected outcome follow. Recorded in the manifest `changedFields`, pinned in the manifest test, inventory digest updated.
  > - Setup: refuses a hidden Cases setting, starts Cases off (cleanup restores the target's value), and probes GET/PATCH experimental settings with the agent's own key (403 `board_access_required`, setting unchanged).
  > - Human journey: checks that Cases is still off after the agent's turn and that `/<prefix>/cases` redirects to the dashboard. Then follows the Experimental settings link that the reply rendered (no typed-URL fallback), turns Cases on, checks it after reload, and opens the Cases page.
- Select the AI Connection sign-in computer automatically (#20906)
  > - Select an eligible computer when a managed Codex or Claude login starts without an explicit target. Prefer ready computers and skip active login leases.
  > - Remove the picker and let the browser resume the existing session by environment and connection intent. Continue to honor explicit agent targets in agent settings. Remove the unused picker fallback from the legacy form.
  > - Update the AI Connections guide, the plugin environment contract, and the OpenAPI descriptions.
- test(evals): make scripted chat input name its referent (accept-one-proposal, share-private-task) (#20896)
  > - accept-one-proposal: the second human message names the accepted card by its visible title (`Yes, go ahead with the "Create Acorn launch checklist" request.`). The grader checks this exact scripted message. All other checks stay: one task, still in backlog, one decision bound to the first reply, two runs. Oracle version is now `paperclip.chat-catalog.accept-one-proposal.v2`.
  > - share-private-task: the query is now `Share my private task "Private launch task" with Riley.` The change is recorded in the manifest `changedFields`, pinned in its test, and the inventory digest is updated.
  > - share-private-task: the reply keyword check is removed. The grader requires a rendered link to the named private task. `verifyResult` has the owner open that link and open the task's Share control ("Who can access this task"). Then it confirms that Riley is still denied. The audience check also rejects a grant to any person who is not the owner. Oracle version is now `paperclip.chat-catalog.share-private-task.v2`.
- test(restart-chaos): cells for staging's no-restart failures (re-wake, Nth recovery step, long turn, K15 without volume) (#20893)
  > - New workloads W2, W2Q, W2C (`rewake.ts`): phase 1 ends with the task left open; the harness, as the board, wakes the same task the seeded 0–60 s after its run settled. W2 uses a comment or a board-scheduled issue monitor (by nonce). W2Q parks on an unanswered `request_human_input` question and is woken by a comment before the answer. W2C changes the agent's `adapterConfig.idleTimeoutMs` before the wake. Oracle: phase 1 succeeded, the wake was served by a run that succeeded, `no_failed_attempt`, `done`, markers.
  > - `--faults K0`: cells with no fault, for workloads whose shape is the fault.
  > - `--fail-recovery-step <step>[:<nth>]` (`recovery-step-faults.ts`): when the wave's first restart fault starts its replacement, the harness arms one crash point in a new fail mode; the nth hit throws the error of the real step, once. Steps: `runnerd-start` (`spawn paperclip-runnerd EAGAIN`), `teardown` (provider destroy did not finish), `directory-lock` (`Agent directory operation is already active`). Crossed tasks get `recovery_step_failed_once` and `no_failed_attempt`.
- Poll asynchronous workspace provider preparation (#20865)
  > - Add an optional workspace preparation method to the plugin SDK.
  > - Validate pending receipts and keep the original binding checks on completed replies.
  > - Poll only during active preparation. Retry a lost status reply within the advertised deadline.
- perf(runner): deliver transport events when they arrive instead of polling every 5 ms (#20862)
  > - `DurablePrpControlPlane` has a new `onReadableStateChange(listener)`. It fires when the state that a reader of `store` can see may have changed. The control plane calls it after every stored-state replacement or save, after a saved checkpoint releases events that were waiting for it, and after an authenticated runner moves the recovered cursor.
  > - The transport subscribes when it creates its control plane. A change wakes one delivery pass in the next task (`setImmediate`). The pass never runs inside the control plane's own commit, and several changes in one task share one pass. This keeps the existing order: the control plane finishes its commit before a reader reacts.
  > - The three `setInterval(..., 5)` timers are gone. `#startPump` and `#stopPump` replace them and keep the same lifecycle: the pump runs from session start, and it stops on release, controller retirement, failure, and close.
- perf(issues): decide task reads for a list in one batch (#20858)
  > - `filterIssuesForActor` asks the authorization service for all read decisions in one `decideReads` call. The route module no longer loops over tasks.
  > - `readableIssueEdgeIds` (relation edges) uses the same filter. It had its own copy of the per-task loop.
  > - Inside one batch, the authorization service reads the membership of an external authorizing user one time. Before, it read it for each task.
- perf(github): check the requester provenance of an issue list's review waits in one batch (#20851)
  > - `server/src/services/chat-channels/run-origin.ts`:
  > - `server/src/services/chat-channels/github/review-wait.ts`: `readInheritedGitHubReviewWaits` checks the provenance of its runs with the batch. A 403 still gives `revoked`. The single-run `readInheritedGitHubReviewWait` does not change.
  > - `server/src/services/chat-channels/run-origin.test.ts`: two new tests (see Verification).
- test(chaos): add DF1, a sandbox disk-full fault on the final workspace save (#20810)
  > - `server/src/services/sandbox-disk-full-fault.ts` (new, test-only): `sandboxDiskFullFaultTarget` wraps the target of a final save only when the harness armed that save. The capture commands then exit 1 with the exact message the in-sandbox script writes on ENOSPC, and the provider download fails with `No space left on device`. The gate follows `@paperclipai/shared/crash-points`: `PAPERCLIP_DISK_FULL_FAULT_CONTROL` is read once at import and ignored under `NODE_ENV=production`. Disarmed, the hook is one comparison and returns the save's own target.
  > - `server/src/services/remote-workspaces.ts`: the capture call in `saveRemoteWorkspace` passes its target through the hook. No other product change. `workspace-recovery.ts` is not touched.
  > - `tests/restart-chaos/disk-full-faults.ts` (new): the DF1 kind, the control-file seam (atomic writes), the rest evidence (`activeRecoveryAction.evidence.systemRecovery.failure.cause.label`), the `disk_full_fault` T check, and the DF1 R3 rule (one expected Retry that must finish the task).
- chore(lockfile): refresh pnpm lockfiles (#20845)
  > - Refresh the dependency lock from the manifests on master.
  > - Refresh the sandbox provider lockfiles from their manifests on master.
  > - Include the matching offline report correction and hashes.
- chore(lockfile): refresh pnpm lockfiles (#20824)
  > - Refresh the dependency lock from the manifests on master.
  > - Refresh the sandbox provider lockfiles from their manifests on master.
  > - Include the matching offline report correction and hashes.
- Carry plugin task refusals and batch project reads (#20762)
  > - Accept a proven admission refusal only as the response to a matching submit operation. Record it on the matching lease so cleanup does not need to find a process. Keep the same-task status path for ambiguous errors.
  > - Read a batch of company-scoped project snapshots. Require the same capabilities as the separate reads and reject the batch if any project is inaccessible, archived, or changes during its read. Apply entity scope before pagination and use the same input validation in the test harness.
- test(restart-chaos): long mid-tool K15 outages, Runner evidence, and TM1 stalls on Daytona (#20711)
  > - `--k15-hold-seconds <n|min-max>` (0–600). A K15 stops the server with SIGTERM, keeps it down for the seeded hold, then starts the next deploy generation. The range is drawn only for K15 cells, so existing seeds keep their plans. Storms use it too.
  > - New opt-in workload `T1`. The agent runs one shell loop in the foreground for about 100 s, then saves `TICKS-<nonce>=50`. Its fault window, 35–80 s after launch, lands mid-tool.
  > - New `daytona-runner-logs.ts`, a read-only probe of each wave sandbox through the Daytona toolbox. It runs only on sandboxes with the wave's labels, and only on `started` ones. It records every process, memory, disk, `dmesg` OOM lines, and the tails of `runnerd.stderr.log`, `runner-process.identity` and `runner-state.json`. It redacts known secrets. It runs right after a K15 stop, just before the next deploy starts, and at wave end. Output goes to `results/<wave>/daytona-runner/` and to `runner_logs_captured` in the timeline. The probe never stops, starts or deletes a sandbox, and a failed capture is recorded instead of thrown.
- test(chaos): close a covering modal before clicking Retry, and name it when it blocks (#20715)
  > - `board-ui.ts`: before and while it waits for Retry, `clickRetryOnPage` closes an open modal that does not itself offer Retry. It clicks "Not now" (or "No thanks", "Maybe later", "Dismiss") when present, else it presses Escape. It also closes a modal that opens after Retry appears and swallows the click, then clicks again. At most three dialogs are closed. Each click has a timeout equal to the per-button request wait.
  > - The click result has a new optional `dismissedDialogs` list (first line of each closed dialog). The observer copies it into the task's retry attempt.
  > - New failure reason `ui_retry_blocked_by_modal` (in `types.ts`): a visible Retry exists in the DOM, it is not reachable by role, and a modal stays open. R3 in `grader.ts` reports it with the banner text, like the other UI reasons.
- test(restart-chaos): K15 redeploy, F1 workspace symlink, and the staging failure census (#20679)
  > - New fault `K15` (redeploy), in `tests/restart-chaos/redeploy.ts` and `redeploy-preload.mjs`. A K15 wave gives every server an emulated host name through a `--import` preload, which changes the process-space hash. It also gives Railway-shaped deployment variables with the instance home as the exclusive volume. A K15 stops the server with SIGTERM and starts the next generation, with a new host name and deployment id.
  > - `supervisor.ts`, `main.ts`, `config.ts` and `types.ts`: select K15 with `--faults K15`. It works on `--env local` and `--env daytona`. The receipt records `redeploy` and the timeline records `redeploy_switched`. Config refuses K15 together with MS1–MS3.
  > - New opt-in workload `F1` (`workloads.ts`): the agent keeps an absolute symlink in its repository workspace. A staging save-back refused such a link and blocked the task. The oracle needs `done` and the saved notes file, with no Retry. `f1-symlink.test.ts` covers the prompt, the grading and the option planning.
- test(recovery): re-arm a capture-policy save whose finished coordinator names a lapsed owner (#20672)
  > - `workspace-recovery-capture-policy.test.ts`: add "re-arms a save whose finished coordinator still names a lapsed finalizer owner". The test uses the staging record: export hold with `historicalWorkspaceSource` and `workspaceFinalizationBlock`, a resolved no-replay sibling, waiting user input, and a lapsed finalizer owner. It drives the worker's own discovery loop until the worker is idle.
  > - No production code changes.
- perf(github): read the inherited review waits of an issue list in one pass (#20673)
  > - `server/src/services/chat-channels/github/review-wait.ts`:
  > - `server/src/services/native-runtime/external-review-wait.ts`: `listExternalReviewWaits` uses the batch read. The rest of the list and its authorization do not change.
  > - `server/src/__tests__/github-review-wait.test.ts`: a new test reads 18 runs in one batch and compares each result with a separate single-run read. The runs include each revocation case (connection disabled or inactive, review state, review agent, repository, repository id, thread, publication, identity, company), a newer unreviewed head, no identity, no task, an endpoint of another agent, a run in another company and a duplicate run. The test also makes sure that the batch sends one review statement and one publication read for each company. The existing revocation tests also check the batch read.
- perf(run-events): stop blocking checkpoint FK checks during event appends (#20670)
  > - `server/src/services/heartbeat-run-events.ts`: lock the run row `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` instead of `FOR UPDATE` in the transactional append path. Add a comment that names the contract.
  > - `heartbeat-run-event-atomic-append.test.ts`: add a regression test. An append transaction stays open. A checkpoint row insert for the same run must finish under a 2 s `lock_timeout`. A second appender must still wait. After release, the sequence is 1, 2 with no gap and no duplicate. The existing test with 40 concurrent appenders on two connections still proves contiguous sequence numbers. Update the existing lock-statement assertion to the new clause.
  > - Add `scripts/benchmark-run-event-checkpoint-lock.ts`. It replays a native event stream with the real append and the real checkpoint writer on separate connections. It times each checkpoint save for a baseline append module and the current one.
- perf(chat): find terminal GitHub responses by issue run indexes (#20671)
  > - Rewrite the latest-run status subquery in the GitHub terminal-response discovery page as two index-backed branches (native binding, and legacy context without a native binding).
  > - Add two integration tests for the sweep. In the first, newer runs of the same agent that do not bind the issue (one with a native binding to another issue, but a legacy context that names this issue) must not hide the stopped run. In the second, a newer active run with a native binding to the issue keeps the request waiting.
  > - Add `scripts/benchmark-github-terminal-response-sweep.ts`. It captures the SQL that the real worker generates at a baseline commit and at `HEAD`, compares all rows, and measures both.
- docs(release): stable notes for the 2026.1110.0-beta.0 soak (v2026.1113.0) (#20610)
  > - Added `releases/beta/v2026.1110.0-beta.0.md` with curated stable notes for v2026.1113.0 in the same layout as `releases/v2026.1106.0.md`: overview, Breaking Changes, Highlights, Improvements, Fixes, Upgrade Guide, Contributors
  > - The notes are delta-framed against `releases/v2026.1106.0.md`. Features that stable already describes are not repeated
  > - Breaking Changes lists the Google Workspace connector gated off by default (#19247), one execution workspace per subtask (#19860), agent-created task trees limited to parent and child (#19312), native agents reporting status through `paperclip_finish`/`paperclip_block` (#18411, #18489), warm-by-default local Codex and OpenCode Runner agents with migration `0443` (#19888), skill packages moving out of PostgreSQL with this release as the rollback floor (#18082), Enter to submit (#19272), and the narrower `pi_local` environment (#14503)
- Gate agent plugin tabs by execution environment (#20657)
  > - Add an optional `agentEnvironmentDriver` declaration for agent-only detail tabs.
  > - Check the saved agent environment before listing settings links, opening direct views, or offering task panels.
  > - Keep unrestricted contributions available and hide restricted views while their configuration is unknown.
- Keep project environment mappings independent of shared defaults (#20651)
  > - Add nullable `projects.environment_id` and expose `Project.environmentId`. Save the effective creation default once on the project. Keep environment placement separate from checkout/isolation policy.
  > - Read and write Environment directly in Project Configuration. Keep the control available with isolated workspaces disabled. Each form reads its project's value, even when environment choices share a query cache.
  > - Offer environments compatible with the current storage owner. Change the target and storage together when the existing storage selector attaches a core project to a plugin. Preserve retained-data restrictions and validate the observed storage owner again on save.
- test(native): cover unknown-outcome recovery and hold settlement without a recorded PID space (#20649)
  > - `durable-prp-control-plane.test.ts`: an authority that throws `semantic_tool_outcome_unknown` settles as `tool_call_interrupted_by_restart` for `earlier_controller`, and rethrows for `this_controller`. Any other rejection rethrows.
  > - `completed-turn-cleanup-hold-settle.test.ts`: a coordinator row without `controllerHostHash`, whose server boot is recorded stopped in `controller_instances`, settles the run's holds through the controller-instance proof. No attestation is needed.
- perf(native): parallelize durability eligibility checks (#20646)
  > - Check the existing terminal-controller metadata and checkpoint-existence predicate within the existing eight-reader source batch.
  > - Validate harness independence receipts with at most four concurrent readers per existing page.
  > - Stop admitting more harness reads after an invalid result or error. Join all started reads before returning or throwing.
- test(restart-chaos): save a screenshot and banner HTML when the Retry button wait fails (#20643)
  > - `tests/restart-chaos/board-ui.ts`: add `saveRetryMissingDiagnostics`. On a failed Retry wait it saves `ui-retry-missing-<issue>-<time>.png` (full page) and a `.json` file with the page URL, each `[role=status]`/`[role=alert]` banner's `outerHTML`, and every Retry-named button's visibility, bounding box, banner membership and HTML. Diagnostics never change the click result.
  > - `tests/restart-chaos/main.ts`: the wave's board helper writes to `<wave>/ui-retry-diagnostics`.
  > - `tests/restart-chaos/observer.ts`, `types.ts`: the task's retry attempt records the saved file paths.
- perf(task-chat): open tasks from a preloaded cache and cold tasks in one request (#20592)
  > - `ui/src/lib/issue-chat-bootstrap.ts`: one module decides when to send a bootstrap and lets page reads share it (`readFromIssueChatBootstrap`). Seeding uses the existing `issueChatBootstrapCache` (replace-style reads are refreshed when newer, merge-style reads are filled only when empty, other companies are ignored). It skips tasks that are loading, loaded in the last 30 seconds, or already fresh in cache, under the id or the identifier. It never aborts a request in flight, so a click that unmounts the list still uses the answer. Each request is bound to the account that sent it; a request sent before the first session read is bound to the first account the session reports, and a sign-out revokes it. A page read shares a bootstrap only while its key holds no data (a refresh after a write always reads the server), and waits at most 5 seconds for it. Every ref of a task (id and identifier) is tracked at request start. Entries expire after 30 seconds.
  > - Task lists preload the chat of their top 10 rows in one request, after the list's own reads settle and the browser is idle (`usePreloadTaskChats`, `when-page-settles.ts`): the Coordinator task list (`TaskList` with `preloadCompanyId`), the streamlined and legacy issues lists and the streamlined Inbox. Recents now uses the same path, so a task in both places loads once. A list that stays open preloads each task at most once in five minutes: live events (#20563's lanes) keep a preloaded task's cache current or mark it stale, and a stale key is read from the server, never from an older bootstrap. All seeded keys are per-task keys on the fast live lane, not the ambient lane.
  > - Open intent (press, click, resting hover or focus) on a task with no chat history in cache warms it with one bootstrap request instead of six separate reads (`prefetchIssueDetailForNavigation`). A task seen before still refreshes its cached reads one by one.
- test(restart-chaos): wait for any visible Retry, not the first match (#20616)
  > - `tests/restart-chaos/board-ui.ts`: wait on the `Retry` role locator filtered to visible elements (`filter({ visible: true })`), instead of the first match in document order. After the wait, re-query the visible buttons and click them banner first, as before.
  > - `tests/restart-chaos/board-ui.test.ts`: add a page with an earlier zero-size `Retry` and a visible banner `Retry`. The test fails before the change (`ui_retry_button_missing`) and passes after it.
- perf(realtime): share permission reads among queued live events (#20624)
  > - `live-event-delivery.ts` gives each queued event a sequence number. It passes a shared read set to `authorize`. A set serves only the events that were in the queue when the set opened. The delivery replaces the set after 250 ms or 100 events and discards it when the queue is empty.
  > - `sharedDeliveryRead` returns the pending read for a key, or starts it. It removes a read that fails, so the next event reads again.
  > - `live-events-ws.ts` uses the shared set for the membership or key check, the transcript scope decision, the run binding and the task read decision.
- refactor: remove SPEC docs and repoint spec citations (#20565)
  > - Deleted `doc/SPEC.md` and `doc/SPEC-implementation.md`.
  > - Moved these spec sections, because live code comments cite them:
  > - Did not move any other part of the specs.
- perf(plugins): read the lifecycle inbox from per-resource cursors (#20576)
  > - `packages/db/src/schema/plugin_lifecycle_resource_cursors.ts`: new table `plugin_lifecycle_resource_cursors`. It has one row per plugin, company, resource type and resource id. `acknowledged_through_id` means: every event of that resource at or below this id is acknowledged by the plugin. Plugin and company deletion cascade.
  > - Migration `0462_plugin_lifecycle_resource_cursors`: creates the table and carries existing acknowledgments forward (see Risks for the previous-release story).
  > - `server/src/services/plugin-lifecycle-inbox.ts`:
- perf(issues): read created-from tasks by index and inbox review waits by page (#20573)
  > - `server/src/services/issue-creation-origin.ts`: `createdFromIssueCondition` is now `issues.id <> source AND issues.id IN (three branches)`. Each branch starts from an index:
  > - `packages/db`: add `issues_company_legacy_origin_run_idx` on `issues (company_id, origin_run_id) WHERE origin_issue_id IS NULL AND origin_run_id IS NOT NULL`. Migration `0461` creates it with `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY IF NOT EXISTS` and is registered with the other concurrent migrations. `doc/DATABASE.md` documents the index and the benchmark command.
  > - `server/src/services/issues.ts`: add `blockedInboxReviewWaitIssueIds`. It returns the in-review tasks in the blocker closure of the page rows. The blocked-inbox map reads review waits only for these tasks. The liveness classifier reads a wait only for a row or for a blocker that it reaches from a row, and the caller reads only the findings of the rows. The classifier does not change.
- chore(lockfile): refresh pnpm lockfiles (#20615)
  > - Refresh the dependency lock from the manifests on master.
  > - Refresh the sandbox provider lockfiles from their manifests on master.
  > - Include the matching offline report correction and hashes.
- Keep Slack form custody and receipt transfer durable (#19825)
  > - Preserve form grants, sessions, encrypted evidence, terminal receipts and company-authorized Retry.
  > - Check initial form enrollment before the provider send. Keep a typed preparation wait only while an exact current enrollment obligation exists.
  > - Refund the attempt only when the claimed attempt and credential lease still match and provider invocation has not started. Possible or accepted sends retain uncertain-delivery custody. An ordinary error with the same text still uses the normal five-attempt limit.
- perf(workspaces): probe workspace run bindings through indexes (#20568)
  > - `expirationScope` builds `relevant_runs` from a `UNION` of index probes, one for each binding. The key sets go in as arrays (`= ANY(ARRAY(...))`). With `IN (subquery)`, the planner chose a hash join over a full scan, because it does not count the cost of reading TOASTed contexts.
  > - The legacy context keys use `context_snapshot ->> 'key'`.
