This expression matches the existing indexes `heartbeat_runs_company_ctx_issue_created_idx` and `heartbeat_runs_company_ctx_task_created_idx`. For a non-object context, `->>` gives `NULL`, as the strict JSONPath decode did. Such a run can still match through a native binding.
  > - New partial index `heartbeat_runs_company_ctx_execution_workspace_idx` on `(company_id, context_snapshot ->> 'executionWorkspaceId') WHERE context_snapshot ? 'executionWorkspaceId'`. The query repeats the predicate. For an array context, `?` can be true, but `->>` gives `NULL`, so the result does not change. The index is partial so that it serves only this probe. A full index that starts with `company_id` made the planner choose it for an unrelated company-scoped query. Migration 0460 builds it with `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY IF NOT EXISTS`. The migration is in the list of migrations that run outside a transaction.
- perf(native): default remote sandbox runs to a warm, reusable sandbox for measured harnesses (#20048)
  > - **One rule for the default.** `defaultsRemoteSandboxRunToWarm` (`native-runtime/native-warm-capability.ts`) decides the remote-sandbox warm default for both the lease (environment view) and the runner (native session policy), in one vocabulary (runtime backend plus ACPX agent). It is applied only when no lifecycle was chosen anywhere: admitted session, environment `runnerLifecycleMode`, or agent `lifecycleMode`. It holds only when:
  > - **The harness resolves as the runtime resolves it.** `resolveWarmProviderSelection` uses `resolvePaperclipRunnerProviderProfile`, so an omitted `provider` is Codex and ACPX without an agent is Claude, in both decisions. A config the runtime would refuse gets no default.
  > - **Lease side** (`runner-environment-lifecycle.ts`, `environment-run-orchestrator.ts`): for an eligible run only, the orchestrator asks the provider before acquisition (`providerSupportsReusableLeases` in `environment-runtime.ts`: the acquisition rule, one registry lookup for a plugin provider, no worker-ready wait). With support, the run-scoped view sets `reuseLease: true` and forwards the warm idle window to the provider's auto-stop. The shared environment is never rewritten. Every other run resolves exactly as on master, without the probe.
- Choose project storage from compatible environments (#20587)
  > - Show **Available in** when configured environments require different project storage. Each option names the environments that can use the same storage owner. Hide the selector when all choices are compatible.
  > - Derive compatibility from repository staging and managed binding ownership. Local, SSH, and sandbox environments use core staging. Managed storage requires a registered task driver from the same installed storage plugin. Separate storage plugins remain incompatible. Unknown plugin contracts and the fake sandbox are excluded.
  > - Follow the creating agent's environment, or the person's primary agent, then the instance default and local fallback. Apply managed-only and forced Kubernetes policy. The product has no separate user or company environment-default field today.
- perf(native): narrow backup eligibility to selected runs (#20599)
  > - Keep `AS MATERIALIZED` as an intentional join-order fence: build selected task-session and last-run identities before joining backup leases.
  > - Keep only narrow identity columns in that materialized set. Read the JSON receipt flag through the selected run's primary key after the lease and current-backup filters, so profiles for unrelated identities do not widen the intermediate rows.
  > - Preserve company and agent bindings, exact normalized-session text comparison, current-backup exclusion, scope deduplication, ordering, and the 100-row page.
- perf(native): index the backup lease lookup by scope digest (#20571)
  > - Add the partial expression index `environment_leases_native_backup_scope_idx` on `(metadata->'nativeHarnessBackup'->>'sessionScopeSha256')`, where that value is not null.
  > - Add migration `0459`. It uses `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY IF NOT EXISTS`. The migration runner runs it as a top-level statement, the same as migrations 0454–0458.
  > - Add a regression test. It checks the lease-matching rules: run leases, a lease with only the backup stamp, and the identity's own lease match. Another digest, a non-string stamp, a top-level key with the same name, and the same digest in another company do not match. The test also checks that the custom plan and a forced generic plan of the generated SQL use `BitmapOr` with the new index and no sequential scan.
- docs(rules): correct the scope of the API route and company-scope rules (#20596)
  > - The API route rule and `AGENTS.md` section 8 now apply to routes that read or write company data.
  > - The API route rule names the instance-level routes and says not to add a company check to them.
  > - The status code list is now a list of common codes. Reviewers flag an incorrect code, not an unlisted code.
- docs(agents): add a core rule that points to the module-boundaries rule (#20598)
  > - Added rule 4 to `AGENTS.md` section 5, "Keep behavior with its owning module". The rule links to `rules/module-boundaries.mdc`.
  > - An earlier commit on this branch added a copy of the pstack `principle-boundary-discipline` skill. A later commit removed it. The net change is `AGENTS.md` only.
- perf(recovery): probe recovery source runs and stale-continuation runs by key (#20584)
  > - `workspace-recovery-fallback-discovery.ts`: the source run, finalizer and lease probes compare `id = canonicalExecutionUuid(evidence->>'runId')`. The helper returns null unless the text is the canonical lowercase uuid form. Thus the probe matches exactly the rows that `id::text = evidence->>'runId'` matched. The helper already exists in `execution-identity.ts`.
  > - `nextWorkspaceRecoveryAt` and worker discovery use these conditions. Their SQL changes only in these probes.
  > - `checkStaleDroppedComments` reads in two stages. The first stage keeps comments that a stale-continuation cancellation dropped and that no open wake carries. The second stage applies the run probes. The run probes use the `taskRuns` helper from #20412, which is now shared by both checks. The stale-wake lookup ends with `offset 0`, so it stays a per-comment index probe.
- perf(ui): cut repeated reads while a task is open (#20563)
  > - `ui/src/lib/query-invalidation-batcher.ts`: Add a minimum spacing option to the invalidation batcher. A quiet lane flushes after the normal batch interval. A busy lane flushes at most once per spacing window. Add `createLaneRoutingBatcher`, which sends each invalidation to one of two batchers by query key.
  > - `ui/src/context/LiveUpdatesProvider.tsx`: Route company-wide keys to a 5-second ambient lane. These keys are task lists and summaries, the agent list, heartbeats, company live runs, sidebar badges, the dashboard, costs and the company activity feed. All other keys, such as task detail, comments, task runs and browsers, keep the 300 ms lane.
  > - `ui/src/lib/live-runs-cache.ts`: While the company connection is open, a successful active run list or active run polls every 15 seconds instead of every 1 or 3 seconds. Run lifecycle events already refresh these queries, and progress events patch them.
- refactor: move AGENTS.md section detail to docs and rules, add a bug-fixing section (#20585)
  > - The scoped-rules section now tells agents to list the rule headers with one `grep` command. Agents read a rule body only for a design decision in its area, when the description is not sufficient, or when a reviewer cites it.
  > - `rules/README.md` says that each `description` must state the required behavior in one sentence.
  > - `rules/pull-request-review.mdc` has a description that states its requirements.
- perf(chat): index chat-admission checks by company and payload issue id (#20562)
  > - Migration `0458_chat_admission_issue_index` builds `chat_actions_company_issue_kind_idx` with `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY IF NOT EXISTS`. It runs through the existing top-level statement path, which repairs an invalid index on retry.
  > - The Drizzle schema declares the index. The snapshot chain moves to `0458` and keeps five snapshots.
  > - New regression test `server/src/__tests__/chat-admission-condition.test.ts` pins the admission semantics on real PostgreSQL:
- perf(workspaces): reuse duplicate cohort source archives (#20574)
  > - Reuse a completed archive when the canonical source and all capture options match within one cohort.
  > - Retain at most one shared archive per physical source root. A different capture scope uses the existing direct capture path.
  > - Copy the completed archive with the native clone hint into each member's scratch directory.
- perf(attention): read each retention candidate kind in its own bounded branch (#20566)
  > - `server/src/services/attention-retention.ts`: replace the single union scan with `candidateQuery`. Each source kind is a branch that has its own filters, retention join and one-page limit. The outer query still orders by `(source_kind, source_id)` and keeps 100 rows.
  > - Only add a filter when the scope sets it. There is no `param is null or ...` form now.
  > - A single-source read compares `id = $uuid`. It returns no rows for text that is not the canonical lowercase uuid form. The former `id::text = $text` comparison also never matched such text.
- perf(chat): find the conversation head from open delivery work (#20564)
  > - `delivery-ledger.ts`: add `openConversationDeliveryIds(endpointId)`. It returns the endpoint's open delivery IDs. It selects the same set as the old state predicate for that endpoint.
  > - `conversation-queue.ts`: the conversation head query uses `id in (openConversationDeliveryIds(endpointId))` in place of the state `or`. The thread filter, reaction exclusion, order, limit and lifecycle dependency read do not change.
  > - `inbound-queue-discovery.test.ts`: add a real PostgreSQL test. It compares the new set with the old predicate on three endpoints. It covers each state, issued and processing wakes, duplicate wakes, wakes of non-processed deliveries, processed, other-kind and null-delivery actions, an action on a different endpoint of the same company, and reactions. It also checks that the real captured head query returns the oracle's first row.
- perf(native): index heartbeat runs by company and native session (#20560)
  > - `packages/db`: add `heartbeat_runs_company_native_session_idx` on `(company_id, native_session_id) WHERE native_session_id IS NOT NULL`. Migration `0457_native_session_run_index` builds it with `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY IF NOT EXISTS`. The migration is registered as a top-level-statement migration, the same way as 0454–0456.
  > - Add a regression test in `native-harness-backup-expiration.test.ts`. It captures the real retention lookup and proves that its plan can use the index. It also pins the company and session scope: a live run of another company with the same session ID does not protect a checkpoint. A live run of the same agent in another session does not protect it. A live run of the same session does protect it.
  > - Add `scripts/benchmark-native-session-run-index.ts`. It runs the real checkpoint retention pass (dry run), captures the exact SQL, and replays it with and without the index. It also times the migration through the normal migration runner.
- test(restart-chaos): measure TTFT in a real repository workspace, after an idle restart, and on Daytona (#20047)
  > - `--workspace-repo <path>`: the seed uses that repository as the project's primary workspace (git_worktree isolation), and tasks are created in the project.
  > - A `postfault` phase: K1 while idle, then a follow-up on each task; reported in its own table.
  > - `--env daytona`, `--daytona-image`, `--daytona-cpu`, `--daytona-warm` (the reusable warm Daytona fixture). The Daytona controller preparation moved to `daytona-controller.ts`, and every sandbox is cleaned up at the end; incomplete cleanup fails the run.
- perf(chat): authorize the waits of an issue page with one read (#20551)
  > - `server/src/services/native-runtime/chat-attachment-reuse.ts`:
  > - `server/src/services/native-runtime/external-review-wait.ts`: `listExternalReviewWaits` uses the batch authorization. The inherited GitHub review-wait reads do not change.
  > - `server/src/__tests__/batched-chat-authorization.integration.test.ts`: new tests on real PostgreSQL. For each case, the batch result must equal the result of separate single-run reads. The cases include each denial: a missing or foreign comment, no comments, no execution marker, a wrong provider, agent or issue, a person without a link or without a membership, a viewer member, a revoked link, a disabled or unavailable destination, a paused endpoint, a closed conversation, an unprocessed delivery, a delivery without a person, and a verifying endpoint. A test also makes sure that one person's decision never applies to another person in the same batch, and that a batch sends one message-link statement.
- docs(agents): shorten AGENTS.md and move data-path detail to a scoped rule (#20557)
  > - Added an ownership statement at the top of `AGENTS.md`.
  > - Section 1 now says that the code and tests are the source of truth. The spec documents are background.
  > - Removed the spec from the definition of done and from rule 4.
- perf(workspaces): migrate with one archive and no readback (#20554)
  > - Add single-pass capture that fills fixed-length checksum fields in the existing archive header after copying.
  > - Remove migration readback, test extraction, fresh-source recapture, and Git integrity scans.
  > - Compute the encrypted archive checksum while writing it.
- test(restart-chaos): fire a planned restart at native start (#20538)
  > - New `tests/restart-chaos/native-trigger.ts`: `--native-trigger native-start|provider-event[+<seconds>]` parsing and a `NativeTrigger`. The trigger arms each task's planned fault at its planned time. Each wave loop pass, it reads the task's running runs' events through the board API with a per-run cursor. It fires the fault the given delay after the first matching event created after arming. It records receipts, misses (`task_rested`, `no_matching_event`), the fault id, a summary section and a shortfall check.
  > - `config.ts`, `types.ts`, `main.ts`: registry lines only. They add the option, construct the trigger next to `--storage-trigger`, arm faults instead of firing them by time, tick the trigger in the wave loop, keep the loop alive while it fires, and write `native-trigger.json` plus the summary section.
  > - New `tests/restart-chaos/native-trigger.test.ts`: parsing and conflicts, event matching, firing after the delay, ignoring events from before arming and from non-running runs, waiting while another fault runs, misses, and fault linking.
- test(runner-e2e): resync the SDK public source pins with master (#20550)
  > - `tests/runner-e2e/source-sdk-public-inputs.json`: source pins regenerated with `node tests/runner-e2e/source-sdk-public-inputs.mjs 902f3767960dfa4fda5b3268cf78568c7422e840 --write` (11 changed blobs, plus new source files). The script reports `runtimePinsChanged: false`: the lock, compiler, parser and zod pins are unchanged.
- test(evals): land the chat catalog eval harness on master (#19614)
  > - Most of the diff (808 of 851 files) is under `tests/runner-e2e/`: chat-catalog domains, scenarios, descriptors, the TestOps manifest, private evidence tooling, and their tests.
  > - Graders now check saved state, setup literals and rendered or clicked links. Many keyword and regex checks on free-form replies are removed. Each changed grader has a bumped `oracleVersion` and calibration tests (a valid reply, a reworded valid reply, and broken variants that must fail).
  > - The shared link helper `hasLink` no longer treats a list link (for example `/COM/issues?view=mine`) as a link to every task without a URL key.
- perf(ui): open sidebar Recents tasks from cache (#20450)
  > - `ui/src/hooks/useIssueNavigationPrefetch.ts`: the open-intent handlers from `IssueLinkQuicklook` move into one hook (light warm on pass-over; `history: true` on a resting hover or focus, a mouse or pen press, and a click). `IssueLinkQuicklook` now uses the hook. Its behavior does not change.
  > - `ui/src/components/RecentTaskNavItem.tsx`: one Recents link wrapper that applies the hook. The classic list (`SidebarRecentTasks`), the Coordinator task rows and `DesignSidebar` use it. `SidebarNavItem` accepts the handlers through a new optional `linkHandlers` prop.
  > - Classic Recents links now open `/issues/<identifier>` (Coordinator already did). The old UUID link remounted the task page after a redirect to the identifier route.
- test(restart-chaos): anchor two-server speed samples on the primary's restart (#20535)
  > - `wave-speed.ts`:
  > - `wave-speed.test.ts`: six tests built from s302's saved receipts, including two `collectWaveSpeed` cases (a run frozen before its first activity; a retry after an MS2 freeze).
  > - README: one note on how the speed grade treats MS faults.
- test(restart-chaos): two servers on one database (MS1-MS3) and a cross-server invariant (#20518)
  > - New `tests/restart-chaos/multi-server.ts`:
  > - `supervisor.ts`: records every server's process window and freezes, spawns and stops B (`<wave>/server-b.log`), and runs the MS steps. TM freezes are recorded too.
  > - `types.ts`, `scheduler.ts`, `journey-matrix.ts`, `config.ts`: MS1–MS3 fault kinds, seeded holds, and validation (local only; no storm, crash point or K14).
- test(restart-chaos): provider faults across K2/K4 restarts, per-workload message, per-run speed split (#20515)
  > - `--provider-fault-restart` accepts K1, K2 and K4. A comma list (for example `K4,K4,K2,K2`) cycles across PV1/PV2 tasks in plan order. K4 holds the control plane down for 120 s.
  > - `--provider-fault-message` accepts `WORKLOAD=message` entries next to the default, for example `S1=interrupt_ack,terminal`.
  > - G1 is removed from `PROVIDER_FAULT_WORKLOADS`. The config check that told G1 to add a restart is removed, because G1 is now rejected.
- test(restart-chaos): arm a provider-fault Stop on the first live provider turn (#20512)
  > - `observer.ts`: the run-event cursor now tracks a live provider turn (`turn.started` with no `turn.completed/failed/cancelled/interrupted` yet). Run samples expose it as `providerTurnActive`.
  > - New `stopArmed()`: a task with a provider fault arms its Stop on the first poll that shows a live provider turn. Other tasks keep the seeded wall-clock window, unchanged.
  > - A runner replacement or stop clears the live-turn mark, as it already clears open tools.
- test(restart-chaos): add --rr-stop-on recovering so RR follow-up stops land inside recovery (#20511)
  > - `repeated-restarts.ts`: `--rr-stop-on recovering` is parsed by `parseRepeatedRestartOptions`. It needs an RR fault and does not combine with `--rr-gap-seconds` or `--replay`. `runRepeatedRestarts` takes `stopOn`: with it, no gap is slept, each stop waits on the new `waitForStopPhase` hook, and only the last replacement is waited on to health.
  > - `supervisor.ts`: `waitForStopPhase` reuses the K6 phase poll (`waitForCrashLoopPhase`, 50 ms). The record carries `stopOn`.
  > - `types.ts`, `config.ts`, `main.ts`: the `rrStopOn` option, its flag, and one line to pass it to the supervisor.
- test(restart-chaos): add ST4 partial multipart and ST7 read-after-write lag storage faults (#20504)
  > - `storage-s3-ops.ts` (new): names the S3 operation from the method and query, and reads the part number.
  > - `storage-multipart.ts` (new): ST4 rule planning.
  > - `storage-lag.ts` (new): ST7 rule planning, and a tracker of successful `PutObject` and `CompleteMultipartUpload` writes per key.
- test(restart-chaos): keep process start identity out of TM2/TM3 aging (#20500)
  > - `time-faults.ts`: add `isProcessIdentityTime`. `agingStatements` skips columns named for a process start. `paperclip_shift_json` keeps JSON keys named for a process start (any spelling) unchanged. `unshiftJson` skips the same keys, so the grader does not move them forward.
  > - `time-faults.test.ts`: a unit test for the statements and the unshift.
  > - `time-faults-db.test.ts`: the embedded-Postgres aging test now checks that a `controller_process_started_at` column and `processStartedAt` / `processStartedAtMs` JSON keys keep their values.
- test(chaos): fire a planned restart while the server is mid S3 transfer (#20451)
  > - New `tests/restart-chaos/storage-trigger.ts`: targets `snapshot-upload`, `snapshot-part` (multipart `UploadPart`), `snapshot-download` and `run-log`; the `StorageTrigger` that arms faults, fires on a match, holds the request and records receipts.
  > - `storage-proxy.ts`: an `observe(listener)` hook. The listener sees every request and can hold it (the existing latency path).
  > - `main.ts`, `config.ts`, `types.ts`: registry lines only. `--storage-trigger` (needs `--storage s3` and `--triggers time`); a due fault is armed instead of fired; the wave writes `storage-trigger.json`.
- test(restart-chaos): remove storage fault window time from speed budgets (#20501)
  > - `wave-speed.ts`: `storageFaultWindows()` turns receipts into windows (an open window ends now). `mergeWindows()` joins overlapping windows, so `outageFreeClock()` removes shared time once. `collectWaveSpeed()` takes the storage receipts and removes their time from spans; `gradeWaveSpeed()` removes it from restart recoveries. The storage share is reported apart from the restart outage share.
  > - `main.ts`: one line passes `storageChaos.receipts` to `collectWaveSpeed()`.
  > - README: one paragraph in the Cloud storage section.
- test(restart-chaos): add repeated restarts (RR1-RR3): a restart inside the previous restart's recovery (#20480)
  > - Add `RR1`, `RR2` and `RR3` to `--faults` (`repeated-restarts.ts`):
  > - Add `--rr-gap-seconds <n>` or `<min>-<max>` (1–300). It replaces every RR gap. RR faults do not combine with `--storm-minutes`.
  > - The gaps are seeded per task (`restartSteps` in `plan.json`, also in `--matrix` plans). They are drawn only for RR tasks, so the plans of existing seeds do not change. `--replay` restarts again after the recorded gaps.
- Reuse identical captured workspace archives (#20476)
  > - Compare the captured manifest, plaintext archive hash and byte count with the current snapshot in the same company and workspace. Check its wrapped encryption key and object size before reuse.
  > - Complete reuse through the normal save transaction. Recheck the writer, epoch and sandbox location. Mark only an empty, unused generation as deleted and keep a durable replay receipt. Read that receipt again when another caller finishes between registration and snapshot lookup.
  > - Preserve earlier upload attempts, uncertain work, save settlement, export cleanup, repository metadata and parked waiter notifications.
- test(chaos): provider message faults for Claude (ACPX) and across a K1 restart (#20454)
  > - `acpx-provider-faults.ts`: builds a per-wave stand-in runner package under `<repo>/node_modules/.restart-chaos-<wave>/` (symlinks to the real package, except a generated `dist/cli/acpx-runtime-sidecar.cjs`). It also parses `--provider-fault-restart`.
  > - `provider-shim/acpx-sidecar-hook.mjs`: a server-only resolve hook that loads `runnerd-codex-transport` (source `src/live/*.ts` or built `dist/live/*.js`) from the stand-in package. Product code then hashes the stand-in sidecar, and the runner verifies and launches it. The module's own imports resolve to the real package.
  > - `provider-shim/acpx-fault-tap.cjs`: the stand-in installs this tap and then runs the real sidecar bundle in the same process, as the runner's verified CommonJS loader does. For Claude, `terminal` is the provider's ACP `session/prompt` response after a `session/cancel`. `interrupt_ack` is the sidecar's `turn.cancel` response, because ACP's cancel has no acknowledgement. The provider process is still spawned and admitted by product code.
- test(restart-chaos): pin each wave's API URL to its own loopback server (#20462)
  > - `tests/restart-chaos/supervisor.ts`: new `waveServerUrlEnvironment(port)` sets `PAPERCLIP_API_URL` to `http://127.0.0.1:<port>` in every wave server environment (also after restarts and a K14 switch).
  > - `tests/restart-chaos/journey-driver.ts`: the Q3 agent script reads `PAPERCLIP_API_URL` again. The hard-coded loopback workaround is removed.
  > - `tests/restart-chaos/journeys.test.ts`: two tests.
- test(restart-chaos): one-row db pressure, bound accepted-Retry waits in U1, live L1 results (#20472)
  > - `contention.ts`: each pressure statement updates one running run row. The old statement updated every running row at once and deadlocked with product transactions.
  > - `universal.ts` (U1): an accepted Retry that waits on the system (`retryWait`) is an owner only for the queue bound (5 min of server-ready time). While it waits, the Retry button, failed notice and blocker do not count as actionable items, because the person already acted. A scheduled retry is still a timer.
  > - `contention-db.test.ts`: a real PostgreSQL test for `collectContentionEvidence` (receipt count, duplicate producer events, terminal receipt, accounting fields, company scope).
- test(restart-chaos): add ST5 credential rejection and ST5R key rotation storage faults (#20455)
  > - `tests/restart-chaos/storage-credentials.ts` (new): ST5 and ST5R rule planning, a wave-owned `AWS_CONFIG_FILE` profile whose `credential_process` prints the current key, MinIO restart with a new root key on the same loopback port, and a proxy wrapper that holds traffic during the restart and publishes the new key when the window ends.
  > - `storage-proxy.ts`: the `expired_credentials` fault takes an S3 error code; a `credential_rotation` marker passes traffic through.
  > - `storage-chaos.ts`, `types.ts`, `config.ts`, `main.ts`: registry lines for ST5/ST5R and the rotation wiring.
- Connect Codex and Claude login to agent computers (#20417)
  > - Admit plugin login only when the manifest and live worker support both PTY transport and recoverable creation.
  > - Validate a saved Runner agent in the same company and selected environment, including setup state.
  > - Carry login purpose, fixed command, agent target, and provider-attested absolute deadline through the existing allocation path.
- test(restart-chaos): cap store bandwidth during ST2 slow-store windows (#20452)
  > - New `tests/restart-chaos/storage-bandwidth.ts`: a shared byte-rate pacer (`ByteRate`), a pacing stream (`paced`), and the seeded ST2 bandwidth draw (2–16 MiB/s).
  > - `storage-proxy.ts`: the `latency` fault takes an optional `bytesPerSecond`. The proxy paces the request body and the response body through one rate per direction for each rule.
  > - `storage-chaos.ts`: ST2 windows set `bytesPerSecond`. Saved receipts keep the rule, so a replay uses the same cap.
- test(restart-chaos): add time faults (TM1-TM3): freeze, sleep with aged rows, aged outage (#20458)
  > - Add `TM1`, `TM2` and `TM3` to `--faults` (`time-faults.ts`, `time-faults-config.ts`):
  > - Add `--time-age-hours` (1–72, default 26). TM faults run only with `--env local`, and not with storms, crash points or K14.
  > - Aging is one transaction. It moves every `timestamp` column, and every time inside a `jsonb` column (ISO strings in their own format, and epoch milliseconds in the wave's window), back by the same interval. Triggers are off for the shift. Copies of a time in JSON (for example the comment `updatedAt` in a continuation receipt) still match their columns. The first wave showed that a shift of the columns only causes false failures.
- test(restart-chaos): human-in-the-loop journeys (Q2, Q3), journey triggers and a journey x fault matrix (#20448)
  > - New workload **Q2** (`journeys.ts`, `journey-driver.ts`): the agent saves a question with its native `request_human_input` tool. The board adds an unfinished `backlog` prerequisite, marks the task `blocked` by it, and answers through the task-thread respond route. The oracle requires that the answer reaches the agent in bounded answer mode while the task stays blocked, that the prerequisite relation stays, that no run has `issue_dependencies_blocked`, and that no stale Retry notice stays at rest. Then the board finishes the prerequisite and the agent finishes the task.
  > - New workload **Q3**: a harness-owned OAuth 2.1 + MCP app on loopback (`journey-oauth-app.ts`, discovery, dynamic client registration, PKCE, rotating refresh tokens, one write tool). The board connects it through the Apps routes and sets the tool to Ask first for a scripted `process` agent. When the review is pending, the harness moves the review's saved timestamps and the token expiry back 61 minutes in the wave's own database, runs Check connection (which refreshes the token through the app), and approves. The oracle requires one review, a refresh between request and decision, an accepted approval, exactly one `tools/call` with the reviewed arguments, an `executed` request, and an agent report that carries a receipt only the provider result contains.
  > - New triggers `asked` and `answered` fire a restart fault 0–3 s after the saved request is first seen and after the board's response. They work for the existing **Q1** saved question (#20432) too. The board answers before an `answered` fault fires and after every other planned fault.
- test(restart-chaos): add database contention workload and universal checks U1-U4 (#20421)
  > - `contention.ts` (new): workload L1 (five 3,000-line shell outputs as five tool calls, then a brief), its accounting oracle, and `RunRowPressure`, the `--db-pressure <writers>` knob. The writers run `update heartbeat_runs set updated_at = updated_at` on the wave company's running runs, in the wave's own isolated database only.
  > - L1 oracle: no `native_event_replay_conflict` or `runner_event_commit_stalled`; each producer event journaled once; a terminal receipt for each succeeded native run; finished runs accounted; per-run cost equals its cost events; the board's spend equals the cost-event sum with no runs pending accounting; and proof that the task produced contention.
  > - `universal.ts` (new): U1 liveness, U2 no silent retry loop, U3 diagnosable failures, U4 no stale notices. `grader.ts` grades them on every task. U1, U2 and U4 fail a task. U3 is a warning.
- test(chaos): read remote R1 files from the execution workspace and record why a read failed (#20440)
  > - File reads add `workspace=execution`, so the server reads the issue's execution workspace and not the `auto` choice.
  > - `readRemoteWorkspaceFile` and `captureRemoteWorkspaceFiles` report each failure through a callback. The observer keeps the last message per file in `record.remoteFileErrors` and logs `remote_file_read_failed` once per distinct message.
  > - The remote-workspace file reader stays in `remote-workspace-files.ts`; `observer.ts` is at 993 lines.
- perf(plugins): pick each lifecycle resource's head in one pass (#20406)
  > - `pluginLifecycleInbox.list()` uses a `lifecycle_heads` CTE with `DISTINCT ON` to pick one head per resource, then filters by the `afterId` cursor and orders by id with the limit.
  > - New test in `server/src/__tests__/resource-lifecycle-events.test.ts`: one resource with 2,000 acknowledged events and a 2,000-event backlog, plus a second resource. It asserts the exact heads in id order and a list time below 1 s (3.5 s before, 8–24 ms after).
- perf(workspaces): release retained cohort parent scratch early (#20447)
  > - Release three named parent archives and the restored tree after a successful online cohort capture, before that worker takes another member.
  > - Keep parent scratch roots and all nested-child verification areas. Keep every final fresh child and publication check.
  > - Retain payloads for archived repository intents. Use the existing writable-tree cleanup for read-only restored directories.
- test(restart-chaos): add config fault waves (CF1-CF3) and the CF4 design (#20446)
  > - `tests/restart-chaos/config-faults.ts` (new, pure): the catalog of broken references, the seeded plan, `parkState`, the task-level message check and `gradeConfigTask`. CF1 is a missing setup script. CF2 is a missing personal OpenRouter default. CF3 is a seeded walk over a deleted secret, a missing workspace path, a missing base ref, an unknown model, plus CF1 and CF2. `CONFIG_REFERENCES_NOT_BROKEN` lists the settings CF3 cannot break, and why.
  > - `tests/restart-chaos/config-wave.ts` (new): the wave runner. Each task gets its own agent, credential secret and project. The runner breaks the setting through board routes, launches an A1 brief task, polls, fires K1 or K2 while the task is parked, repairs the setting, uses the offered action and grades the task. It reuses the existing supervisor, seed and A1 oracle. Waves are limited to 4 tasks and a concurrency of 2. The instance is deleted at the end.
  > - `tests/restart-chaos/config-faults.test.ts` (new): unit tests for the plan, the arguments, `parkState` and the grader. These include the CF2 bug signature and the CF1 "message only on the run page" signature.
- docs: explain individual agent interaction permissions (#20422)
  > - Documented the eleven settings, sparse defaults, ownership rules, and existing authorization boundaries.
  > - Added CLI inspect, disable, and reset examples.
  > - Explained queued-input revocation, deployment order, and limits on historical information.
- test(chaos): add provider message faults (PV1-PV3) and a saved-question scenario (#20432)
  > - `provider-faults.ts` and `provider-shim/`: the PV1–PV3 fault family.
  > - `question-handoff.ts`: Q1 workload. The agent saves one question with `request_human_input`. After the asking run settles, the board answers through the respond route. The oracle checks that the question was asked once, the asking run succeeded, no run failed, nothing ran before the answer, and the brief has the answer.
  > - PV faults run only with `--env local --adapters codex`, only with workloads that interrupt a turn (Q1, S1, G1), and not in the same wave as restart faults. A PV1 delay passes only when the held message was released.
- test(perf): guard Mine against waiting on the company-wide workspace list (#20419)
  > - `tests/perf/collections/workspace-fixture.ts` (new): a retained company with 6,000 execution workspaces. 4,000 of them belong to finished tasks. 12 Mine tasks each run in their own isolated workspace. Mine tasks come from the API. The API cannot create execution workspaces, so the bulk rows go to the disposable instance database directly (same pattern as `tests/e2e/legacy-failure-continuation.spec.ts`). The database URL comes from `PAPERCLIP_CONFIG` or `PAPERCLIP_COLLECTION_PERF_DATABASE_URL`, loopback only. The fixture uses the existing retain/404 rule.
  > - `tests/perf/collections/mine-workspaces.spec.ts` (new), cold load of `/:prefix/issues?view=mine`:
  > - `tests/perf/collections/README.md`: documents the fixture, its database prerequisite, and the checks.
- perf(inbox): stop optional reads from holding Mine rows hidden (#20407)
  > - User directory: it no longer gates task rows. It still gates rows when they are grouped by assignee, because user names name and order those groups.
  > - Approvals and join requests: they still gate rows (see "Kept gates" below). The gate now covers only the first attempt (`isLoading && failureCount === 0`). That attempt uses a 10 s request timeout (`INBOX_OPTIONAL_READ_TIMEOUT_MS`). After a failure or timeout, rows reveal and the query retries in the background.
  > - Timeout notice: a timed-out approvals or join-request read is a transient error, which the inbox hides. Once the app's retries end, the inbox now shows the "Some inbox items couldn’t load" notice and "Retry missing items" button for a `TIMEOUT` on those reads. Other transient errors stay hidden as before.
- perf(auth): resolve a Cloud tenant actor in one database round trip (#20390)
  > - `resolveCloudTenantActorOnce` starts the purge-and-sync, the additional-membership read and `resolveOwnerInstanceAdmin` together and awaits them with `Promise.all`. A membership read failure still degrades to the primary company. The owner-elevation read still fails closed.
  > - New regression test `server/src/__tests__/cloud-tenant-actor-round-trips.test.ts`. It connects through a TCP proxy that delays every client write by 150 ms and asserts that a synced resolution finishes in less than 4 × the delay. Before the change it takes about 6 × (914 ms); after the change about 2 × (310 ms).
  > - `auth-session-route.test.ts`: the fake database binds each `returning()` to its own insert, and the test matches inserts by content instead of position, because the instance-settings insert can now happen in parallel with the tenant sync.
- perf(workspaces): group same-root cohorts with bounded workers (#20437)
  > - Raise the canonical service, saved-cohort and maintenance-request member bounds to 32. Keep the capture-worker bound at eight and single-workspace modes at one.
  > - Require one exact canonical physical root for more than eight members. Keep the existing full source-scope comparison, leaf rule and P/I/L/F guards.
  > - Use one private stage mapper with batches of at most eight. Join every started peer before an error can leave the stage. Stop future batches after a failure or cancellation.
- perf(runner): write preparation span rows to the run log in the background (#20426)
  > - `native-run-trace.ts`: `end` and `record` take an optional `deferRunLog`. With it, `end` queues the run-log row on a per-trace chain registered with `defer`, so rows are written one at a time in order and `finish` waits for them. A row requested after the trace finished is dropped, as for other deferred diagnostics.
  > - `native-session-executor.ts`: the preparation spans and the `environment.startup` scope use `deferRunLog: true`.
  > - `doc/run-log-events.md`: note that native preparation span rows are written late, in order.
- perf(runner): run launch artifacts in place from a trusted read-only root (#20428)
  > - `qualified_launch.rs`: new `TrustedLaunchRoot` and `set_trusted_launch_root` (process-global, set once at startup, like `--task-memory-cgroup`).
  > - `paperclip-runnerd.rs`: parse `--trusted-launch-root <dir>`. Older runnerd builds ignore unknown flags, so a host can pass it before every Runner understands it.
  > - The trusted path is Linux-only. On macOS and Windows, `TrustedLaunchRoot::open` always refuses.
- perf(workspaces): share cohort child-source verification (#20418)
  > - Add invocation-local initial and final proof maps to the existing cohort child verifier.
  > - Retain the existing canonical source-owner check before and after shared results.
  > - Defer cohort final nested checks until all captures finish, and join every final reader before publication or fence release.
- Extend login PTY contracts for existing computers (#20410)
  > - Export the login contract types from the main SDK entry point.
  > - Forward login purpose and command through plugin allocation and retain them in lease metadata.
  > - Forward the PTY deadline and give each input write a stable identity.
- perf(chat): index GitHub review lookups and share review-wait reads (#20400)
  > - Migration `0456_chat_review_lookup_indexes` builds four indexes with `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY IF NOT EXISTS`. It runs through the existing top-level statement path, which repairs an invalid index on retry:
  > - `githubReviewCheckState` reads the delivery's comment IDs first. If there is a comment, it reads the newest same-agent run with one of these `wakeCommentId` values. If there is no comment, it skips the run read. The comparison stays text equality on `comment_id::text`.
  > - `createExternalReviewWaitReader` shares one read per issue between the review and blocked-inbox attention maps of one issue-list request. The reader exists only for that request.
- perf(api): rank Mine inbox candidates from the participation set (#20413)
  > - Extracted the participation UNION from `unreadInboxIssueIds` into `mineParticipationIssueIds()`. The unread badge uses it unchanged.
  > - New `IssueFilters.touchedCandidates: "participation"`. When it is set with `touchedByUserId` (and there is no search and no `oneChildPerParent`), `list`:
  > - Only the Mine route sets the flag. Every other `svc.list` caller keeps the old SQL.
- perf(db): index pending budget Stops instead of scanning all run results (#20391)
  > - Added the partial index `heartbeat_runs_pending_budget_cancellation_idx` on `heartbeat_runs (company_id)`. Its predicate is identical to `pendingBudgetRunCancellationPredicate`.
  > - Added migration `0455_pending_budget_cancellation_index.sql`. It uses `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY IF NOT EXISTS`, and it is in the list of migrations that run outside a transaction, as `0436` is.
  > - Moved the scoped run-control read and the idle-sleep check into exported builders in `budget-cancellation.ts`, next to the scope list query. The SQL does not change. The callers and the test now share the same statements.
- perf(recovery): probe run bindings per comment in the lost-wake check (#20412)
  > - `checkUserCommentsWithoutWake` reads in three materialized stages:
  > - Each run lookup is two probes: one on the legacy context ID and one on the native issue column. Both use existing indexes. `exists (A or B)` becomes `exists (A) or exists (B)`, which is logically the same.
  > - Each probe ends with `offset 0`. This keeps it a per-row index lookup. Without it, PostgreSQL can build a hashed subplan over all runs, or a hash join over all item events of the company. The repository already uses this fence in `execution-blocker.ts`.
- test: preserve runner commit stall acknowledgement invariants (#20383)
  > - Add two saved-state assertions to each existing stall diagnostic case.
  > - Clarify the limits of historical failure attribution in the run-log documentation.
  > - Resolve conflicts by retaining master's implementation and diagnostic cases from #20385. Drop the case-search summary commit because it is already upstream. No production code changes remain in this PR.
- perf(inbox): read only the execution workspaces Mine rows reference (#20388)
  > - `packages/shared`: add `executionWorkspaceSummaryIdsQuerySchema` (comma-separated UUIDs, 1–100 distinct values) and `EXECUTION_WORKSPACE_SUMMARY_IDS_MAX`.
  > - `server`: `GET /companies/:companyId/execution-workspaces` accepts `ids`. Bad input returns 422. The service adds `inArray(execution_workspaces.id, ids)`. The company scope and the actor read condition still apply, so privacy does not change.
  > - `ui/src/api/execution-workspaces.ts`: `listSummaries` accepts `ids`. New `listSummariesByIds` reads ids in chunks of 100 and concatenates the results.
- perf(api): per-phase Server-Timing for the Mine inbox snapshot (#20408)
  > - Added `server/src/services/inbox-mine-timing.ts`. It times closed, content-free phases and formats a Server-Timing value. It emits no spans and no telemetry events.
  > - `issueService.list` accepts an optional third argument, `{ timePhase }`. It wraps the list's own reads: `query`, `labels`, `active_runs`, `user_stats`, `read_stats`, `last_activity`, `archives`, `blocked_by`, `live_descendants`, `attention`. Callers that do not pass it are unchanged.
  > - The Mine route reports `inbox_access`, `inbox_issues`, `inbox_parents`, `inbox_failures`, `inbox_failure_context` and `inbox_issues_<list phase>`. The existing middleware merges these with `paperclip_api`.
- perf(ui): skip socket reconcile for reads that have no live events (#20398)
  > - New `ui/src/lib/live-reconcile.ts`: `LIVE_EVENT_FREE_QUERY_KEYS` (instance settings, general and experimental settings, plugin UI contributions, workspace preferences, sidebar preferences), `isLiveReconciledQuery`, and `applyLiveEventFreeQueryDefaults`.
  > - `LiveUpdatesProvider` passes `isLiveReconciledQuery` as the predicate on its invalidate-all paths: tab return, disconnected polling, socket open, `live.resync`, and the transcript recovery that runs after a resync. The first healthy open still also skips session and health.
  > - `main.tsx` applies the 5-minute stale time to those keys with `setQueryDefaults`. No observer sets its own stale time for them.
- perf(ui): serve every project list from one includeArchived read (#20395)
  > - New `companyProjectsQuery(companyId, { includeArchived? })` in `ui/src/lib/company-projects-query.ts`. It always uses the `includeArchived: true` key and fetch. For active-only use it adds a stable, module-level `select` that keeps rows without `archivedAt`.
  > - 22 files that read the active list now spread `companyProjectsQuery(...)`, and so do the plugin SDK project picker and 16 archive-aware pages that wrote the shared key and fetcher inline. Their `enabled` and other options are unchanged. `OnboardingWizard`'s one imperative read is unchanged. `pages/Inbox.tsx` already reads the same key and fetcher, so it shares the entry without an edit.
  > - Invalidation: project mutations and live events invalidate `queryKeys.projects.all(companyId)`, which matches the shared key. The onboarding wizard invalidated the old active-only key; it now invalidates `projects.all`.
- perf(ui): read skills and routines only when an editor mounts (#20392)
  > - `EditorAutocompleteProvider` holds a `requested` flag. Both queries are `enabled` only after it is set.
  > - `useEditorAutocomplete()` sets the flag in an effect on first use. The flag stays set, so the lists stay current through the existing live-update invalidations for skills and routines.
  > - New unit test `EditorAutocompleteContext.test.tsx`: no reads without an editor; one read of each list when two editors mount.
- perf: fetch recent task summaries in one request (#20389)
  > - `ISSUE_SUMMARIES_MAX_IDS` is now 50 (was 5). The shared validator, the route's 400 message, the service guard and the OpenAPI description use the new bound.
  > - `useRecentTasks` builds all chunks first and fetches them at most three at a time (`RECENT_TASK_SUMMARY_CONCURRENCY`). The recent list has no size cap, so this keeps a long history from becoming a burst. The cache key, omission handling and pruning are unchanged. A failed chunk still fails the batch, so nothing is pruned on a partial failure.
  > - Tests: the route test accepts exactly `MAX` IDs and rejects `MAX + 1`; a hook test checks that at most three chunks are in flight; the API test uses the constant; `useRecentTasks` tests cover a 25-task history in one request and a 103-task history with three chunks in flight together, with and without a failed chunk.
- perf(workspace): run portable archives in bounded CPU workers (#20404)
  > - Run the existing self-contained archive function in native workers. Its algorithm and format stay byte-identical.
  > - Limit active workers to eight per process, with a 1 GiB old-generation heap limit each. Workers stop when their owned call finishes.
  > - Forward cancellation to the worker's AbortController. Wait for actual exit before returning, throwing or releasing an active slot. Cancel queued calls before they spawn.
- perf(runner): poll the provider right after an ACK releases it (#20374)
  > - `AuthenticatedTransport::receive_queued_json` reads with a 1 ms timeout and then restores `RUNTIME_READ_TIMEOUT`.
  > - New `CommandExecutor::provider_poll_is_live` hook (default `false`). The local Codex executor returns `true` only when it owns a running provider with no open startup attempt, so a poll cannot restore or launch a provider. The native executor forwards it for the local Codex facade only.
  > - In the steady-state loop, after a consumed control frame, the Runner uses `receive_queued_json` when it has sent output that this connection produced (a command or a provider poll) and the executor reports a live provider. If no frame is queued, it polls the provider at once.
- perf(db): index retry-exhaustion receipts for the attention scan (#20386)
  > - Add the partial index `heartbeat_run_events_retry_exhausted_idx` on `(company_id, run_id, id DESC)`. The index covers only rows where `event_type = 'lifecycle'` and `message like 'Bounded retry exhausted%'`.
  > - Add migration `0454`. It uses `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY IF NOT EXISTS`. The migration runner runs it as a top-level statement, the same as migrations 0432, 0436 and 0450.
  > - Add a regression test. It pins the row order (newest run first, then the newest receipt on a tie) and the latest receipt per run. It also checks that the query plan can use the new index. If the query predicate changes so that the index no longer applies, the test fails.
- perf(server): read plugin lifecycle inbox heads in one pass (#20405)
  > - `server/src/services/plugin-lifecycle-inbox.ts`: `list` now selects one head per resource with `DISTINCT ON (resource_type, resource_id)`. The head is the unacknowledged creation, else the oldest unacknowledged event. Then the query applies `afterId`, id order and the limit. This is the same set that the old `firstForResource` predicate admits. `acknowledge` keeps the old predicate for its single-event check.
  > - `plugin-lifecycle-inbox.ts`: new `hasPending(companyId, types)`. It returns whether any unacknowledged event exists. Each resource with an unacknowledged event has a head, so this gives the same answer as `list(companyId, 1).length > 0`.
  > - `server/src/services/plugin-lifecycle-delivery.ts`: the idle-sleep journal check uses `hasPending`. This keeps its early exit. A one-row `list` page would read the full journal.
- perf(server): make run liveness backfill lookup issue-scoped (#20393)
  > - `server/src/services/run-liveness-backfill-query.ts`: new `missingRunLivenessCandidatesQuery`. It replaces the `OR` and the correlated `exists` with `id in (context branch union all activity branch)`. Both branches are filtered by company and task.
  > - `server/src/services/activity.ts`: the backfill uses the new query and returns the number of rows it found. `scheduleRunLivenessBackfill` keeps a bounded in-memory map (at most 10,000 tasks, oldest entry is removed first) of tasks with nothing left to repair. It skips these tasks for 10 minutes.
  > - `server/src/__tests__/activity-service.test.ts`: four new real-database tests.
- perf(workspaces): reserve cohort members concurrently (#20401)
  > - Replace only the cohort reservation caller loop with `Promise.allSettled`.
  > - Preserve the existing per-member transaction, preassigned operation ID, source fingerprint and all source fences.
  > - Extend the real PostgreSQL failure fixture to require overlapping reservations and complete peer closure before fence release.
- Pause agents automatically when moving to a plugin environment (#20375)
  > - Save the new environment and automatic pause in the same transaction. Keep the existing plugin selection during stop.
  > - Use the existing completion of a pause without holds to resume automatically. Set the saved resume target to `verifying` so the new environment must pass its test.
  > - Preserve independent pause holds, lifecycle version checks, notifications, resource status events, and worker recovery.
- perf(workspaces): validate cohort members concurrently (#20387)
  > - Run cohort preflight member checks with `Promise.allSettled`. The existing input limit bounds this phase to eight readers.
  > - Run the member checks after finalizer lock acquisition the same way.
  > - Throw the first rejected result in the original member order after every reader has joined.
- perf(workspaces): index legacy workspace companion lookups (#20372)
  > - Add one concurrent index on company ID and the saved native workspace ID expression. The index has no state, runtime, or status predicate.
  > - Use nested JSON operators in companion discovery. Existing migration lookups already use this expression. Keep both accepted states and the same ordering.
  > - Register the migration on the existing top-level migration path. Retain its advisory lock, interrupted index repair, and completion journal behavior.
- perf: remove repeated reads and checks from run preparation (#20358)
  > - `object-byte-journal`: remove the `expectedOwner` input and comparison from `claim()`. Remove the extra head read in its three callers (run log `begin`, provider trace capture, run evidence migration).
  > - `object-run-log-store`: `begin` reads the source-prefix proof only when the claimed head has `generation > 1`.
  > - `skill-execution-content`: split `withVersion` into the access check and `withReadableVersion`. `withVersion` keeps the check for its other callers.
- test(chaos): fire planned faults at a fixed time after launch with --fault-after-seconds (#20345)
  > - `--fault-after-seconds <n>` (5–3600) sets `faultAfterMs`. `planTasks` still draws the seeded fire time, so nonces, holds and triggers are unchanged, but it uses `faultAfterMs` for every planned fault except K13.
  > - The option is rejected with storms and crash-point waves, which have no planned fault times.
  > - README: one line under Options.
- test(chaos): wait for the closing save before reading a remote R1 workspace's files (#20348)
  > - `remoteWorkspaceFile` retries a 422 every 2 s for up to 4 minutes (`REMOTE_FILE_WAIT_MS`). Any other failure, including 404 for a file the agent never wrote, returns `null` at once.
  > - README: notes the wait.
- test(chaos): run waves against S3-compatible storage with a fault proxy, and click Retry in the real board UI (#20288)
  > - `--storage s3`:
  > - `storage-proxy.ts`: a transparent, non-retrying S3 fault proxy that keeps the signed Host header. Rules match by method, path and remaining count. Faults are outage (503 `ServiceUnavailable`), latency with jitter, a 5xx or `SlowDown` error rate, connection reset, and 403 `ExpiredToken`. It counts requests by method and status, written to `storage-proxy.json`.
  > - `--storage-faults ST1,ST2,ST3` (`storage-chaos.ts`): seeded 60–180 s windows of outage, 1–10 s latency, or 30% 503 / `SlowDown`, applied while the wave runs. They're logged in the timeline and in `storage-faults.json`.
- perf(workspaces): time a restore's sandbox upload and extraction separately (#20331)
  > - `remote-workspace-transport.ts` (`restoreRemoteWorkspaceDirectory`): the archive copy into the sandbox runs inside `workspace.snapshot.sandbox_upload`, and the in-sandbox restore command inside `workspace.snapshot.sandbox_extract`. Both are children of the existing `workspace.snapshot.sandbox_restore`.
  > - `execution-preparation-timing.ts`: the two new phase names. A traced `measureRunPhase` scope also carries its preparation ancestor's run (`recordRunId`). Without it, with an OTLP endpoint set, a step nested inside another step (here, upload and extract inside `sandbox_restore`) never reached the run log. Execution attempts still record nothing.
  > - `doc/run-log-events.md`: the two rows in the durable storage timing table.
- perf(workspaces): verify saved Git objects without a single-threaded full fsck (#20329)
  > - `remote-workspace-snapshot-archive.ts`: both `git fsck --full --no-dangling` calls (the offline source check and the normalized-copy check) now call a new `verifyGitObjects`. It runs:
  > - `fsck --no-full` was not used: in a shallow clone it reports reflog entries that point at packed objects as invalid.
  > - Tests (`remote-workspace-snapshot-archive.test.ts`):
- Guard Native recovery with process-space proof and explicit containment (#19813)
  > - Record and verify the controller's kernel/PID namespace. Keep unknown and foreign unfinished ownership held. Preserve independent durable stop evidence and the existing remote lease fences.
  > - Use the shared accepted-result ownership reconciler. Bind settlement to the observed lease token, generation, boot, PID, namespace and process birth. Retain finalizer, cleanup, current-controller, live-owner and remote exclusions.
  > - Share the remote-owner reader across recovery consumers. Scope runner leases to the same company, task and run. Do not make local PID probes for remote accepted-result ownership.
- perf(runner): skip the turn-end provider restart in a per-turn Runner (#20312)
  > - `DurableRunnerConfig` has a new `turn_scoped` field.
  > - `paperclip-runnerd` sets it from `--lifecycle-mode`, which the controller already passes (`durable-prp-control-plane.ts` pushes `--lifecycle-mode <mode>`), but which `runnerd` ignored until now. Only `per_turn` sets it. `warm`, and launches without the flag (older controllers), keep today's behavior.
  > - `NativeProviderCommandExecutor::prepare_next_turn` returns early when `turn_scoped` is set. The lazy restart at the next turn start is unchanged.
- perf(task-chat): warm the whole first frame before a task opens (#20286)
  > - New `ui/src/lib/issue-chat-history-queries.ts`:
  > - `ui/src/hooks/useIssueIdBatches.ts`: new `prefetchIssueIdBatches` fills the same batch keys that the hook reads. The hook and the prefetch share one batching function.
  > - `prefetchIssueDetailForNavigation` accepts `history: true` and then also warms that set, plus the comment input facts.
- perf(run-logs): read object-store log tails in parallel, once, and cached (#20284)
  > - `object-byte-journal.ts`, `readRange`:
  > - `object-byte-journal.ts`, `load`:
  > - `object-run-log-store.ts`, `read`:
- perf(storage): time workspace snapshot save and restore steps and count storage retries (#20280)
  > - `server/src/storage/request-stats.ts` (new): an async-local collector of storage request statistics. Counts only:
  > - `server/src/storage/s3-failure.ts`: `withS3FailureDiagnostics`, which wraps every S3 send, records each request's SDK `$metadata.attempts` and `totalRetryDelay`, on success and on failure.
  > - `server/src/storage/s3-multipart.ts`: records each uploaded part and each resend wait, flagged throttled when the backend returned 503, 429 or SlowDown.
- test(runner-e2e): delete each runner E2E instance when it ends and expire kept instances after 6 hours (#20246)
  > - New shared `tests/runner-e2e/instance-retention.ts`:
  > - `createRunnerE2ETemporaryRoot` runs the sweep, so every caller gets it.
  > - Callers:
- perf(task-chat): stop a slow run transcript from hiding the chat (#20277)
  > - `TaskChatThread` now waits at most `INITIAL_TRANSCRIPT_REVEAL_BUDGET_MS` (600 ms) for run transcripts. The time starts when saved history, the plan and chat metadata are ready. The budget is one deadline. A transcript that loads early does not restart it.
  > - When the budget expires, the slow runs are latched for that task, so the decision does not change later:
  > - A live run that finishes after the reveal keeps its visible transcript. Only runs that were already settled when the budget expired are deferred.
- perf(issue-detail): measure task opens on real instances (#20276)
  > - Added `server/src/middleware/api-server-timing.ts`. Every `/api` response now has a `paperclip_api;dur=<ms>` Server-Timing entry. The value is the server time from request arrival until headers are written, including admission waits. The entry is appended, so the existing `paperclip_issue` phases stay first.
  > - Registered the middleware first in `server/src/app.ts`.
  > - Added `tests/perf/issue-detail/staging-click-harness.js`. It clicks a task row and records, frame by frame, when the composer and the latest message become visible, layout shifts, movement of the latest message, and the requests that run before the page is ready. The summary labels each sample instead of using the task reference. It replaces query values and every path segment that contains a digit. A fetch that is still running when the sample ends is listed as pending.
- test(chaos): close review-thread gaps in speed grading, S1 stops, readiness and stall reads (#20244)
  > - Speed: aggregate rows breach only from 5 samples (`insufficient sample (n < 5)` otherwise). Restart recoveries over the control-plane p95 budget now count in the per-run check, so a single slow recovery still fails.
  > - Config: the default workloads skip M1 when fewer than two adapters are given; an explicit `--workloads M1` with one adapter still fails.
  > - `runEvents` throws when a full page has no later `seq`; the run's speed read fails instead of hanging.
- test(runner-e2e): scope each agent's sandbox to its own run's saved state (#20253)
  > - `agent-filesystem-sandbox.py`: new `--expose PATH` option. It grants a path beneath a hidden directory. Landlock does not check traversal, so the run uses its own entry while the parent stays unlistable. The helper refuses a missing exposure, an exposure outside every hidden path, or one that would reveal a hidden path. It fails the launch unless every exposed path is readable.
  > - `agent-filesystem-sandbox.ts`: the launcher passes the instance root in the sandbox configuration. `runnerdLaunchSandbox` adds per-launch hidden paths: `durable-sessions/`, `runner/`, `control-plane/`, `opencode/` under `runtime/paperclip-runner/`, and `data/run-logs/`, `data/provider-traces/`, `data/backups/`, `db/`, `secrets/`. It exposes only the launch's own durable-session directory (from `--state-dir`), Codex runner state directory, OpenCode session directory (from `--session-id`, named as the OpenCode driver names it) and provider trace file.
  > - The wrapper creates the exposed entries, and the shared instance directories that the product creates on first use, before runnerd starts. It logs the run-scoped hidden and exposed paths for each launch.
- perf(heartbeat): retry a preparation a restart interrupted at once (#20255)
  > - `server/src/services/heartbeat/retries.ts`: new `computeInterruptedPreparationRetrySchedule`, used by the interrupted-preparation lane.
  > - `retries.test.ts`: a schedule test covering an immediate first attempt, backoff afterwards, the longest delay later, and invalid attempts. It's red on master.
- test(chaos): treat a crash-restart EPERM as a warning once the server pid is dead (#20232)
  > - `crashForRestart` catches EPERM from the group SIGKILL. It then sends SIGKILL to the server pid directly if needed and waits for it to exit within the forced timeout.
  > - If the server exited, it re-observes the tree and sends SIGKILL to each remaining group on its own, so one group's EPERM does not leave the others to drain gracefully. It logs a warning naming the server pid and any group that still refused SIGKILL, then continues with the normal post-kill cleanup.
  > - If the server is still alive, or the error is not EPERM, the error is rethrown as before.
- test(restart-chaos): do not grade a recovery that a back-to-back fault interrupted (#20243)

  > - `tests/restart-chaos/wave-speed.ts`:
  > - Tests:

  > - `cloneWorkspaceRepository` runs `git remote remove origin` after the clone and any pinned-commit fetch.
  > - The clone test asserts that both the plain and the pinned clone have no remote, that `git push origin` fails, and that no branch appears in the source.
